# Kernel facade (`partsmith-occt-sys`)

This spec defines the safe Rust facade over OCCT 7.9.3 (ADR 0003) and the C++ shim behind it. It implements the "Kernel facade" contract of `docs/design/architecture.md` and the kernel hygiene rules of technical design section 5. `partsmith-occt-sys` is the only crate with C++ and `unsafe`; its public API is safe Rust with no C++ types. The additions for helpful errors (solid and shell counts, `width_across`, `element_display`, and the hints and messages of section 4.3) are decided in [ADR 0026](../adr/0026-kernel-facade-additions-for-errors.md), and the hints and messages follow [the error guidelines](../guidelines/errors.md).

## 1. Purpose and scope

The facade turns OCCT into deterministic, thread-safe Rust functions that take plain values and immutable `Shape` handles and return new shapes with the history and face roles that `partsmith-features` needs to name elements (`docs/spec/naming.md`). It also measures shapes, describes elements for fingerprints, tessellates into `partsmith_mesh::Mesh`, and reads and writes STEP and binary BRep.

**In the M1 slice:**

- Constants `OCCT_VERSION` and `LINEAR_TOLERANCE`.
- Plain value types: `Point`, `Point2`, `Vector`, `Axis`, `Plane`, `Transform`, `Color`. Bounding boxes and tessellation tolerances use the shared `partsmith_mesh::BoundingBox` and `partsmith_mesh::TessellationTolerance` (mesh.md section 2.4).
- `Shape` (opaque, cheap to clone, `Send + Sync`), `ShapeKind`, `SubShapeKind`, sub-shape indexing, solids, compounds and locating sub-shapes of one shape in another.
- Results with history: `OpResult`, `History`, `InputHistory`, `ElementFate`; face roles for primitives (`Primitive`) and sweeps (`SweepResult`); `SplitResult`.
- Primitives: box, cylinder, sphere, cone, torus, placed by a `Plane` frame.
- Profiles: faces with holes from planar sketch curves (line segments, circular arcs, full circles) placed on a plane, each curve tagged.
- Prism (extrude between two offsets along a direction) and revolution (angle or full).
- Booleans with any number of tools: fuse, cut, common, with coplanar-face unification.
- Constant-radius fillet, chamfer (equal distance, two distances), shell (thick solid, inward, removed faces).
- Transforms: translate, rotate, mirror, uniform scale.
- Split by plane and planar section.
- Measurement: volume, area, center of mass, bounding box, validity, and the solid and shell counts that tell separate pieces and closed cavities apart.
- Element geometry queries (kind, direction, location, radius, centroid, area or length), the point, tangent and outward face normals at the middle of an edge with the continuity recorded between its faces, the width of a face across an edge, and topology (faces of an edge, edges of a face, faces and edges of a vertex, seam and degenerate flags).
- Tessellation into `partsmith_mesh::Mesh` with `f64` positions, per-triangle face ids, edge polylines with edge ids, and vertex markers with vertex ids, from a `partsmith_mesh::TessellationTolerance`; the display geometry of one face, edge or vertex alone, for the ghost of a lost element.
- STEP write to bytes and read from bytes (AP214, millimeters, body names and colors); binary BRep write and read for the result cache.
- `KernelError` with operation, kind, C++ exception type and raw message, plus a hint that follows a feature's own sentence, a whole message for hosts, and the technical detail for reports.
- Shim rules, thread safety, determinism, and the `build.rs` contract.

**In M2, wave 1** ([ADR 0063](../adr/0063-cancel-through-a-host-flag-outside-the-session.md), [ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)):

- Interrupts (section 3.13): `Interrupt`, the trailing `interrupt` parameter of the calls that poll OCCT's progress range, `KernelErrorKind::Canceled`, `Operation::WaitForInterrupt`, `wait_for_interrupt` for the tests of callers, and on the web core the JavaScript import `partsmith_cancel_word`.
- Edge and vertex history pinned by tests, and the rule for the generators that later operations report (section 3.3 items 7 and 8, [ADR 0060](../adr/0060-direct-generator-names-for-edges-and-vertices.md)).
- The contract of the conic and B-spline profile curves of the M2 plan's item 14 (section 2.5), built in wave 2.
- The kernel spikes (section 8), whose results go into this spec before the feature specs of waves 2 to 4. No product code merges from them.
- `read_step` refuses a file whose check list holds a fail, before any transfer (section 3.8.1 read item 4, [ADR 0067](../adr/0067-fuzzing-with-cargo-fuzz-in-a-crate-outside-the-workspace.md) choice 10). It is the fix of the first fuzz finding (delivery.md section 3.8).
- `read_step` checks the bytes before OCCT reads them. It refuses a parameter list that breaks STEP's syntax, an instance number larger than OCCT reads, a number defined more than once, a scope, and instance references that form a cycle or a chain of more than 500 references (section 3.8.1 read items 6 and 7, [ADR 0073](../adr/0073-a-check-of-step-bytes-and-a-patch-to-occt-7-9-3.md)). OCCT 7.9.3 is built with Partsmith's patch `scripts/occt-7.9.3.patch`, which makes two changes to its STEP reader (read item 8). An oriented edge whose edge element is an oriented edge gives a fail of the check list, which bounds a recursion that overflowed the stack. An edge curve whose ends are not vertex points gives a load fail, where OCCT read through a null. They are the fixes of fuzz findings 2 to 7 (delivery.md section 3.8).

**In M2, wave 2** ([ADR 0082](../adr/0082-conic-and-spline-profile-curves-in-the-kernel.md), [ADR 0083](../adr/0083-extrusions-that-end-on-a-face-or-a-shape-and-revolutions-between-two-angles.md), [ADR 0084](../adr/0084-wrap-curves-and-modeled-threads-in-the-kernel.md)):

- Conic and B-spline profile curves (section 2.5, item 14): the `PlanarCurve` variants `Ellipse`, `EllipticalArc`, `Parabola`, `Hyperbola` and `Bspline`, a piece of a B-spline given by its parameter range (`range`), the value type `Vector2`, their checks, their signed areas (section 3.4.2) and their extremes for the revolve's half-plane check (section 3.4.4). `PlanarCurve` and `TaggedCurve` derive `Clone` and no longer `Copy`.
- `extrude_to` (sections 2.6 and 3.4.3, item 17): an extrusion that ends at an offset, on faces of a shape that lie on one plane or one cylinder, or on the faces of a shape it meets first, with an optional taper. It adds `ExtrudeEnd`, `SweepFace::Target`, `Operation::ExtrudeTo` and the error kinds `TargetNotReached`, `TargetPartlyReached` and `TargetStartsInside`. `revolve` takes a start and an end angle (section 3.4.4).
- Wrap surfaces, helices and modeled threads (sections 2.15 and 3.4.14, items 19 and 20, [ADR 0084](../adr/0084-wrap-curves-and-modeled-threads-in-the-kernel.md), [ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md)): `WrapSurface`, `Helix` with its `HelixHand`, `WrapCurve`, and `wrap_curves` (a cylinder's or a cone's parameter-plane lines as edges, one per turn of a helix); and `modeled_thread`, ADR 0074's tooth lofted between helical wires whose every edge runs forward, divided per turn and joined to its base, with the role or generator of every face, edge and vertex of the tooth and a check that the join kept both. They add `Operation::WrapCurves` and `Operation::ModeledThread`.
- The pass criteria of three follow-up spikes, fixed before they run (sections 8.8 to 8.10, [ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md) alternative 2).
- The core size that every kernel change records (section 5, [ADR 0076](../adr/0076-occt-built-for-the-web-at-oz.md) decision 6).

**Explicitly later (not in M1):**

- Sweep, loft, helix, draft, thicken, offset face, rib, emboss, thread geometry (the architecture lists sweep, loft and draft for the facade; they are not needed by the M1 features).
- Ellipses, elliptical arcs and B-splines as profile curves.
- Variable-radius and chord-length fillets, setback corners; distance-angle chamfers.
- Outward shells, per-face shell thickness, closed-shell hollowing with drain holes as one operation.
- Non-uniform scale (`gp_GTrsf`).
- Split by face, body or sketch; split face.
- Fuzzy booleans, glue options, user-controlled unification.
- Per-face incremental re-tessellation.
- STEP AP242, PMI, assemblies preserved as assemblies, IGES and text BRep import and export, mesh-to-BRep conversion, sewing open shells into solids on import.
- OCCT internal parallelism (`SetRunParallel`, parallel meshing).
- Projection of edges onto planes and silhouettes (for sketch reference geometry).

M2 schedules sweep, loft, helix, rib, emboss and thread geometry (the M2 plan's items 18 to 20, 28, 30 and 31), conic and B-spline profile curves (item 14), up-to and tapered prisms (item 17) and the projection of edges and silhouettes (item 26). Section 8 holds the spikes that settle their constructions. Each item's spec adds its calls to this spec when its wave starts. Draft, thicken and offset face stay later.

## 2. Public API

Everything is exported from the crate root. Since M2 the calls that poll OCCT's progress range take `interrupt: &Interrupt` as their last parameter (sections 2.14 and 3.13): `extrude`, `extrude_to` (since wave 2), `revolve`, `fuse`, `cut`, `common`, `fillet`, `chamfer`, `shell`, `split`, `modeled_thread` (since wave 2), `Shape::tessellate`, `write_step` and `read_step`. A caller that never stops passes `&Interrupt::new()`. No public type derives `serde` traits: kernel values never appear in documents or protocol messages (BRep and STEP are byte buffers). All lengths are millimeters and all angles radians, `f64`.

```rust
/// The pinned OCCT version (ADR 0003); part of recompute cache keys.
pub const OCCT_VERSION: &str = "7.9.3";
/// Modeling tolerance in millimeters; equal to `partsmith_model::LINEAR_TOLERANCE` (section 3.1).
pub const LINEAR_TOLERANCE: f64 = 1e-6;
```

### 2.1 Value types

```rust
/// A point in model space.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Point { pub x: f64, pub y: f64, pub z: f64 }

/// A point in a plane's own 2D coordinates (along its x axis and y axis).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Point2 { pub x: f64, pub y: f64 }

/// A direction or displacement in model space.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Vector { pub x: f64, pub y: f64, pub z: f64 }

/// A direction or displacement in a plane's own 2D coordinates (M2, the axes of section 2.5's conics).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Vector2 { pub x: f64, pub y: f64 }

impl Point {
    /// Creates a point.
    pub const fn new(x: f64, y: f64, z: f64) -> Point;
    /// The point moved by a vector.
    pub fn offset(self, vector: Vector) -> Point;
    /// Euclidean distance to another point.
    pub fn distance(self, other: Point) -> f64;
}

impl Vector {
    /// Creates a vector.
    pub const fn new(x: f64, y: f64, z: f64) -> Vector;
    /// Euclidean length.
    pub fn length(self) -> f64;
    /// Unit vector in the same direction; `None` if the length is below 1e-12.
    pub fn normalized(self) -> Option<Vector>;
    /// Dot product.
    pub fn dot(self, other: Vector) -> f64;
    /// Cross product.
    pub fn cross(self, other: Vector) -> Vector;
    /// The vector multiplied by a scalar.
    pub fn scaled(self, factor: f64) -> Vector;
}

/// A line in space: a point and a direction (normalized by the facade where used).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Axis { pub origin: Point, pub direction: Vector }

/// A plane with an in-plane frame. Its local y axis is `normal x x_axis`, so (x, y, normal) is right-handed.
/// Also used as the placement frame of primitives (local z is `normal`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Plane { pub origin: Point, pub normal: Vector, pub x_axis: Vector }

impl Plane {
    /// Origin plane XY: normal +Z, x axis +X.
    pub const XY: Plane;
    /// Origin plane XZ: normal -Y, x axis +X (local y is +Z).
    pub const XZ: Plane;
    /// Origin plane YZ: normal +X, x axis +Y (local y is +Z).
    pub const YZ: Plane;
    /// Unit local y axis, `normal x x_axis` after normalizing both.
    pub fn y_axis(&self) -> Vector;
    /// The model-space point of plane coordinates `(x, y)`.
    pub fn to_world(&self, point: Point2) -> Point;
}

/// An affine similarity: rotation, translation, mirror, uniform scale, or a composition.
/// Maps `p` to `M p + t` where `matrix[r] = [M[r][0], M[r][1], M[r][2], t[r]]`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Transform { pub matrix: [[f64; 4]; 3] }

impl Transform {
    /// The identity.
    pub const IDENTITY: Transform;
    /// Translation by a vector.
    pub fn translation(vector: Vector) -> Transform;
    /// Rotation by `angle` (right-hand rule) about an axis.
    pub fn rotation(axis: &Axis, angle: f64) -> Transform;
    /// Reflection through a plane (its x axis is ignored).
    pub fn mirror(plane: &Plane) -> Transform;
    /// Uniform scale about a center; `factor` must be greater than 0 when applied.
    pub fn scale(center: Point, factor: f64) -> Transform;
    /// This transform followed by `next`.
    pub fn then(&self, next: &Transform) -> Transform;
    /// Applies the transform to a point.
    pub fn apply_point(&self, point: Point) -> Point;
    /// Applies the linear part to a vector.
    pub fn apply_vector(&self, vector: Vector) -> Vector;
}

/// An sRGB color, each component in [0, 1] (the encoded sRGB value, not linear light).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Color { pub red: f64, pub green: f64, pub blue: f64 }
```

### 2.2 Shapes and indexing

```rust
/// The kind of a whole shape.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ShapeKind { Compound, CompSolid, Solid, Shell, Face, Wire, Edge, Vertex }

/// A kind of indexed sub-shape. The order of variants is the sort order used in history lists.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub enum SubShapeKind { Solid, Shell, Face, Edge, Vertex }

/// One sub-shape: its kind and 1-based index.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord, Hash)]
pub struct SubShape { pub kind: SubShapeKind, pub index: u32 }

/// An immutable B-rep shape. Cloning is cheap and shares the geometry.
#[derive(Clone)]
pub struct Shape { /* private: cxx::SharedPtr to the shim's Shape */ }

// SAFETY rationale in section 3.10.
unsafe impl Send for Shape {}
unsafe impl Sync for Shape {}

impl std::fmt::Debug for Shape {
    /// Writes `Shape { kind: Solid, solids: 1, faces: 6, edges: 12, vertices: 8 }`.
}

impl Shape {
    /// An empty compound (no sub-shapes).
    pub fn empty() -> Shape;
    /// The kind of the whole shape.
    pub fn kind(&self) -> ShapeKind;
    /// Number of distinct sub-shapes of a kind (the size of its index map).
    pub fn count(&self, kind: SubShapeKind) -> u32;
    /// True when the shape has no faces, edges or vertices.
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
    /// Each solid as its own shape, in solid index order (sharing geometry with `self`).
    pub fn solids(&self) -> Result<Vec<Shape>, KernelError>;
    /// For each sub-shape of `part` of `kind` (in `part`'s index order), its index in `self`, or `None`.
    pub fn locate(&self, part: &Shape, kind: SubShapeKind) -> Result<Vec<Option<u32>>, KernelError>;
    /// True when OCCT's `BRepCheck_Analyzer` finds the shape valid.
    pub fn is_valid(&self) -> Result<bool, KernelError>;

    /// Enclosed volume in cubic millimeters (sum over solids; 0 without solids).
    pub fn volume(&self) -> Result<f64, KernelError>;
    /// Total face area in square millimeters.
    pub fn area(&self) -> Result<f64, KernelError>;
    /// Volume centroid; fails for shapes without volume.
    pub fn center_of_mass(&self) -> Result<Point, KernelError>;
    /// Tight axis-aligned bounds; fails for empty shapes.
    pub fn bounding_box(&self) -> Result<partsmith_mesh::BoundingBox, KernelError>;

    /// Geometry of one face, edge or vertex.
    pub fn element(&self, kind: SubShapeKind, index: u32) -> Result<ElementGeometry, KernelError>;
    /// Geometry of every face, edge or vertex, in index order.
    pub fn elements(&self, kind: SubShapeKind) -> Result<Vec<ElementGeometry>, KernelError>;
    /// Adjacency of all faces, edges and vertices.
    pub fn topology(&self) -> Result<Topology, KernelError>;
    /// The point and tangent at the middle of an edge, the outward normal there of each face containing it, and the continuity recorded between them (section 3.6, item 5).
    pub fn edge_normals(&self, edge: u32) -> Result<EdgeNormals, KernelError>;
    /// How wide a face is across an edge: the largest distance from the edge to a sampled point of the face (section 3.6, item 7).
    pub fn width_across(&self, face: u32, edge: u32) -> Result<f64, KernelError>;

    /// Triangulates the shape into a mesh with face ids, edge polylines and vertex markers (section 3.7). Polls `interrupt` (section 3.13).
    pub fn tessellate(&self, tolerance: &partsmith_mesh::TessellationTolerance, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<partsmith_mesh::Mesh, KernelError>;
    /// The display geometry of one face, edge or vertex, without meshing the rest of the shape (section 3.7, item 9).
    pub fn element_display(&self, kind: SubShapeKind, index: u32, tolerance: &partsmith_mesh::TessellationTolerance) -> Result<ElementDisplay, KernelError>;

    /// Serializes to OCCT binary BRep (section 3.8.2).
    pub fn to_brep(&self) -> Result<Vec<u8>, KernelError>;
    /// Deserializes OCCT binary BRep written by `to_brep`.
    pub fn from_brep(bytes: &[u8]) -> Result<Shape, KernelError>;
}
```

`SubShapeKind::Solid` is valid for `count`, `locate` and `solids`, and `SubShapeKind::Shell` for `count` and `locate` (section 3.6, item 6); `element`, `elements` and `element_display` accept `Face`, `Edge` and `Vertex` only.

### 2.3 Results and history

```rust
/// A new shape plus, for each operation input, what became of its faces, edges and vertices.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct OpResult {
    /// The result shape.
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// History relative to the operation's inputs.
    pub history: History,
}

/// History of one operation. `inputs[k]` describes the operation's k-th input shape (order documented per operation).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct History { pub inputs: Vec<InputHistory> }

/// History of one input shape: entry `i` of each list describes its sub-shape `i + 1`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct InputHistory {
    pub faces: Vec<ElementFate>,
    pub edges: Vec<ElementFate>,
    pub vertices: Vec<ElementFate>,
}

/// What became of one input element.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct ElementFate {
    /// Output elements of the same kind that are this element or its images (unchanged, modified, split or merged into), ascending.
    pub modified: Vec<u32>,
    /// Output elements of any kind generated from this element, sorted by kind then index.
    pub generated: Vec<SubShape>,
}

impl ElementFate {
    /// True when no output element of the same kind descends from this element.
    pub fn is_deleted(&self) -> bool;
}

/// Where an output element came from.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct Origin {
    /// Index into `History::inputs`.
    pub input: usize,
    /// The input element.
    pub element: SubShape,
}

impl History {
    /// Input elements whose `modified` list contains output element `index` of `kind`, sorted.
    pub fn modified_from(&self, kind: SubShapeKind, index: u32) -> Vec<Origin>;
    /// Input elements (any kind) whose `generated` list contains output element `index` of `kind`, sorted.
    pub fn generated_from(&self, kind: SubShapeKind, index: u32) -> Vec<Origin>;
}
```

### 2.4 Primitives

```rust
/// A primitive solid with the role of each face.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct Primitive {
    /// The solid.
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// Role of face `i + 1`.
    pub faces: Vec<PrimitiveFace>,
}

/// The role of a primitive's face, in the placement frame (local x = `x_axis`, local z = `normal`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum PrimitiveFace { Left, Right, Front, Back, Bottom, Top, Side, Surface }

/// Box spanning [0, length] x [0, width] x [0, height] in the placement frame.
pub fn make_box(placement: &Plane, length: f64, width: f64, height: f64) -> Result<Primitive, KernelError>;
/// Cylinder on local z from 0 to `height`, axis through the placement origin.
pub fn make_cylinder(placement: &Plane, radius: f64, height: f64) -> Result<Primitive, KernelError>;
/// Sphere centered at the placement origin, poles on local z.
pub fn make_sphere(placement: &Plane, radius: f64) -> Result<Primitive, KernelError>;
/// Cone (or frustum) on local z, `bottom_radius` at z = 0 and `top_radius` at z = `height`.
pub fn make_cone(placement: &Plane, bottom_radius: f64, top_radius: f64, height: f64) -> Result<Primitive, KernelError>;
/// Torus centered at the placement origin with axis local z.
pub fn make_torus(placement: &Plane, major_radius: f64, minor_radius: f64) -> Result<Primitive, KernelError>;
```

### 2.5 Profiles

```rust
/// A planar curve in a plane's 2D coordinates. Since M2 it derives `Clone` and no longer `Copy`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum PlanarCurve {
    /// Segment from `start` to `end`.
    Line { start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// Arc of the circle (`center`, `radius`), counterclockwise (in plane coordinates) from `start` to `end`.
    Arc { center: Point2, radius: f64, start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// Full circle.
    Circle { center: Point2, radius: f64 },
    /// Full ellipse, counterclockwise: the points `center + a cos(t) u + b sin(t) n` (M2).
    Ellipse { center: Point2, major: Vector2, minor_radius: f64 },
    /// Arc of the ellipse of `Ellipse`, counterclockwise from `start` to `end` (M2).
    EllipticalArc { center: Point2, major: Vector2, minor_radius: f64, start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// Arc between `start` and `end` of the parabola `vertex + (t^2 / (4 f)) w + t m` (M2).
    Parabola { vertex: Point2, focus: Point2, start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// Arc between `start` and `end` of the hyperbola's branch `center + a cosh(t) u + b sinh(t) n` (M2).
    Hyperbola { center: Point2, major: Vector2, minor_radius: f64, start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// A B-spline, or the piece of it over `range` (M2).
    Bspline {
        /// From 1 to 25.
        degree: u32,
        /// The control points.
        poles: Vec<Point2>,
        /// One weight per pole for a rational B-spline; `None` when every weight is 1.
        weights: Option<Vec<f64>>,
        /// The distinct knots, strictly increasing.
        knots: Vec<f64>,
        /// The multiplicity of each knot: `degree + 1` at both ends of an open (clamped) spline.
        multiplicities: Vec<u32>,
        /// True for a periodic (closed) spline.
        periodic: bool,
        /// The piece's parameter interval `[low, high]` with `low < high`; `None` for the whole curve.
        range: Option<[f64; 2]>,
    },
}

/// A curve plus a caller-chosen tag that comes back on the edge and face it produces. Since M2 it derives `Clone` and no longer `Copy`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct TaggedCurve { pub tag: u32, pub curve: PlanarCurve }

/// A closed chain of curves, listed in chain order; each curve may be listed in either direction.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct PlanarLoop { pub curves: Vec<TaggedCurve> }

/// An outer loop with zero or more hole loops.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct PlanarRegion { pub outer: PlanarLoop, pub holes: Vec<PlanarLoop> }

/// Planar faces built from regions, with the tag of every edge.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct PlanarProfile {
    /// A face (one region) or a compound of faces (several regions, in region order).
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// The plane the profile lies in.
    pub plane: Plane,
    /// Tag of edge `i + 1` of `shape`.
    pub edge_tags: Vec<u32>,
    /// The regions it was built from.
    pub regions: Vec<PlanarRegion>,
}

/// Builds faces from regions on a plane; every face's normal is the plane normal.
pub fn make_profile(plane: &Plane, regions: &[PlanarRegion]) -> Result<PlanarProfile, KernelError>;
```

The names carry a `Planar` prefix, like `PlanarCurve`, so they do not collide with `partsmith_sketch::Profile`, `Region` and `ProfileLoop` inside `partsmith-features`.

**Conic and B-spline curves (M2, item 14, [ADR 0082](../adr/0082-conic-and-spline-profile-curves-in-the-kernel.md)).** `PlanarCurve` has one variant for each `ProfileCurve` variant of sketch.md section 2.7, with the same name and its geometry, points as `Point2`, vectors as `Vector2` and no direction flag (`ccw`, `forward`), as `Arc` carries a sketch arc. A `Bspline` also leaves out the sketch's `start` and `end` (the kernel measures them, item 6) and carries its knots in OCCT's form (item 1). The formulas are those of sketch.md section 3.20.1. With `a = norm(major)`, `u = major / a`, `n` the vector `u` turned a quarter turn counterclockwise (`(-u.y, u.x)`) and `b = minor_radius`, an ellipse is `center + a cos(t) u + b sin(t) n` and a hyperbola's branch `center + a cosh(t) u + b sinh(t) n`, the branch that opens along `u`. With `f = norm(focus - vertex)`, `w = (focus - vertex) / f` and `m = (-w.y, w.x)`, a parabola is `vertex + (t^2 / (4 f)) w + t m`. `b` may be larger than `a` on an ellipse and on a hyperbola, as sketch.md section 3.20.1 allows.

1. **What converts to what.** Each sketch `ProfileCurve` converts to one `PlanarCurve` (sketch.md section 3.13, its consumers, and features.md section 3.7). An elliptical arc is the part of its ellipse counterclockwise from `start` to `end`, as an `Arc` is of its circle. A sketch elliptical arc whose `ccw` is false is therefore passed with `start` and `end` swapped, as features.md section 3.7 passes an arc. An arc of a parabola or of a hyperbola's branch is the part between `start` and `end`, in either order. Conics and fit splines arrive as `Bspline`s, rational or polynomial, in the form the sketch derives for them (sketch.md section 3.20.2, m2-plan.md choice 11, [ADR 0066](../adr/0066-sketch-entities-of-format-version-3-operations-and-display-polylines.md)). The sketch's knot vector, one value per knot with its repeats (sketch.md section 3.20.5), becomes `knots` and `multiplicities`: each run of consecutive equal values (compared with `==`) becomes one knot whose multiplicity is the run's length. A periodic spline's `poles + 1` strictly increasing values become knots of multiplicity 1, OCCT's periodic form of the same curve, whose period is the last knot minus the first.
2. **Pieces.** A piece of a spline or conic that the sketch's arrangement split at its vertices (sketch.md section 3.13.2) is the whole `Bspline` with `range`, the piece's parameter interval `[low, high]` in the spline's own parameter, `low < high`. On a periodic spline the interval may run past the last knot, and the piece then continues from the first. A piece traversed against the spline's direction is passed as it is: the chain of section 3.4.2 item 2 picks the direction of every curve. A piece of an ellipse, a parabola or a hyperbola is an `EllipticalArc`, `Parabola` or `Hyperbola` with the piece's ends as `start` and `end`, the vertices the sketch shares bitwise with the neighboring curves. Their parameters follow from those points (item 5), and they carry no range.
3. **Ends.** The ends of a curve are `start` and `end` for an `EllipticalArc`, a `Parabola` and a `Hyperbola`, and for a `Bspline` its points at the two ends of `range`, or of its first and last knots without one, as the shim measures them (item 6). The chain meets neighbors through these points within `LINEAR` (section 3.4.2 item 2). A spline's measured ends are checked against its neighbors within `LINEAR`, and the shim builds the junction vertices from them.
4. **Closed curves.** An `Ellipse`, a periodic `Bspline` without a `range`, and any other `Bspline` whose two measured ends lie within `LINEAR` of each other are closed. A closed curve is the only curve of its loop, as `Circle` is. Its edge has one vertex: the point at its parameter 0 for an `Ellipse` (the end of `major`), and for a `Bspline` the midpoint of its measured ends, the junction of a loop of one curve.
5. **The exact curves.** The shim builds each new variant as an exact 2D OCCT curve in plane coordinates. Its edge is `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge` of that curve on the profile's own `Geom_Plane` (the overload that takes a `Geom2d_Curve` and a `Geom_Surface`), between its junction vertices and its parameters there, and `BRepLib::BuildCurve3d` gives the edge its 3D curve. On a plane that is `GeomLib::To3d`, exact for every curve below. The shim never uses `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge2d` or `BRepLib::Plane`: both read a process-wide plane that OCCT creates on first use (section 3.10 item 9).
   - `Ellipse`: a `Geom2d_Ellipse` on the axes `gp_Ax22d(center, u, n)` with major radius `a` and minor radius `b` when `a >= b`. When `b > a` (`gp_Elips2d` refuses that as given), on `gp_Ax22d(center, n, -u)` with major radius `b` and minor radius `a`: the same points in the same direction, at OCCT's parameter `t - pi / 2`.
   - `EllipticalArc`: that ellipse from the parameter of the junction at `start` counterclockwise to the parameter of the junction at `end`, a `Geom2d_TrimmedCurve` whose end parameter lies in `(first, first + 2 pi)`.
   - `Parabola`: a `Geom2d_Parabola` on `gp_Ax22d(vertex, w, m)` with focal distance `f`, whose parameter is `t`, between the parameters of its two junctions.
   - `Hyperbola`: a `Geom2d_Hyperbola` on `gp_Ax22d(center, u, n)` with major radius `a` and minor radius `b`, never swapped (`gp_Hypr2d` takes `b > a`), whose parameter is `t` on the branch that opens along `u`, between the parameters of its two junctions.
   - `Bspline`: a `Geom2d_BSplineCurve` of the poles, the weights when given, the knots and multiplicities, the degree and the periodic flag. OCCT's periodic curve at a parameter in the span from knot `j` to knot `j + 1` (0-based) combines the `degree + 1` poles from pole `j` on, taken cyclically, and repeats with the period. partsmith-sketch's evaluation of a periodic spline follows the same convention (sketch.md section 3.20.5): the spline of test 198 is at (20/3, 20/3) at parameter 0 and at (0, 55/6) at parameter 0.5. With `range`, the curve is a `Geom2d_TrimmedCurve` of that B-spline from `low` to `high`.

   The shim trims a conic arc at its junction vertices, as it trims an arc (section 3.4.2 item 1): the parameter of a junction on a conic is that of the nearest point of the whole conic (`Extrema_ExtPElC2d`, the solution with the smallest distance, the lowest parameter on a tie; on an ellipse taken in `[0, 2 pi)`). The facade checks the curve's own `start` and `end` against the conic first, and a point farther than `LINEAR` from it fails the call (section 3.4.2 item 7.6). The edge lies on the exact curve, and each junction vertex's tolerance covers the distance from it to every curve end that meets it, at least `LINEAR`, as for arcs. An elliptical arc's edge, like a full ellipse's, runs counterclockwise and is reversed when the chain runs the curve clockwise, as an arc's is. A spline's edge runs toward increasing parameter and is reversed when the chain runs it backward. A parabola's or a hyperbola's edge runs toward increasing `t` and is reversed when the junction where the chain enters it has the larger parameter.
6. **Measuring splines.** The facade cannot evaluate a B-spline itself: this crate depends on no spline code, and the sketch's evaluation lives in `partsmith-sketch` (m2-plan.md choice 10). Once checks 1 to 3 of section 3.4.2 item 7 have passed on every curve of the call, and before it checks any loop, it gives every `Bspline` of the call to the shim's `measure_splines`. That call builds each curve as item 5 does and returns its points at the two ends of its range (`Geom2d_Curve::Value`), its length (`GCPnts_AbscissaPoint::Length` of a `Geom2dAdaptor_Curve` over the range, tolerance 1e-9) and its signed area term of section 3.4.2 item 3. The facade's checks, its chain and its orientation use these numbers (section 3.4.2). The shim measures and the facade decides: every message comes from Rust in the order of section 3.4.2.
7. **Bridge data** (`src/ffi.rs`). No cxx type reaches a public signature (section 3.9 item 8), and every struct stays `Copy` except `SplineData` with its `Vec`s.
   - `ProfileCurveData` gains the kinds `Ellipse`, `EllipticalArc`, `Parabola`, `Hyperbola` and `Bspline` in `CurveType`, and the fields `axis_x` and `axis_y` (an ellipse's or hyperbola's `major`, a parabola's `focus`), `minor_radius` and `spline` (the index of the curve's entry in `make_profile`'s new second argument, a slice of `SplineData`). `center_x` and `center_y` hold an ellipse's or hyperbola's center and a parabola's vertex. `start_x` and `start_y` stay the junction where the chain enters the curve, and the shim takes the junction where it leaves from the next curve, as for arcs. `clockwise` becomes `reversed`, true when the chain runs a curve against its own direction: a circle, an arc, an ellipse or an elliptical arc clockwise, a spline toward decreasing parameter. It is false for lines, parabolas and hyperbolas, whose edges the shim orients by their junctions alone (item 5).
   - `SplineData` holds `degree`, `periodic`, `poles` (x and y interleaved), `weights` (empty for a polynomial spline), `knots`, `multiplicities`, and `low` and `high` (the range, or the first and last knots).
   - `measure_splines` takes a slice of `SplineData` and returns one `SplineMeasureData` per entry: `start_x`, `start_y`, `end_x`, `end_y`, `length` and `area`.
   - `spline_extremes` takes one `SplineData`, the axis line's origin and unit normal in plane coordinates, and returns the smallest and largest signed distance (section 3.4.4).

The edges of the new kinds report the element kinds `Ellipse`, `Parabola`, `Hyperbola` and `BsplineCurve` (section 3.6 item 3): `BRepAdaptor_Curve` looks through a trimmed curve to its basis. `extrude` makes their lateral faces `Extrusion` surfaces and `revolve` makes them `Revolution` surfaces.

### 2.6 Sweeps

```rust
/// A swept solid with the role of each face.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct SweepResult {
    /// The solid (or compound of solids for a multi-region profile).
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// Role of face `i + 1`.
    pub faces: Vec<SweepFace>,
}

/// The role of a swept face.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum SweepFace {
    /// Swept from the profile edge with this tag.
    Lateral { tag: u32 },
    /// The cap at the start of the sweep.
    StartCap,
    /// The cap at the end of the sweep: the far cap of an extrusion of an offset, and the face where an extrusion up to a face ends.
    EndCap,
    /// Where an extrusion up to a shape ends: a piece of face `face` of that shape (M2, `extrude_to`).
    Target { face: u32 },
}

/// Sweeps the profile from `start` to `end` millimeters along the unit `direction` (start < end).
pub fn extrude(profile: &PlanarProfile, direction: Vector, start: f64, end: f64, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<SweepResult, KernelError>;

/// Where an extrusion ends (M2).
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub enum ExtrudeEnd {
    /// At this offset along the direction, in millimeters, as `extrude`'s `end`.
    Offset(f64),
    /// Where the prism meets the one plane or cylinder that faces `faces` (1-based, at least one) of `shape` lie on,
    /// within those faces.
    Face { shape: Shape, faces: Vec<u32> },
    /// Where the prism first meets the faces of `shape`.
    Shape(Shape),
}

/// Sweeps the profile from `start` millimeters along the unit `direction` to `end`, its lateral faces turned outward
/// by `taper` radians (0 for none, negative inward) (M2, section 3.4.3).
pub fn extrude_to(profile: &PlanarProfile, direction: Vector, start: f64, end: &ExtrudeEnd, taper: f64, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<SweepResult, KernelError>;

/// Revolves the profile about an axis in its plane from angle `start` to angle `end` (right-hand rule about the axis;
/// 0 < |end - start| <= 2 pi; 2 pi is a full revolution). Since M2 it takes a start angle (section 3.4.4).
pub fn revolve(profile: &PlanarProfile, axis: &Axis, start: f64, end: f64, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<SweepResult, KernelError>;
```

`extrude(profile, direction, start, end, interrupt)` is `extrude_to(profile, direction, start, &ExtrudeEnd::Offset(end), 0.0, interrupt)` with the operation `Extrude`: the same checks, the same construction and the same result bytes. `extrude_to` reads the profile's `shape`, `plane` and `edge_tags`, never its `regions`. A `PlanarProfile` that another kernel call builds without `make_profile`, such as item 28's thickened rib profile (wave 3), extrudes the same way. Its spec states that call. Such a profile's `plane` is the plane of its face, since section 3.4.3 items 1.1 and 1.5 check `direction` against `plane.normal`: the rib's direction lies in its sketch plane and is the normal of its thickened face. Up to a shape, `extrude_to` refuses a profile that starts partly inside the shape with `TargetStartsInside` (section 3.4.3 item 3). A rib profile that ends inside its body, section 8.6's `inside`, is such a profile: item 28's spec trims it to the body first or adds a kernel option that skips that check (open issues 21 (e) and 23). A `Face` end takes a list because a face reference can resolve to several faces, such as the pieces of a face that a later feature split, and features/extrude.md ends on them when they lie on one plane or one cylinder ([ADR 0086](../adr/0086-up-to-targets-direction-and-reach-checks.md) decision 1). Until M2, `revolve` took one `angle`. Its M1 calls are `revolve(profile, axis, 0.0, angle, interrupt)`, with the same result. Tests 30 to 33, 106, 138 and 160 call it that way.

```rust
/// Union of the target with every tool. History inputs: [target, tools...].
pub fn fuse(target: &Shape, tools: &[Shape], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;
/// The target minus the union of the tools. History inputs: [target, tools...].
pub fn cut(target: &Shape, tools: &[Shape], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;
/// The part of the target inside every tool. History inputs: [target, tools...].
pub fn common(target: &Shape, tools: &[Shape], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;
```

### 2.8 Fillet, chamfer, shell

```rust
/// One edge to round and its radius.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct EdgeRadius { pub edge: u32, pub radius: f64 }

/// Rounds edges with constant radii. History inputs: [shape].
pub fn fillet(shape: &Shape, edges: &[EdgeRadius], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;

/// The size of a chamfer.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub enum ChamferSize {
    /// The same distance on both faces.
    Equal { distance: f64 },
    /// `distance` measured on `face` (adjacent to the edge), `other_distance` on the other adjacent face.
    TwoDistances { distance: f64, other_distance: f64, face: u32 },
}

/// One edge to chamfer and its size.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct ChamferEdge { pub edge: u32, pub size: ChamferSize }

/// Bevels edges. History inputs: [shape].
pub fn chamfer(shape: &Shape, edges: &[ChamferEdge], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;

/// Hollows a single solid inward with wall `thickness`, opening the removed faces. History inputs: [shape].
pub fn shell(shape: &Shape, remove_faces: &[u32], thickness: f64, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;
```

### 2.9 Transforms, split, section, compound

```rust
/// Applies a similarity transform. Element indices are preserved. History inputs: [shape].
pub fn transform(shape: &Shape, transform: &Transform) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;

/// Which side of a plane: `Positive` is where the plane normal points.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum PlaneSide { Positive, Negative }

/// A face created on the split plane, and the side of the piece it bounds.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct SectionFace { pub face: u32, pub side: PlaneSide }

/// Result of splitting a shape by a plane.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct SplitResult {
    /// The pieces (sharing no sub-shapes) and history. History inputs: [shape].
    pub result: OpResult,
    /// Side of solid `i + 1` of the result.
    pub solid_sides: Vec<PlaneSide>,
    /// Faces created on the plane, ascending by face index.
    pub section_faces: Vec<SectionFace>,
}

/// Splits every solid of the shape by an unbounded plane.
pub fn split(shape: &Shape, plane: &Plane, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<SplitResult, KernelError>;

/// Intersection curves of the shape with an unbounded plane, as a compound of edges.
pub fn section(shape: &Shape, plane: &Plane) -> Result<Shape, KernelError>;

/// Groups shapes into one compound without fusing. History inputs: the shapes in order.
pub fn compound(shapes: &[Shape]) -> Result<OpResult, KernelError>;
```

### 2.10 Element geometry and topology

```rust
/// The geometric kind of a face, edge or vertex (mirrors `partsmith_naming::GeometryKind`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum GeometryKind {
    Plane, Cylinder, Cone, Sphere, Torus, Revolution, Extrusion,
    BsplineSurface, BezierSurface, OffsetSurface, OtherSurface,
    Line, Circle, Ellipse, Hyperbola, Parabola,
    BsplineCurve, BezierCurve, OffsetCurve, OtherCurve,
    Point,
}

/// Geometry of one element, for fingerprints, sketch placement and measurement.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct ElementGeometry {
    /// Surface, curve or point kind.
    pub kind: GeometryKind,
    /// Unit direction as defined per kind in section 3.6.
    pub direction: Option<Vector>,
    /// A characteristic point as defined per kind in section 3.6.
    pub location: Option<Point>,
    /// Radius as defined per kind in section 3.6.
    pub radius: Option<f64>,
    /// Area centroid (face), length centroid (edge) or position (vertex).
    pub centroid: Point,
    /// Area (face), length (edge) or 0 (vertex).
    pub measure: f64,
}

/// Adjacency of every element; all index lists are 1-based, distinct and ascending.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Topology {
    pub faces: Vec<FaceTopology>,
    pub edges: Vec<EdgeTopology>,
    pub vertices: Vec<VertexTopology>,
}

/// One face.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct FaceTopology {
    /// Edges bounding the face, including seams and degenerated edges.
    pub edges: Vec<u32>,
    /// Vertices of the face.
    pub vertices: Vec<u32>,
}

/// One edge.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct EdgeTopology {
    /// Faces containing the edge (one for a seam).
    pub faces: Vec<u32>,
    /// Vertices of the edge (one for a closed edge).
    pub vertices: Vec<u32>,
    /// True for a degenerated edge (no 3D curve, such as a sphere pole).
    pub degenerate: bool,
    /// True when the edge bounds the same face on both sides (a seam).
    pub seam: bool,
}

/// One vertex.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct VertexTopology {
    /// Edges containing the vertex.
    pub edges: Vec<u32>,
    /// Faces containing the vertex.
    pub faces: Vec<u32>,
}

/// An edge at the middle of its parameter range, with the outward normal there of each face containing it and the continuity recorded between them (section 3.6, item 5).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub struct EdgeNormals {
    /// The point of the edge's curve at the middle of its parameter range.
    pub point: Point,
    /// Unit tangent at `point`, running from the edge's first vertex toward its last.
    pub tangent: Vector,
    /// One entry per face containing the edge, ascending by face index (the order of `EdgeTopology::faces`).
    pub faces: Vec<FaceNormal>,
    /// The continuity recorded between the edge's two faces, or for a seam between its face and itself; `None` for an edge with one face that is not a seam, no face, or more than two faces.
    pub continuity: Option<Continuity>,
}

/// The continuity the shape records between the two faces that meet at an edge (`BRep_Tool::Continuity`), as the operation that built the edge set it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Continuity {
    /// Only the positions agree (`GeomAbs_C0`), so the faces may meet at an angle; also the value when nothing is recorded.
    C0,
    /// The tangent planes agree (`GeomAbs_G1`) or better (`C1`, `G2`, `C2`, `C3`, `CN`).
    G1OrBetter,
}

/// One face containing an edge, with its outward unit normal at the edge's middle.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct FaceNormal {
    /// 1-based face index.
    pub face: u32,
    /// Unit surface normal at `EdgeNormals::point`, flipped when the face is reversed, so it points out of the material of a valid solid.
    pub normal: Vector,
}
```

### 2.11 Tessellation

`Shape::tessellate` takes the shared `partsmith_mesh::TessellationTolerance` (mesh.md section 2.4): `chord_deviation` is the maximum distance between a triangle and the surface in millimeters (absolute), and `angular_deviation` the maximum angle in radians between consecutive segments along curves and between adjacent triangle normals. The facade defines no tolerance type of its own; section 3.7 maps the fields to OCCT's parameters. `Shape::element_display` takes the same tolerance and returns:

```rust
/// The display geometry of one face, edge or vertex, in millimeters with locations applied (section 3.7, item 9).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum ElementDisplay {
    /// A face's triangles, three points each, wound counterclockwise seen from outside.
    Triangles(Vec<Point>),
    /// An edge's points from its first vertex to its last; a closed edge repeats its first point at the end.
    Polyline(Vec<Point>),
    /// A vertex's position.
    Position(Point),
}
```

### 2.12 STEP

```rust
/// One body in a STEP file.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct StepBody {
    /// The body's shape (must contain at least one solid when writing).
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// Product name.
    pub name: String,
    /// Surface color, if any.
    pub color: Option<Color>,
}

/// The bodies read from a STEP file, with counts of the content that was left out or could not be repaired.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct StepImport {
    /// One body per solid, in file order.
    pub bodies: Vec<StepBody>,
    /// Open shells, faces outside shells, wires outside faces, edges outside wires and vertices outside edges, left out
    /// because they are not closed solids (section 3.8.1, read item 3).
    pub skipped_shapes: usize,
    /// Solids of `bodies` still invalid after repair, kept as they are (section 3.8.1, read item 3).
    pub invalid_solids: usize,
    /// The same content in the kernel's technical words, for details and logs, never shown as a message: one sentence per
    /// solid still invalid after repair, in body order, then one for the skipped shapes.
    pub warnings: Vec<String>,
}

/// Writes bodies to an AP214 STEP file in millimeters.
pub fn write_step(bodies: &[StepBody], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<Vec<u8>, KernelError>;
/// Reads solids, names and colors from a STEP file, converted to millimeters.
pub fn read_step(bytes: &[u8], interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<StepImport, KernelError>;
```

### 2.13 Errors

```rust
/// A failure in the kernel facade. Implements `std::error::Error`; `Display` writes `user_message()` (section 4.3).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct KernelError {
    /// The facade operation that failed.
    pub operation: Operation,
    /// The classified failure.
    pub kind: KernelErrorKind,
    /// The C++ exception type, such as `StdFail_NotDone`; empty when a Rust argument check raised
    /// the error, and `Partsmith_ReadError` when the Rust check of STEP bytes refused them (section
    /// 4.1).
    pub exception: String,
    /// The raw message from the kernel, or the facade's own message for `InvalidInput`.
    pub message: String,
}

impl KernelError {
    /// The whole plain-language message, for hosts that show a kernel error without a sentence of their own (section 4.3).
    pub fn user_message(&self) -> String;
    /// One sentence that follows a feature's own sentence, "Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build {this}." (section 4.3).
    pub fn hint(&self) -> String;
    /// The technical text for reports: operation, kind, exception and message; never shown as the message (section 4.3).
    pub fn detail(&self) -> String;
}

/// Classification of a failure.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum KernelErrorKind {
    /// The arguments were rejected before or while building; `message` is the facade's sentence of section 4.2.
    InvalidInput,
    /// The kernel could not perform the operation.
    Failed,
    /// The operation produced a shape that stayed invalid after repair.
    InvalidResult,
    /// A mirror or scale `transform` produced a shape that stayed invalid after repair, and the shape it was given was not valid either, such as a solid `read_step` kept although repair could not fix it (section 3.5).
    InvalidInputShape,
    /// The operation produced no solid where one is required.
    EmptyResult,
    /// Input bytes could not be parsed.
    ReadFailed,
    /// An unexpected C++ exception (not an OCCT failure).
    Internal,
    /// The call stopped because its interrupt was requested (section 3.13). Since M2.
    Canceled,
    /// `extrude_to` met no point of the face or shape it was to end on (section 3.4.3 item 5). Since M2.
    TargetNotReached,
    /// `extrude_to` met the face or shape it was to end on with only part of the profile (section 3.4.3 item 5). Since M2.
    TargetPartlyReached,
    /// `extrude_to` up to a shape whose profile starts inside that shape (section 3.4.3 item 3). Since M2.
    TargetStartsInside,
}

/// The facade operation, displayed in snake_case (`make_box`, `write_step`).
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum Operation {
    MakeBox, MakeCylinder, MakeSphere, MakeCone, MakeTorus,
    MakeProfile, Extrude, Revolve,
    /// `extrude_to` (section 2.6). Since M2.
    ExtrudeTo,
    Fuse, Cut, Common,
    Fillet, Chamfer, Shell,
    Transform, Split, Section, Compound,
    Measure, Query, Tessellate,
    WriteStep, ReadStep, WriteBrep, ReadBrep,
    /// `wait_for_interrupt` (section 2.14). Since M2.
    WaitForInterrupt,
    /// `wrap_curves` and `modeled_thread` (section 2.15). Since wave 2 of M2.
    WrapCurves, ModeledThread,
}
```

`Operation` implements `Display` with the snake_case names. The spike's `make_box(dx, dy, dz)` and its tests are replaced by this API.

### 2.14 Interrupts (M2)

```rust
use std::sync::Arc;
use std::sync::atomic::AtomicU64;

/// How a running facade call learns that its host asked it to stop (section 3.13).
/// Clones share their state. `Send + Sync`, cheap to clone.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Interrupt { /* private: a word, the request it waits for, and on the web core whether it also reads the page's word */ }

impl Interrupt {
    /// An interrupt with a word of its own, requested only once `request` is called on it or a clone.
    pub fn new() -> Interrupt;
    /// Requested while `word` holds `request` (the desktop host's flag, engine.md `CancelFlag::word`).
    /// Never requested when `request` is 0.
    pub fn word(word: Arc<AtomicU64>, request: u64) -> Interrupt;
    /// Requested while the web page's cancel word holds `request`, read through the import
    /// `partsmith_cancel_word`, or once `request` was called on it or a clone. Never requested when
    /// `request` is 0.
    #[cfg(target_os = "emscripten")]
    pub fn page(request: u64) -> Interrupt;
    /// Makes it requested: stores its request in its word. Does nothing when its request is 0.
    pub fn request(&self);
    /// True while it is requested: one atomic load, plus one call of the import for `page`. Never panics.
    pub fn is_requested(&self) -> bool;
}

impl Default for Interrupt {
    /// `Interrupt::new()`.
    fn default() -> Interrupt;
}

/// For the tests of callers: polls `interrupt` through OCCT's progress range in a C++ loop about
/// once per millisecond. Fails with kind `Canceled` once it is requested, and returns `Ok(())` once
/// `limit` has passed (section 3.13 item 8).
pub fn wait_for_interrupt(interrupt: &Interrupt, limit: std::time::Duration) -> Result<(), KernelError>;
```

### 2.15 Wrap surfaces, helices and modeled threads (M2)

The parameter-plane map of spikes 8.2 and 8.3 as one code path (section 8.3's Feeds), for the coil's helix (item 19), the modeled thread (item 20) and later item 31's emboss ([ADR 0084](../adr/0084-wrap-curves-and-modeled-threads-in-the-kernel.md), [ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md)). Section 3.4.14 specifies both calls.

```rust
/// A cylinder or a cone about the normal of its frame. Angles on it start at the frame's x axis and turn
/// about the normal by the right-hand rule. Heights are measured along the normal from the frame's origin.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub enum WrapSurface {
    /// A cylinder of `radius`.
    Cylinder { frame: Plane, radius: f64 },
    /// A cone of radius `radius` at height 0 whose radius grows by `tan(half_angle)` per millimeter of height;
    /// a negative `half_angle` narrows it. `half_angle` is the angle between its lines and its axis, in radians.
    Cone { frame: Plane, radius: f64, half_angle: f64 },
}

/// Which way a helix turns as it rises along its surface's normal.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum HelixHand {
    /// Counterclockwise seen from the tip of the normal (a right-hand thread).
    Right,
    /// Clockwise seen from the tip of the normal (a left-hand thread).
    Left,
}

/// A helix on a wrap surface: from angle `phase` at `height`, it rises `pitch` per turn for `turns` turns.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct Helix {
    /// The angle of its start, in radians.
    pub phase: f64,
    /// The height of its start.
    pub height: f64,
    /// Its rise per turn, in millimeters, greater than 0.
    pub pitch: f64,
    /// Its number of turns, greater than 0, not necessarily whole.
    pub turns: f64,
    /// Which way it turns.
    pub hand: HelixHand,
}

/// A curve on a wrap surface. Item 31's emboss (wave 4) adds the developed plane's arcs and B-splines
/// (section 8.3). It derives `Clone` and not `Copy` for them.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq)]
pub enum WrapCurve {
    /// A segment of the developed plane: the plane that touches the surface along its line at angle 0, with x
    /// along the circle at height 0 and y along that line. It maps exactly onto a cylinder only.
    Segment { start: Point2, end: Point2 },
    /// A helix.
    Helix(Helix),
}

/// The piece of a curve an edge or a vertex of `wrap_curves` comes from.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct WrapPiece {
    /// The curve's index in the call's `curves`, from 0.
    pub curve: u32,
    /// For an edge, its turn from the curve's start, from 0 (always 0 for a segment). For a vertex, the number
    /// of the curve's edges before it: 0 at the curve's start, the number of its edges at its end.
    pub piece: u32,
}

/// Curves on a wrap surface as edges.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct WrapResult {
    /// A compound of one wire per curve, in the order of the curves.
    pub shape: Shape,
    /// Generator of edge `i + 1`.
    pub edges: Vec<WrapPiece>,
    /// Generator of vertex `i + 1`.
    pub vertices: Vec<WrapPiece>,
}

/// Maps each curve onto the parameter plane of `surface` as edges with exact curves on it (section 3.4.14).
pub fn wrap_curves(surface: &WrapSurface, curves: &[WrapCurve]) -> Result<WrapResult, KernelError>;

/// A thread's tooth: in each half-plane through the axis, a trapezoid whose inner side, `inner_width` wide
/// along the axis, lies on `surface`, and whose outer side, `outer_width` wide and centered on it, lies `depth`
/// farther from the axis. The lower corner of its inner side runs along `helix`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq)]
pub struct ThreadTooth {
    /// The cylinder of the inner side, or its cone for a tapered thread.
    pub surface: WrapSurface,
    /// The radial distance from the inner side to the outer side.
    pub depth: f64,
    /// The width of the inner side along the axis.
    pub inner_width: f64,
    /// The width of the outer side along the axis.
    pub outer_width: f64,
    /// The helix of the inner side's lower corner; the others follow it with the same pitch, turns and hand.
    pub helix: Helix,
}

/// How a tooth joins its base.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ThreadKind {
    /// Fused to the base. The outer side is the crest, the inner side the root.
    External,
    /// Cut from the base. The inner side is the crest, the outer side the root.
    Internal,
}

/// The crest or the root side of a tooth.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ToothSide { Crest, Root }

/// A flank of a tooth: `Lower` faces against the frame's normal, `Upper` along it.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ToothFlank { Lower, Upper }

/// The role of a face of a tooth. `turn` counts the tooth's pieces from 0 at its start.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ToothFace {
    /// The face of the side over turn `turn`.
    Side { side: ToothSide, turn: u32 },
    /// The face of the flank over turn `turn`.
    Flank { flank: ToothFlank, turn: u32 },
    /// The face that closes the tooth where its helices start.
    Start,
    /// The face that closes the tooth where its helices end.
    End,
}

/// The generator of an edge of a tooth. `position` is 0 at the start and the number of pieces at the end.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum ToothEdge {
    /// The piece over turn `turn` of the helix where `side` meets `flank`.
    Helix { side: ToothSide, flank: ToothFlank, turn: u32 },
    /// The line across `flank` from its root helix to its crest helix at `position`.
    Line { flank: ToothFlank, position: u32 },
    /// The line across `side` from its lower helix to its upper helix at `position`: the start's or the end's
    /// segment, or where the side's face is divided between two turns.
    Across { side: ToothSide, position: u32 },
}

/// The generator of a vertex of a tooth: the point at `position` of the helix where `side` meets `flank`.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct ToothVertex { pub side: ToothSide, pub flank: ToothFlank, pub position: u32 }

/// A modeled thread joined to its base.
#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
pub struct ThreadResult {
    /// The base with the thread, and the history of the join. History inputs: [base, tooth].
    pub result: OpResult,
    /// The tooth, the join's second input.
    pub tooth: Shape,
    /// Role of tooth face `i + 1`.
    pub faces: Vec<ToothFace>,
    /// Generator of tooth edge `i + 1`.
    pub edges: Vec<ToothEdge>,
    /// Generator of tooth vertex `i + 1`.
    pub vertices: Vec<ToothVertex>,
}

/// Builds the tooth, fuses it to `base` (`External`) or cuts it from `base` (`Internal`), and checks that the
/// result kept both (section 3.4.14).
pub fn modeled_thread(base: &Shape, tooth: &ThreadTooth, kind: ThreadKind, interrupt: &Interrupt) -> Result<ThreadResult, KernelError>;
```

The names carry a `Wrap`, `Helix`, `Thread` or `Tooth` prefix so they do not collide with the feature types of features/helix.md, features/coil.md and features/thread.md inside `partsmith-features`.

## 3. Behavior

### 3.1 Conventions

1. Units: millimeters and radians. Coordinates are right-handed with Z up (architecture conventions).
2. Tolerances: `LINEAR` is the public `LINEAR_TOLERANCE` = 1e-6 mm (the value of `partsmith_model::LINEAR_TOLERANCE`; this crate does not depend on `partsmith-model`, so it defines the same constant and a test in `partsmith-features` asserts equality), `ANGULAR` = 1e-9 rad.
3. Immutability: no facade function modifies any input `Shape`, including tolerances, pcurves and triangulations of its sub-shapes. Where an OCCT algorithm may write to its inputs, the shim passes a copy (`BRepBuilderAPI_Copy`) and composes the copy's history. Booleans run in non-destructive mode, and on copies of the target and tools that share their geometry (`BRepBuilderAPI_Copy` without geometry): the boolean itself leaves its arguments unchanged, but the unification of section 3.4.5 adds and removes curves on faces of the edges the result shares with its arguments, also when the boolean then fails (test 114). A copy has the input's indices, so its history is the input's.
4. Validation order: every function first checks its arguments in Rust (finite values, ranges, index bounds, plane and axis validity) and returns `InvalidInput` with the exact messages of section 4.2 without calling C++; checks that need geometry run in the shim and raise `Partsmith_InvalidInput` with the same kind of message.
5. A `Plane` is valid when `normal` and `x_axis` have length at least 1e-12 and, after normalizing, `abs(normal . x_axis) <= 1e-9`. An `Axis` is valid when its direction has length at least 1e-12. The facade normalizes both before use.
6. Every `f64` argument must be finite; otherwise `InvalidInput` with `A value is not a finite number.`
7. Result normalization: an operation whose result has exactly one solid returns that `Solid`; a result with several solids returns a `Compound` of solids; a boolean with no remaining solid returns an empty `Compound`. Solid operations never return free faces, edges or vertices.

### 3.2 Indexing

1. Sub-shape `i` of kind `K` is the `i`-th entry (1-based) of OCCT's `TopExp::MapShapes(shape, K, map)` with a `TopTools_IndexedMapOfShape`. Orientation is ignored (`IsSame` semantics). Index 0 is never valid.
2. The shim's `Shape` builds the five index maps (solids, shells, faces, edges, vertices) once when it is created and never changes them, so `count`, `kind` and index lookups are O(1) and cannot fail.
3. Indices are deterministic: the same operation on the same inputs gives the same indices. They are preserved by `transform` (element `i` of the input is element `i` of the output, for every kind) and by the BRep round trip (`from_brep(to_brep(s))` has the same counts and element `i` has the same geometry). They are not stable across other operations; history relates them.
4. `locate(part, kind)` matches sub-shapes by `IsSame` (same underlying `TShape` and location). `solids()` returns shapes that share sub-shapes with `self`, so `self.locate(&solid, Face)` maps every face of a solid.
5. Indices never leave `partsmith-features` (architecture rule); this crate exposes them only to it.

### 3.3 History semantics

The shim builds `History` from OCCT's `BRepTools_History` (or the algorithm's `Modified`, `Generated` and `IsDeleted`), normalized as follows, for every input and every face, edge and vertex `s` of that input:

1. If `s` itself is in the result (same `TShape` and location), `modified = [its output index]` (unchanged elements list themselves; OCCT's `Modified` is empty for them).
2. Otherwise `modified` is the output indices of OCCT's `Modified(s)` that are in the result, deduplicated and ascending. Images that are not in the final result (for example removed by a later internal step) are dropped.
3. `generated` is OCCT's `Generated(s)` mapped to `SubShape`s that are in the result (any kind among face, edge, vertex), deduplicated and sorted by kind then index.
4. An element is deleted exactly when `modified` is empty, whether or not it generated something (a filleted edge is deleted and generates a face).
5. When an operation runs several OCCT steps (boolean then unification, splitter then copy, algorithm then repair), the histories are merged (`BRepTools_History::Merge`) so `History` always relates the facade inputs to the final result.
6. A merged output element (for example two coplanar faces unified into one) appears in the `modified` list of every input element it came from.
7. **Edges and vertices** (M2, [ADR 0060](../adr/0060-direct-generator-names-for-edges-and-vertices.md)). Items 1 to 6 hold for edges and vertices as for faces, in every operation that returns `OpResult`. A kept edge or vertex lists its one image in `modified`. An edge cut in pieces lists every piece. An edge that unification made from several input edges is in the `modified` list of each of them. An edge or vertex that the operation removed lists nothing, such as an edge a fillet rounds away or the edges and vertices where a fuse joins two faces into one. The shim augments OCCT's history wherever it falls short of this item, as section 3.4.9 does for the faces of a shell. On 2026-11-07 at a3f4573, OCCT's history as the shim merges it (`cpp/history.cpp`) met this item with no augmentation for the fuse, cut, fillet, split and shell of tests 146 to 150. naming.md section 3.12 carries the direct-generator names of edges and vertices through these lists. An edge or vertex whose image is missing loses the names it held.
8. **Generators of later operations** (M2). An operation added in M2 that makes edges or vertices from something a feature can name reports that generator for each such edge and vertex in its result type, as `SweepResult::faces` reports face roles: one entry per output edge and per output vertex, in index order, or none for an element without a generator. A generator is a profile curve or an end of one, a path segment, or a part of the operation's own construction, such as a helix, a thread's crest or root, or the start or end of a curve. The shim reads it from the history of the OCCT builder that made the shape (`BRepBuilderAPI_MakeShape::Generated`, `BRepPrimAPI_MakeSweep::FirstShape` and `LastShape`, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipe::Generated` with the spine and profile shapes, `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections::GeneratedFace`). It never matches positions. The spec of each such operation names its result type when its feature lands (naming.md section 3.12.8). Since wave 2, `wrap_curves` reports the curve and the piece of each edge and vertex (`WrapResult`), and `modeled_thread` the role of each face of its tooth and the generator of each edge and vertex there (`ThreadResult`): the helices where the crest and the root meet the flanks, the lines across the flanks and the sides at the start, the end and each turn, and the points where they meet (section 3.4.14 item 6). It reports them on the tooth, the join's second input, whose images in the result the join's history lists (item 7 here). The sweep's and the coil's calls name their result types in stage 2 of wave 2, from spike 8.8. Section 8 records which history each spike's construction offers. `extrude_to` (wave 2, section 3.4.3) makes its edges and vertices from profile curves and from the target, but reports face roles only, as `extrude` does: naming names a sweep's edges and vertices by the faces that meet there (naming.md section 3.6). Its roles come from the merged history of the prism, the taper and the split (section 3.4.3 item 7), never from positions.

Per-operation specifics are in section 3.4. Every operation documents its `inputs` order.

### 3.4 Operations

#### 3.4.1 Primitives

Built in the placement frame `gp_Ax2(origin, normal, x_axis)`. Face roles (Partsmith conventions, which differ from OCCT's `BRepPrimAPI_MakeBox::FrontFace` naming; the shim classifies by geometry in the local frame, not by OCCT's accessor names):

- Box: `Left` local x = 0, `Right` x = length, `Front` y = 0, `Back` y = width, `Bottom` z = 0, `Top` z = height. 6 faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices.
- Cylinder: `Side`, `Bottom` (z = 0), `Top` (z = height). 3 faces, 3 edges (two circles and the seam, which lies in the local xz half-plane on the +x side), 2 vertices.
- Cone: `Side`, `Bottom` (absent when `bottom_radius == 0`), `Top` (absent when `top_radius == 0`). With both radii positive: 3 faces, 3 edges, 2 vertices; with one radius 0: 2 faces, 3 edges (one degenerated at the apex), 2 vertices.
- Sphere: `Surface`. 1 face, 3 edges (the seam in the local xz half-plane on the +x side, two degenerated edges at the poles), 2 vertices (the poles).
- Torus: `Surface`. 1 face, 2 edges (both seams), 1 vertex.

Validation: a size that must be positive must be at least `LINEAR`, because OCCT rejects smaller ones with generic exceptions or builds degenerate solids. Box dimensions at least `LINEAR`; cylinder radius and height at least `LINEAR`; sphere radius at least `LINEAR`; cone height at least `LINEAR`, each radius 0 or at least `LINEAR`, and the radii differing by at least `LINEAR` (one may be 0); torus minor radius at least `LINEAR` and major radius exceeding the minor radius by at least `LINEAR`. The messages (section 4.2) still say "greater than 0 mm", since smaller values display as 0 mm.

#### 3.4.2 Profiles

`make_profile(plane, regions)`:

1. `regions` must be non-empty and every loop non-empty. A `Circle` must be the only curve in its loop. Lines must have length at least `LINEAR`; arcs must have a radius of at least `LINEAR`, each of `start` and `end` within `LINEAR` of the circle (`abs(norm(p - center) - radius) <= LINEAR`), and `start != end` (beyond `LINEAR`); circles must have radius at least `LINEAR`. An arc's edge lies on the exact circle and its vertices get a tolerance of at least `LINEAR`, so profile vertices that sketch.md places within `PROFILE_TOLERANCE` of their curves (sketch.md section 3.13.2) build.
2. Chain: curves are traversed in list order. The first curve's direction is chosen so its end meets the second curve (at either of the second curve's endpoints); each following curve is traversed starting at the point where the previous one ended. Consecutive curves, and the last and first, must meet within `LINEAR`; otherwise `InvalidInput` naming the two tags and the gap, which is the smaller of the distances from the end of the first curve to the two endpoints of the next. Junction vertices are shared, so a loop's edges are connected exactly.
3. Orientation: the shim orients each outer loop counterclockwise and each hole clockwise in plane coordinates (by signed area), so every face's outward normal equals the plane normal.
4. Output: one face per region, each curve producing exactly one edge (curves are never merged or split), so `edge_tags` has one entry per curve; `edge_tags[i]` is the tag of the curve that produced edge `i + 1`. Tags need not be unique.
5. The face must pass `BRepCheck`; a loop crossing itself or another loop, or a hole outside its outer loop, gives `InvalidInput` (`The profile region crosses itself or has a hole outside its outer boundary.`).
6. Regions are not checked against each other. Callers pass disjoint regions; adjacent regions sharing a boundary would produce touching solids (the features spec merges adjacent selected regions first).
7. **Conic and B-spline curves** (M2, section 2.5). Items 1 to 6 hold for them, with these checks in this order, after the finite-value check of section 3.1 item 6 (every coordinate, radius, pole, weight, knot and range value):
   1. Sizes. An `Ellipse`, `EllipticalArc` or `Hyperbola` needs `norm(major)` and `minor_radius` of at least `LINEAR`, else `Curve <tag> of the profile needs radii greater than 0 mm.` A `Parabola` needs `norm(focus - vertex)` of at least `LINEAR`, else `Curve <tag> of the profile needs its focus away from its vertex.`
   2. Splines, in this order. `degree` from 1 to 25 (OCCT's `BSplCLib::MaxDegree()`, equal to sketch.md's `MAX_SPLINE_DEGREE`), else `Spline <tag> needs a degree from 1 to 25.` At least 2 knots and one multiplicity per knot. The knots increase, each greater than the one before it by at least 1e-6 of the knot range (the last knot minus the first) and by more than OCCT's `Epsilon` of the absolute value of the one before. `Epsilon` (`Standard_Real.hxx`) is `f64::MIN_POSITIVE` at 0 and otherwise the distance from the value to the next larger `f64`. `Geom2d_BSplineCurve` throws at or below it. OCCT builds curves with closer knots but integrates them wrong. On test 199's spline (range 2) with its inner knot moved to 1e-10, `GCPnts_AbscissaPoint::Length` measured half the curve, and at 1e-12 the face's area was 3.4e-5 off. At 1e-9 the area was 1e-8 off, and at 1e-6 and 2e-6 within 5e-11 (measured on 2026-11-23). Every multiplicity is from 1 to `degree`. An open spline's first and last are `degree + 1` instead: an open spline is clamped, its ends at its first and last poles, as sketch.md section 3.20.5 makes it. A periodic spline's first and last multiplicities are equal, and its knots give at least `degree + 1` poles. Else `Spline <tag> has knots out of order, too close together or with the wrong multiplicities.` Exactly as many poles as the knots and degree give (`BSplCLib::NbPoles`: the sum of the multiplicities less `degree + 1` for an open spline, less the last multiplicity for a periodic one), else `Spline <tag> needs <count> poles for its knots and degree.` with the count it needs. `weights`, when given, holds one value per pole, each greater than `f64::MIN_POSITIVE` (OCCT's `gp::Resolution()`), else `Spline <tag> needs one weight greater than 0 per pole.`
   3. Ranges. A `range` `[low, high]` needs `low < high`. On an open spline both lie within its knots, `knots[0] <= low` and `high <= knots[knots.len() - 1]`. On a periodic spline `high - low` is less than the period, the last knot minus the first. Otherwise `Spline <tag> has a range outside its knots.`
   4. Closed curves (section 2.5 item 4). A closed curve in a loop of more than one curve fails with `Closed curve <tag> must be the only curve in its loop.` A `Circle` keeps its message of item 1.
   5. Zero length. An `EllipticalArc`, `Parabola` or `Hyperbola` whose `start` and `end` lie within `LINEAR` of each other has zero length, as an arc does, and so does a `Bspline` whose measured length (section 2.5 item 6) is below `LINEAR`: `Curve <tag> of the profile has zero length.`
   6. Ends on the curve. An `EllipticalArc`, `Parabola` or `Hyperbola` whose `start` or `end` lies farther than `LINEAR` from its conic fails with `Curve <tag> does not have its start and end on its curve.` The facade checks the conics in its own arithmetic. With `p` the point and `q = p - center` (`q = p - vertex` for a parabola), it finds the parameter `t` of the nearest point of the conic by Newton's method on `(curve(t) - p) . curve'(t) = 0`, from `t0 = atan2((q . n) / b, (q . u) / a)` on an ellipse, `t0 = q . m` on a parabola and `t0 = asinh((q . n) / b)` on a hyperbola, stopping once a step is below `1e-15 * max(1, abs(t))` or after 50 steps, and compares `norm(p - curve(t))` with `LINEAR`. The same `t` is the parameter of the point for the signed area below. The shim trims the conic at the nearest points of its junction vertices instead (section 2.5 item 5), as it trims an arc. A junction vertex lies at the midpoint of the two curve ends that meet there, within `LINEAR / 2` of each (item 2). An edge's end then lies within `2 LINEAR` of the point checked here, and its vertex's tolerance covers the distance from the junction to it. A `Bspline` has no such check: its ends are its measured points (section 2.5 item 3), and the chain compares them with its neighbors.
   7. Items 2 to 5 then run as for the M1 kinds, with the ends of section 2.5 item 3 in the chain and the signed area below in item 3.

   The checks of a call run in this order, and the first failure ends the call. First section 3.1 item 6's check of every value, section 3.1 item 5's check of the plane, and item 1's check that `regions` is not empty. Then checks 1 to 3 above, curve by curve over every curve of the call: regions in order, each region's outer loop before its holes, each loop's curves in list order. A spline must be well formed before the shim builds it. Then the shim measures every `Bspline` of the call (section 2.5 item 6). Then each loop, in the same order: item 1's check that the loop is not empty and its `Circle` check, check 4, then curve by curve item 1's checks of a line, an arc or a circle and checks 5 and 6 of a conic or a spline, then the chain of item 2 and the signed area of item 3. A call of lines, arcs and circles alone is checked in M1's order.

   Signed area (item 3) is the sum, over the loop's curves in chain direction, of `(x(t) y'(t) - y(t) x'(t)) / 2` integrated along each curve, as the M1 kinds sum it. For the conics it has a closed form. With `s` and `e` the curve's start and end in chain direction, `cross(p, q) = p.x q.y - p.y q.x` and `t0` and `t1` the parameters of `s` and `e` from item 7.6 (sub-item 6), an elliptical arc adds `(cross(center, e - s) + a b (t1 - t0)) / 2`, with `t1 - t0` the counterclockwise sweep in `(0, 2 pi)` for a counterclockwise traversal and its negative for a clockwise one; a parabola `(cross(vertex, e - s) - (t1^3 - t0^3) / (12 f)) / 2`; a hyperbola `(cross(center, e - s) + a b (t1 - t0)) / 2`; and a full ellipse `pi a b`, negated when the chain runs it clockwise. A `Bspline` adds the `area` that `measure_splines` returns for its own direction, negated when the chain runs it backward. `measure_splines` integrates it on each knot span inside the range by Gauss-Legendre quadrature with `degree + 1` points for a polynomial spline (exact up to rounding: the integrand has degree `2 degree - 1`) and `2 degree + 2` points for a rational one, using `Geom2d_Curve::D1`. A loop whose absolute area is at most `LINEAR^2` fails with the invalid region message, as in M1.

#### 3.4.3 Extrude

`extrude(profile, direction, start, end)` sweeps the profile translated by `start * d` to the profile translated by `end * d`, where `d` is the normalized `direction`. Requirements: `direction` nonzero; `abs(d . plane.normal) > ANGULAR` (the direction must leave the plane; oblique directions are allowed); `end - start >= LINEAR`. Face roles: each lateral face carries the tag of the profile edge that generated it; `StartCap` is the face at `start`, `EndCap` the face at `end`. Every face has exactly one role. Profile edges never merge, so lateral faces correspond one to one with profile edges.

`extrude_to(profile, direction, start, end, taper)` (M2, item 17, [ADR 0083](../adr/0083-extrusions-that-end-on-a-face-or-a-shape-and-revolutions-between-two-angles.md)) builds the constructions that kernel spike 8.5 measured and picked: the split prism up to a face or a shape, and `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle` for a taper. With `d` the normalized `direction`, `n` the plane's normal and the moved profile the profile translated by `start * d`:

1. **Checks**, after section 3.1 items 4 to 6 and the profile check of `extrude`, in this order. Each fails with `InvalidInput` and its message of section 4.2. Two unit vectors are parallel, either way, when the length of their cross product is at most `ANGULAR`: the angle between their lines is then at most `ANGULAR`.
   1. `direction` is nonzero and leaves the plane, as for `extrude`.
   2. `Offset(end)`: `end - start >= LINEAR`, as for `extrude`.
