Walks the free shapes depth first in document order, applying assembly locations, and returns one `StepBody` per solid in that order. `name` is the name of the nearest labeled ancestor (the part), trimmed; an empty or missing name stays empty (the engine names the imported body, engine.md section 3.14). `color` is the solid's surface color, else its generic color, else its part's surface or generic color, else `None`; its components are read with `Quantity_Color::Values(Quantity_TOC_sRGB)`, so a color written by `write_step` reads back unchanged.
3. Each solid is repaired with `ShapeFix_Shape` (precision `LINEAR`). A solid still invalid is kept, counted in `invalid_solids`, and adds the sentence `Solid <n> is not valid after repair; booleans with it may fail.` to `warnings`, `<n>` its 1-based position in `bodies`. A kept solid can have no volume, such as one whose void is its own outer shell (`SELF_VOID` of engine.md test 162, its faces once in each shell): its volume is 0 up to rounding, which the engine reports as a body without volume, not as a failure (engine.md section 3.11). Closed shells that are not solids are turned into solids (`BRepBuilderAPI_MakeSolid` plus `ShapeFix_Solid`); open shells, free faces, wires, edges and points are skipped and counted in `skipped_shapes`, and when any are, one sentence follows the solids' sentences: `Skipped 1 shape that is not a closed solid.` for one, `Skipped <k> shapes that are not closed solids.` for more. Callers word the counts (features/import.md section 3.3); the sentences are technical text, like `KernelError::detail()`, which no host shows as a message.
4. Bytes that do not parse give `ReadFailed`; a file with no solid gives `EmptyResult`. Since M2, a file whose check list holds a fail also gives `ReadFailed`, before any transfer ([ADR 0067](../adr/0067-fuzzing-with-cargo-fuzz-in-a-crate-outside-the-workspace.md) choice 10, delivery.md section 3.8 finding 1). After `ReadStream` returns `IFSelect_RetDone` and after the check for nothing to transfer, the shim reads the complete check list of the reader's work session, `reader.ChangeReader().WS()->ModelCheckList(Standard_True)`: the load check of every entity and OCCT's semantic checks, of which OCCT 7.9.3 keeps the last entity's alone (open issue 20). When it holds a fail (`IsEmpty(Standard_True)` is false), the call raises `Partsmith_ReadError` with the message `STEPCAFControl_Reader found <n> fails in the file's check list, the first: <text>`. `<n>` is the sum of `NbFails()` over the list's checks. The message reads `1 fail` when it is 1. `<text>` is `CFail(1)` of the first check in the list's order that holds a fail. A list with warnings and no fail reads as before. The order keeps the empty file of test 83 an `EmptyResult`: both its lists hold one fail, `DATA NOT AVAILABLE FOR CHECK`, and it has nothing to transfer. OCCT 7.9.3 transfers a file with fails. A transfer over an unresolved reference can read through a null entity. On a native build that ends the process on a signal (section 3.9 items 7 and 9). Measured on 2026-11-07 with a probe that reads as the shim does: the finding's input holds 54 fails in 23 checks; the import example's STEP file without its line `#13 = DIRECTION('',(0.,0.,1.));` holds 2 and transferred before this item; the two STEP files of `examples/`, that file with an unknown entity added, and the STEP texts of tests 135, 139 to 141 and 144 (`VOID_TWICE`, `BOTH` and `SELF_VOID` among them) hold none.
5. Round trip: `read_step(write_step(bodies))` returns the same number of bodies in order, with names and colors preserved and volumes within 1e-6 relative error.
6. Since M2, `read_step` checks the bytes in Rust before it calls the shim (`check_step_bytes` in `src/step_check.rs`, [ADR 0073](../adr/0073-a-check-of-step-bytes-and-a-patch-to-occt-7-9-3.md), delivery.md section 3.8 findings 2 to 4 and 7). The check runs before the STEP mutex and before the first poll (section 3.13 item 2). It refuses with `ReadFailed`, exception `Partsmith_ReadError` and the texts of section 4.3. It reads the bytes with the rules of OCCT 7.9.3's lexer, `src/StepFile/step.lex`:
   - `/*` to the first `*/` after it is a comment. An unclosed comment runs to the end of the bytes.
   - A string runs from an apostrophe to the next apostrophe that optional spaces, double quotes, CR and LF and then `)` or `,` follow. A doubled apostrophe and one before any other character stay in the string.
   - `#<digits>` followed by optional spaces and tabs and then `=` defines an instance. Any other `#<digits>` is a reference. Leading zeros name the same number.
   - Nothing after `END-ISO[0-9-]*;` is read. `ENDSTEP;` takes the rest of its line.
   - Every other token is the longest match of the lexer's rules, the first rule winning a tie. A word is the longest run of letters, digits and `_`. A keyword inside a word is part of the word.
   - A reference belongs to the instance whose definition comes last before it. The references of every part of a complex instance are that instance's.

   It reads the text once, in order. The first instance number larger than 2147483647, in a definition or a reference, refuses the file with the message `the STEP file's instance number #<digits> is larger than 2147483647`, the digits as the file writes them: OCCT reads them with `atoi` into an `int`. The second definition of a number refuses the file with `the STEP file defines the instance number #<n> more than once, again on line <l>`, n the number without leading zeros and l the line of that definition. When a number is defined more than once, OCCT 7.9.3 numbers the records in a second pass (`StepData_StepReaderData::SetEntityNumbers`), which reads entries of two tables it never set. Valgrind reports such reads for test 189's file, and the fuzzer stopped on a timeout inside that pass (delivery.md section 3.8 finding 7). OCCT already gave a file that defines a number twice outside a scope the fail `Ident defined SEVERAL TIMES` in its check list. `&SCOPE` or `ENDSCOPE` refuses the file with `the STEP file opens or closes a scope on line <l>`. A scope is the one place where ISO 10303-21 lets a file define a number twice. OCCT 7.9.3 does not process the export list of an `ENDSCOPE`, and its parser prints `***  Warning : Export List not yet processed` to standard output for one, past the messenger (section 3.9 item 7, test 190). The first of these rules, or of item 7's, that the text breaks refuses the file. Then the check walks the references depth first with an explicit stack, never by recursion. It starts from each instance in the order the file defines them that it has not reached yet, and follows each instance's references in the order of its text. A reference to a number the file does not define leads nowhere. The first cycle the walk closes refuses the file with `the STEP file's instance references form a cycle: #<h> reaches itself through <k> references`, with `1 reference` when k is 1: `#<h>` is the cycle's instance that the walk reached first, and k the number of references along the cycle. The first instance the walk finishes whose longest chain of references holds more than 500 refuses the file with `the STEP file's instance references form a chain of more than 500 references from #<h>`. Unresolved references, unknown entity types and every other rule of the schema stay OCCT's to check (read item 4). Measured on 2026-11-15 (ADR 0073's note): every STEP file Git tracks, the 270 STEP files `partsmith export` writes for the examples and OCCT's own `data/step/linkrods.step` and `screw.step` pass, and their deepest chain holds 15 references. The check took 14.3 ms on the 1,588,996 bytes of `bench/fifty-features`' export, measured while OCCT built on all four cores, where `ReadStream` takes 130 ms.
7. Since M2, the same check refuses a parameter list that breaks STEP's syntax, in every section, with the message `the STEP file breaks STEP's syntax inside a parameter list on line <n>`: n is the line of the token that breaks it, lines counted by LF as OCCT counts them. A parameter list starts at a `(` right after a word, as an entity's own list and a typed parameter do. It holds nothing, or parameters separated by single commas, and ends at `)`. A parameter is a reference, a value of one token (a number, a string, an enumeration, a binary, `$`, `*` and any other character the lexer reads alone), a list, or a word followed by a list. Anything else inside a parameter list refuses the file: an empty parameter, two parameters without a comma, a word without its list, a definition, `;`, `=`, `/`, a section keyword, `&SCOPE` or `ENDSCOPE`. Parentheses outside a parameter list, such as a complex instance's own and those of text that OCCT's parser skips, are not checked. OCCT's recovery from a broken list left records that `StepData_StepReaderData::SetEntityNumbers` could not number, and `Interface_FileReaderTool::LoadedEntity` read a null entity (delivery.md section 3.8 finding 3). Every file it refuses breaks ISO 10303-21, or is one that OCCT's lexer misreads: a string with a doubled apostrophe right before `,` or `)` ends at that apostrophe for OCCT (open issue 19). OCCT 7.9.3's parser records a syntax fail for each such list in the check list of read item 4.
8. Since M2, OCCT 7.9.3 is built with Partsmith's patch `scripts/occt-7.9.3.patch` ([ADR 0073](../adr/0073-a-check-of-step-bytes-and-a-patch-to-occt-7-9-3.md), delivery.md section 3.8 findings 5 and 6). It makes two changes. `RWStepShape_RWOrientedEdge::ReadStep` gives the fail `edge_element is an oriented_edge` for an oriented edge whose edge element is an oriented edge (ISO 10303-42 oriented_edge WR1), and drops that edge element. `StepShape_OrientedEdge::EdgeStart` and `EdgeEnd` then follow it at most one step. A cycle of oriented edges that OCCT's error recovery builds, which the bytes do not hold, no longer overflows the stack inside `ReadStream` before read item 4 reads the check list (finding 6, test 188). `RWStepShape_RWEdgeCurve::ReadStep` asks for a vertex point at both ends of an edge curve. A plain `VERTEX`, or any other vertex, gives the load fail `Parameter n0.2 (edge_start) : Entity has illegal type`, or the same with `n0.3 (edge_end)`. Read item 4 then refuses the file before the transfer, whose `StepToTopoDS_TranslateVertex::Init` would read the missing point (finding 5, test 176).

#### 3.8.2 BRep

`to_brep` writes `BinTools::Write` format version 4 (`BinTools_FormatVersion_VERSION_4`) without triangulations and normals to a memory stream; `from_brep` reads it with `BinTools::Read`. Bytes are deterministic for a given shape. Counts, indices and element geometry survive the round trip exactly (volume of the reloaded shape equals the original bit for bit). Bytes that are not a valid stream give `ReadFailed`. These bytes are a cache format: they are only read by the same OCCT version that wrote them, and the cache key includes the OCCT version (`OCCT_VERSION`).

### 3.9 Shim rules

1. **Layout.** `cpp/shim.h` declares the C++ `Shape` class, the `rust::behavior::trycatch` customization and the shim's exception types; the implementation is split by area (`cpp/shape.cpp`, `cpp/primitives.cpp`, `cpp/profiles.cpp`, `cpp/sweeps.cpp`, `cpp/booleans.cpp`, `cpp/blends.cpp` (fillet, chamfer, shell), `cpp/transforms.cpp`, `cpp/queries.cpp`, `cpp/measure.cpp`, `cpp/mesh.cpp`, `cpp/mesh_algorithm.cpp` (the face algorithms of section 3.7 item 2), `cpp/exchange.cpp`, `cpp/validity.cpp`, `cpp/history.cpp`), with `cpp/double_double.h` (the arithmetic of section 3.7 item 2) and `cpp/delabella/` (`delabella.h` and `delabella.cpp`, the shim's copy of OCCT's Delabella, MIT, ADR 0039). `cpp/internal.h` declares the helpers the sources share. Since M2, `cpp/interrupt.cpp` holds the progress indicator, the web core's import and `wait_for_interrupt` (section 3.13). Since wave 2 of M2, `cpp/wrap.cpp` holds the parameter-plane map and `wrap_curves`, and `cpp/thread.cpp` holds `modeled_thread` (section 3.4.14). `build.rs` lists every `.cpp` file in `SOURCES`.
2. **Exceptions never cross.** `cpp/shim.h` keeps the spike's `trycatch` customization unchanged in behavior: it is `noexcept`, catches `Standard_Failure` and reports `"<DynamicType()->Name()>: <GetMessageString()>"`, then `std::exception` as `"std::exception: <what()>"`, then everything else as `"unknown: unknown C++ exception"`. cxx uses it for every bridge function declared with `Result`. Every bridge function that can fail is declared returning `Result<...>` in `src/ffi.rs`; the only functions declared without `Result` are those that read values cached at `Shape` construction (kind, counts) and the one creating `Shape::empty()`, whose only failure mode is allocation failure, which terminates the process exactly as a Rust allocation failure does. Since M2 the web core's `page_cancel_word` is declared without `Result` too: it reads one number and cannot fail (section 3.13 item 7). Errors from `solids` and `locate` use operation `Query`.
3. **Failures without exceptions.** When an OCCT algorithm reports failure through `IsDone()`, `HasErrors()` or a status code instead of throwing, the shim throws `StdFail_NotDone` with a message naming the algorithm and its status (for booleans the alert names from `DumpErrors`, for fillets and chamfers the `ChFiDS_ErrorStatus` of each faulty contour, for offsets the `BRepOffset_Error` value).
4. **Shim exception types.** Defined with `DEFINE_STANDARD_EXCEPTION` in `shim.h`, deriving from `Standard_Failure`: `Partsmith_InvalidInput` (geometry-dependent argument checks; message is the sentence of section 4.2), `Partsmith_InvalidResult`, `Partsmith_InvalidInputShape` (section 3.5), `Partsmith_EmptyResult`, `Partsmith_ReadError`, since M2 `Partsmith_Canceled` (section 3.13), and since wave 2 of M2 `Partsmith_TargetNotReached`, `Partsmith_TargetPartlyReached` and `Partsmith_TargetStartsInside` (section 3.4.3 items 3 and 5).
5. **Data across the bridge.** Shapes cross as `SharedPtr<Shape>` (a C++ `std::shared_ptr<partsmith::occt::Shape>`, never null; the facade treats a null pointer as `Internal`). Results cross as cxx shared structs and `Vec`s declared in the bridge module (for example flat index arrays for history), which the facade converts to the public types. No raw pointers, references into C++ memory or C++ containers outlive a call. Since M2 an `&Interrupt` crosses as a reference to an opaque Rust type of the bridge's `extern "Rust"` block, whose one method, `is_requested`, the indicator calls (section 3.13 item 3). The C++ side keeps it only for the call that received it.
6. **The C++ `Shape`** holds a `TopoDS_Shape` and its five index maps, all `const` after construction.
7. **No side channels.** The shim never prints, never calls `exit` or `abort`, never installs signal handlers (`OSD::SetSignal` is not called), never reads environment variables in its own code, and never writes files. The copy of Delabella holds to the same: it has no default error log and the base algorithm sets none, so it prints nothing; and its internal checks, which OCCT's copy writes as `assert` (compiled out in OCCT's release build, an abort in a build without `NDEBUG`, as `cc` makes), throw `std::logic_error`. The base algorithm catches it and triangulates the face's nodes with OCCT's Watson algorithm (section 3.7 item 2). Since M2 the copy also checks that each point it adds gives the hull exactly two more faces and that the edges it sees form one loop, where OCCT's copy reads vertices it never set. Rounding on cocircular nodes in mirror-symmetric positions (the corners of a square with round corners) can break both, and the copy then crashed the process natively and trapped or hung in the web core (test 172, [ADR 0072](../adr/0072-a-face-that-breaks-delabellas-hull-falls-back-to-watson.md)).
8. **Rust facade.** Every `unsafe` block or impl has a `// SAFETY:` comment. The only `unsafe` code is the `unsafe extern "C++"` block and the `Send` and `Sync` impls of `Shape`. No cxx type appears in a public signature. Since M2 the web core's `page_cancel_word` is declared in that block (section 3.13 item 7), and the `extern "Rust"` block of section 3.13 item 3 needs no `unsafe`.
9. **Unwinding.** The shim and the cxx bridge compile with standard C++ unwinding on every compiler, so an exception that leaves a function destroys its automatic objects, the lock guards of section 3.10 among them. GCC, Clang and Emscripten (`-fwasm-exceptions`) do so by default; MSVC only with `/EHsc`, which `build.rs` passes (section 3.12 item 5). Without it cl.exe destroys no automatic object in the frames an exception leaves (warning C4530), and on `windows-2025` a failed fillet and an unreadable STEP file left their mutexes locked, so later blends and STEP calls failed on the same thread and blocked forever on the others (delivery.md open issue 1 (g)). `cpp/shim.h` stops the build on MSVC without it: `#if defined(_MSC_VER) && !defined(_CPPUNWIND)`, then `#error "partsmith-occt-sys needs C++ unwinding: compile the shim with /EHsc"`. Not `/EHa`: a structured exception such as an access violation ends the process, as a signal does on the other platforms, instead of being caught as `Internal`.

### 3.10 Thread safety

1. `Shape` is `Send + Sync`: the C++ object is immutable after construction, OCCT's `Standard_Transient` reference counts are atomic, and no facade function writes to an input (section 3.1 item 3: algorithms that write to their arguments, booleans included, run on copies; tests 43 and 114).
2. Every facade function may be called concurrently from any number of threads, on distinct or shared shapes. Results do not depend on concurrency.
3. OCCT internal parallelism is off (`SetRunParallel(false)`, `InParallel = false`), so OCCT starts no threads and results are identical on desktop and WebAssembly. Parallelism belongs to the recompute layer.
4. OCCT's data exchange layer keeps process-wide state (controller registration, `Interface_Static`). `write_step` and `read_step` are therefore serialized by one mutex inside the shim, and the one-time data exchange initialization runs under `std::call_once`.
5. OCCT's fillet and chamfer builder (`ChFi3d_Builder`, behind `BRepFilletAPI_MakeFillet` and `BRepFilletAPI_MakeChamfer`) assembles every result with the old Boolean builder `TopOpeBRepBuild`, which keeps state between calls in file-level statics. One of them, `STATIC_SOLIDINDEX` in `TopOpeBRepBuild_Builder.cxx`, tells `SplitShapes` which solid an edge is classified against; blends built on several threads at once classified edges against each other's solids, even on distinct shapes, and with 8 threads about one build in five returned a shape that stayed invalid after repair (test 134; ThreadSanitizer on OCCT's `TKFillet` and `TKBool` reports the race). `fillet` and `chamfer` therefore build under one mutex in `cpp/blends.cpp`, from creating the OCCT builder to reading its history; the request checks before it and the validity check and repair after it run in parallel. Blends on several threads take turns, which costs nothing in a single-threaded run (section 5) and nothing in recompute, whose execution is sequential in M1; the WebAssembly build has one thread. Open issue 13 records the alternative.
6. The mutexes of items 4 and 5 and the one-time initialization are the only shared mutable state in the shim. It belongs to OCCT, not Partsmith: it guards third-party process-wide state and is invisible to callers, so the "no global mutable state in the core" invariant holds. Every way out of a guarded region, an exception's included (section 3.9 item 9), releases its mutex, and a one-time initialization that throws runs again on the next call (`std::call_once`), so a failed operation never fails or blocks a later one on any thread (test 136).
7. The shim's one-time initialization also removes the default message printers (`Message::DefaultMessenger()->RemovePrinters`) so OCCT writes nothing to standard output.
8. Since M2 each polling call makes its own progress indicator (section 3.13 item 3) and shares it with no other call. An `Interrupt` is read with atomic loads from any thread. Neither adds shared mutable state to the shim.
9. Since wave 2 of M2, the shim never calls `BRepLib::Plane()` (it creates a process-wide `Geom_Plane` on its first call and returns it to every later one) and never `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge2d` (it builds on that plane, section 2.5 item 5). `extrude_to` runs `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter` and `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle` with no lock: on 2026-11-19 a search of OCCT 7.9.3's `BOPAlgo_Builder`, `BOPAlgo_PaveFiller`, `BOPAlgo_Splitter`, `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter`, `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle` and `Draft` sources found no static that keeps state between calls outside debug builds (`Draft_Modification_1.cxx` holds one, inside `OCCT_DEBUG`), and no run of them on several threads has been measured. Test 223 runs them on 8 threads. If it ever finds a difference, they build under one mutex as blends do (item 5) and an open issue records it.
10. Since wave 2 of M2, `modeled_thread` runs `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections` and `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle` with no lock. On 2026-11-23 a search of OCCT 7.9.3's `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections`, `BRepFill_Generator`, `ShapeUpgrade` (`ShapeDivide`, `ShapeDivideAngle`, `FaceDivide`, `SplitSurface`, `SplitSurfaceAngle`, `WireDivide`, `EdgeDivide`, `SplitCurve2d` and `SplitCurve3d`), `ShapeBuild_ReShape` and `ShapeAnalysis_Edge` sources found no static that keeps state between calls. `BRepFill_Generator::GeneratedShapes` returns a function-static empty list by `const` reference, and nothing writes to it. `BRepLib::Precision()`, read by `BRepLib_MakeEdge`, is a process-wide value that only `BRepLib::Precision(P)` sets, and the shim never calls it. The edges of section 3.4.14 item 3 are built on the shim's own surfaces, never on `BRepLib::Plane()` (item 9). Test 234 runs the thread on 8 threads, with item 9's rule if it finds a difference.

### 3.11 Determinism

On one platform build, every facade function is a deterministic function of its arguments: equal inputs (equal BRep bytes and equal values) give equal outputs (equal BRep bytes, equal histories, equal roles, equal meshes, equal STEP bytes), whatever thread calls, whatever ran before and whatever process runs it. OCCT keeps intermediate results in maps keyed by shape addresses, which differ between runs; where the order of such a map reaches a result, the shim puts the result in an order that depends only on the input (shell, section 3.4.8 item 5: its faces, shells and solids, and each edge's curve representations), and it names every choice OCCT would otherwise read from the environment (the mesh algorithm, section 3.7 item 2). Since no operation writes to its inputs (section 3.1 item 3), a result does not depend on what ran before on the same shapes either. Tests 104 to 107 run every operation repeatedly in one process, with other allocations in between so that OCCT meets other addresses, and in separate processes, and compare BRep bytes, volumes, centers of mass, topology, histories, roles, meshes and STEP bytes; tests 90, 91 and 134 run operations on several threads at once; test 114 checks that a failed boolean leaves its inputs as they were. Across platforms (native x86_64 and aarch64, `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`) shapes are expected to give identical counts and indices and measurements within 1e-9 relative. The triangulation's own arithmetic is the same everywhere (section 3.7 item 2), so a mesh is identical wherever its nodes are bit for bit identical and the faces Watson meshes take the same decisions; the nodes come from OCCT's curve and surface evaluation, which the platform's libm and a compiler that fuses floating-point operations (Apple Clang on arm64, which builds OCCT with its defaults) can move in the last bits, and where nodes are cocircular such a change can flip a diagonal. So meshes can differ across platforms, and the print results computed from meshes agree within the tolerance of delivery.md 2.8 (open issue 14). Differences are corpus regressions, not acceptance failures here.

Since M2, an interrupt that is never requested changes no result (section 3.13 item 9).

### 3.12 `build.rs` contract

1. **OCCT location.** The install root is `OCCT_ROOT_<TARGET>` if set, where `<TARGET>` is the Cargo target triple uppercased with `-` replaced by `_` (for example `OCCT_ROOT_WASM32_UNKNOWN_EMSCRIPTEN`), else `OCCT_ROOT`. If neither is set, the build fails with the message `OCCT_ROOT must point at an OCCT install built with scripts/build-occt.sh`. Both variables are declared with `cargo:rerun-if-env-changed`.
2. **Headers and libraries.** Includes from `<root>/include/opencascade`; libraries from `<root>/lib` (`cargo:rustc-link-search=native`).
3. **Static linking in dependency order.** The toolkits are linked as static libraries in this order, each before the toolkits it depends on (from OCCT 7.9.3 `EXTERNLIB` files): `TKDESTL`, `TKDESTEP`, `TKXCAF`, `TKVCAF`, `TKV3d`, `TKHLR`, `TKService`, `TKCAF`, `TKLCAF`, `TKCDF`, `TKXSBase`, `TKDE`, `TKMesh`, `TKFeat`, `TKOffset`, `TKFillet`, `TKBool`, `TKBO`, `TKShHealing`, `TKPrim`, `TKTopAlgo`, `TKGeomAlgo`, `TKBRep`, `TKGeomBase`, `TKG3d`, `TKG2d`, `TKMath`, `TKernel`. Adding an OCCT package to the shim requires checking that its toolkit is in this list (and in `scripts/build-occt.sh`).
4. **System libraries.** Linux: `pthread` and `dl`. Windows: `advapi32`, `gdi32`, `user32`, `wsock32`, `psapi`. macOS and Emscripten: none beyond the C++ standard library, which `cxx-build` links.
5. **C++ flags.** The bridge and every shim source compile as C++17 (`.std("c++17")`) with `OCCT_STATIC_BUILD` defined (`.define("OCCT_STATIC_BUILD", None)`), as OCCT compiles its static toolkits, so on Windows `Standard_Macro.hxx` (the only header that reads it) declares nothing `dllexport` or `dllimport`; elsewhere the define changes nothing. With an MSVC-like compiler (`get_compiler().is_like_msvc()`): `/EHsc` (section 3.9 item 9). The runtime library is the Rust target's: `cc` compiles with `/MT` when the target has the `crt-static` feature and with `/MD` when it has not. `.cargo/config.toml` gives `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc` that feature (delivery.md 3.6, ADR 0038). `build.rs` sets none of its own (no `static_crt`). The shim and the Rust code it links with always share one runtime. Doc tests get the same runtime: `.cargo/config.toml` gives that target `crt-static` in its `rustdocflags` too. Rustdoc reads them in place of the rustflags (delivery.md 3.6). Every Windows executable takes the Universal C Runtime from Windows: `.cargo/config.toml` also gives that target the link arguments `/NODEFAULTLIB:libucrt.lib` and `/DEFAULTLIB:ucrt.lib`, in both lists. They act at the final link and need nothing here (delivery.md 3.6 item 2, ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-05). With any other compiler: `-ffp-contract=off`, so no multiplication and addition of the shim are fused into one operation, which the arithmetic of section 3.7 item 2 relies on; MSVC's default `/fp:precise` on x64 fuses none. `cc` adds `CXXFLAGS` after these flags, so a build environment whose `CXXFLAGS` turn contraction back on is not supported. For targets containing `emscripten`, also `-fwasm-exceptions` (matching OCCT built by `scripts/build-occt.sh wasm` and the Rust target's native wasm exceptions, ADR 0009).
6. **Rebuild triggers.** `cargo:rerun-if-changed=src/ffi.rs` and `cargo:rerun-if-changed=cpp`.
7. **Crate manifest.** `links = "occt"`; dependencies `cxx`, `thiserror`, `partsmith-mesh`; build dependency `cxx-build`.
8. **Pinned version.** `cpp/shim.h` includes `Standard_Version.hxx` and `<string_view>` and has `static_assert(std::string_view(OCC_VERSION_COMPLETE) == "7.9.3", ...)`, so an install of another version fails to compile. The Rust constant `OCCT_VERSION` holds the same text.
9. **Test link argument (M2).** For targets containing `emscripten`, `build.rs` passes `cargo:rustc-link-arg-tests=--pre-js=<manifest dir>/tests/node/cancel_word.js` and `cargo:rustc-link-arg-benches=--pre-js=<manifest dir>/tests/node/cancel_word.js`, and declares `cargo:rerun-if-changed=tests/node/cancel_word.js`. That file sets `Module['partsmithCancelWord']` to a `BigInt64Array` of length 1 over a new `SharedArrayBuffer(8)` holding `7n`, for test 166 and for the web row of the polls' cost in section 5. It reaches this crate's test and benchmark binaries only, never the web core that `partsmith-wasm` links.

### 3.13 Interrupts (M2)

A host stops a running call through an `Interrupt` ([ADR 0063](../adr/0063-cancel-through-a-host-flag-outside-the-session.md), [ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)). engine.md section 3.26 makes one for each request it runs, and features.md section 2.6 passes it to every polling call a build makes.

1. **The flag.** An `Interrupt` holds an `Arc<AtomicU64>` word, the request id it waits for and, for `page`, a mark that it also reads the page's word. It is requested while its word holds its request id. `new()` makes a word of its own at 0 and waits for 1. `word(word, request)` shares the host's word. `page(request)` makes a word of its own at 0 and also reads the page's word (item 7). An interrupt whose request is 0 is never requested, and its `request()` does nothing. `request()` stores the request id in the word. Clones share the word. `is_requested` reads the word with one atomic load (`Ordering::Relaxed`: the word guards no other data). It is not latched: an interrupt is requested only while its word holds its request id. Neither method blocks or takes a lock.
2. **Who reads it.** During a call, only the shim's progress indicator (item 3) reads the interrupt. The Rust facade passes it on and never reads it. The argument checks of section 3.1 item 4 come first: a call with invalid arguments fails with `InvalidInput` whatever its interrupt holds. Since M2, `read_step`'s check of the bytes (section 3.8.1 read items 6 and 7) comes first too: bytes it refuses fail with `ReadFailed` whatever the interrupt holds.
3. **The indicator.** `partsmith::occt::Interruption` in `cpp/interrupt.cpp` derives from `Message_ProgressIndicator`. Its `UserBreak()` calls `Interrupt::is_requested` through the bridge's `extern "Rust"` block. Once it has answered true, it answers true for the rest of the call without asking again. `Show()` does nothing. The indicator's position is never read. Each polling call makes one indicator. Every OCCT algorithm of the call that takes a `Message_ProgressRange` gets a range of one root scope (`Message_ProgressScope`) over the indicator's `Start()`. OCCT's internal parallelism is off (section 3.10 item 3). `UserBreak` therefore runs on the calling thread.
4. **The shim's own polls.** The shim polls the indicator before the first OCCT algorithm of the call, with two exceptions. In `fillet` and `chamfer` the first poll comes inside the blend mutex of section 3.10 item 5, after the builder's contour checks and before it builds the blend. Its message names `BRepFilletAPI_MakeFillet` or `BRepFilletAPI_MakeChamfer`. In `write_step` and `read_step` the first poll comes right after the call takes the data exchange mutex of section 3.10 item 4. Its message names `STEPCAFControl_Writer` or `STEPCAFControl_Reader`. A call requested before it starts then stops inside its guarded region, as the failures of test 136 do. The shim polls again after each OCCT algorithm, validity check, repair, copy and history merge, before the next one starts. No poll follows the last step: a call whose work is done returns its result.
5. **A call that stops.** Once the indicator has answered true, the call fails with `KernelError { kind: Canceled, exception: "Partsmith_Canceled", .. }` and returns no shape. That holds however its OCCT algorithm ended: done, not done, with an alert (`BOPAlgo_AlertUserBreak`), with a status (`BRepOffset_UserBreak`) or with an exception. OCCT does not stop every algorithm cleanly: on 2026-11-07, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid` stopped at its sixth poll threw `Standard_NoSuchObject: NCollection_DataMap::Find`. The raw message names the step that saw the request. It reads `<OCCT class> stopped by its interrupt` when OCCT's own poll answered true, and `stopped by its interrupt before <OCCT class or shim step>` when the shim's poll did. A failure that the call meets before the indicator first answers true keeps its own kind. The call polls no more after it.
6. **What a stop keeps.** No input changes: every algorithm that writes to its arguments runs on copies (section 3.1 item 3). Every lock the call held is released on the way out (sections 3.9 item 9 and 3.10 item 6). The next call, on any thread and with the same inputs, gives the result it gives when nothing stopped before it.
7. **The web core.** On `wasm32-unknown-emscripten`, `cpp/interrupt.cpp` defines the JavaScript import with `EM_JS(double, partsmith_cancel_word, (), { ... })`. Its body reads `var word = Module['partsmithCancelWord'];` and returns `Number(Atomics.load(word, 0))` when `word` is a `BigInt64Array` of length at least 1, else 0. It never throws. The worker gives it a `BigInt64Array` of length 1 over a `SharedArrayBuffer` (protocol.md section 2.10). The C++ function `double page_cancel_word()` returns the import's value. The bridge declares `fn page_cancel_word() -> f64;` in its existing `unsafe extern "C++"` block, with `#[cfg(target_os = "emscripten")]` and without `Result` (section 3.9 item 2). For `Interrupt::page(request)`, `is_requested` is true when its own word holds `request`, or when `request` is at most 2^53 - 1 and `page_cancel_word()` returns `request as f64`. A larger id is never requested through the page's word, because a double does not hold it exactly. Native builds declare and define neither the import nor `page_cancel_word`. No `unsafe` item is added (section 3.9 item 8). The module's exports stay `_main` and ADR 0010's three, and `partsmith-wasm` changes nothing (ADR 0010's note of 2026-11-07). The core still has no shared memory: only the page's 8-byte word is shared, and only JavaScript reads it.
8. **`wait_for_interrupt(interrupt, limit)`.** Errors use operation `WaitForInterrupt`. The shim makes an indicator over `interrupt` (item 3) and loops over one `Message_ProgressScope`. Each turn calls `UserBreak()` through the scope and fails with `Canceled` (message `wait_for_interrupt stopped by its interrupt`) when it answers true. The loop returns `Ok(())` once `limit` has passed on the steady clock, and otherwise waits about 1 ms. An interrupt requested before the call fails it at the first poll. `limit` crosses the bridge as milliseconds in an `f64`.
9. **Determinism.** The indicator changes no option of any algorithm and reads nothing but its interrupt. A call whose interrupt is never requested, of any form, returns the result the same call returns with `Interrupt::new()`. Section 3.11 holds for it unchanged.
10. **Cancel latency.** From `request()` to the return of a call that stops takes at most the longest step the call does not poll inside, plus one poll. Section 5 sets its budgets.

**The calls that poll.** Measured on 2026-11-07 on the 4-core development container, with OCCT 7.9.3's native release libraries and an indicator that counted `UserBreak` calls in a probe program outside the shim, the interrupt never requested:

| Facade call | OCCT algorithms that get a range | Polls inside OCCT 7.9.3 |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `extrude`, `revolve` | none: `BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism` and `BRepPrimAPI_MakeRevol` build in their constructors, which take no range | none: the prism of `RECT` polled 0 times |
| `extrude_to` (M2) | `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle::Build` for a taper, `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter` up to a face or a shape; `BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism` takes none | the splitter yes: 400 to 663 polls up to a face (spike 8.5). `DraftAngle::Build` takes a range and never polls: its longest gap on the 100-edge profile was 34.7 to 36.8 ms, at most 54.4 ms (open issue 17). The start check of section 3.4.3 item 3 and the reach checks of item 5 take no range; the shim polls before and after them |
| `fuse`, `cut`, `common` | `BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse`, `BRepAlgoAPI_Cut`, `BRepAlgoAPI_Common` | yes: 650 polls for `fuse(&A, &[B])`; 29,290 for the cut of `PLATE`'s 194 cylinders, whose longest gap was 20.5 ms of 440 ms |
| `split` | `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter` | yes: 408 polls for test 59 |
| `fillet`, `chamfer` | `BRepFilletAPI_MakeFillet::Build` and `BRepFilletAPI_MakeChamfer::Build` take a range, but `ChFi3d_Builder` declares none | none: 0 polls for the 12 edges of `BOX` at radius 1 |
| `shell` | `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid`, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffsetShape`, `BRepOffset_MakeOffset`, `BRepAlgoAPI_Defeaturing` and the cuts of section 3.4.8 | yes: 180 polls for `shell(&BOX, &[Top], 1)`; the prisms of section 3.4.8 item 7 none |
| `tessellate` | `BRepMesh_IncrementalMesh::Perform` with the shim's context (section 3.7 item 2) | yes, but not in every phase: with OCCT's default mesher, the one face of a sphere of radius 250 at 0.0125 mm and 5 degrees polled 100,473 times, and its longest gap, 583 ms, fell inside that face. With the shim's context, whose base algorithm runs its own Delabella, the call polled 9 times, the shim's own two polls among them. Its polls and longest gap are recorded in open issue 17 |
| `modeled_thread` (M2) | `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections::Build` and the join's `BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse` or `BRepAlgoAPI_Cut`; `BRepLib::BuildCurve3d` and `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle` take none | the join yes: section 8.2's cap with its boolean polled 2,931 times, with a longest gap of 4.443 ms. `ThruSections::Build` never polls (open issue 17). The volumes of section 3.4.14 item 8 take no range; the shim polls before and after them |
| `write_step` | `STEPCAFControl_Writer::Transfer`; `WriteStream` takes none | yes: 16 polls for `BOX` |
| `read_step` | `STEPCAFControl_Reader::Transfer` and each solid's `ShapeFix_Shape::Perform`; `ReadStream`, the parse, takes none | yes: 60 polls for `BOX` |
| the repair of section 3.5, in any of them | `ShapeFix_Shape::Perform` | yes: 43 polls for `PLATE` |

The validity check (`BRepCheck_Analyzer` and the checks of section 3.5) and the unification of section 3.4.5 (`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain`) take no range in OCCT 7.9.3. The shim polls before and after them. `wait_for_interrupt` polls in its own loop (item 8). Every other facade call takes no interrupt and never stops: the primitives, `make_profile`, `wrap_curves` (since wave 2), `transform`, `section`, `compound`, every measurement and query of `Shape`, `is_valid`, `element_display`, `to_brep` and `from_brep`. They are short, or they run where a stop is not wanted: the ghosts of engine.md section 3.23 and the result cache. The longest of them is a mirror or a scale of a large body, which copies it and checks the copy whole (the check takes 128 ms on `PLATE`, section 3.5). A request during it waits for it. The next polling call of the build then stops at its first poll ([ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md) alternative 10). A step that OCCT does not poll inside runs to its end once it starts. Open issue 17 lists those steps with their lengths.

## 4. Errors

### 4.1 Kind mapping

| Source | `exception` | `kind` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Rust argument check | empty | `InvalidInput` |
| Rust check of STEP bytes (since M2, section 3.8.1 read items 6 and 7) | `Partsmith_ReadError` | `ReadFailed` |
| `Partsmith_InvalidInput` | `Partsmith_InvalidInput` | `InvalidInput` |
| `Partsmith_InvalidResult` | `Partsmith_InvalidResult` | `InvalidResult` |
| `Partsmith_InvalidInputShape` | `Partsmith_InvalidInputShape` | `InvalidInputShape` |
| `Partsmith_EmptyResult` | `Partsmith_EmptyResult` | `EmptyResult` |
| `Partsmith_ReadError` | `Partsmith_ReadError` | `ReadFailed` |
| `Partsmith_Canceled` (since M2, section 3.13) | `Partsmith_Canceled` | `Canceled` |
| `Partsmith_TargetNotReached` (since wave 2 of M2, section 3.4.3 item 5) | `Partsmith_TargetNotReached` | `TargetNotReached` |
| `Partsmith_TargetPartlyReached` (since wave 2 of M2, section 3.4.3 item 5) | `Partsmith_TargetPartlyReached` | `TargetPartlyReached` |
| `Partsmith_TargetStartsInside` (since wave 2 of M2, section 3.4.3 item 3) | `Partsmith_TargetStartsInside` | `TargetStartsInside` |
| any other `Standard_Failure` type (including `StdFail_NotDone`, `Standard_ConstructionError`, `Standard_DomainError`, `Standard_NullObject`, `Standard_OutOfRange`) | its type name | `Failed` |
| `std::exception` (including `std::bad_alloc`) | `std::exception` | `Internal` |
| anything else | `unknown` | `Internal` |

The raw text from the shim is split at the first `": "` into `exception` and `message`; a text without `": "` becomes `exception = "unknown"` with the whole text as `message`.

### 4.2 `InvalidInput` messages

Numbers in messages use the fingerprint number format of `docs/spec/naming.md` section 3.7.3 (at most 6 decimals, trailing zeros removed). `<index>` and `<tag>` are decimal.

These are the facade's own sentences for its callers and tests. Several name kernel indices and curve tags, which never belong in a message a user reads (docs/guidelines/errors.md), so features never show them: they show `hint()` and put the sentence in the detail (section 4.3). Hosts show `user_message()`, and with it these sentences, only for the checks of the operations they call directly (export and mesh: `There is nothing to export.`, `Body "<name>" has no solid to export.`, `Color components must be between 0 and 1.` and the mesh tolerance message), which name none.

| Check | Message |
| --- | --- |
| Non-finite argument | `A value is not a finite number.` |
| Invalid plane | `The plane needs a nonzero normal and a nonzero x axis perpendicular to it.` |
| Invalid axis | `The axis needs a nonzero direction.` |
| Box dimensions | `A box needs a length, width and height greater than 0 mm.` |
| Cylinder dimensions | `A cylinder needs a radius and height greater than 0 mm.` |
| Sphere radius | `A sphere needs a radius greater than 0 mm.` |
| Cone dimensions | `A cone needs a height greater than 0 mm and two different radii of 0 mm or more.` |
| Torus radii | `A torus needs a minor radius greater than 0 mm and a major radius greater than the minor radius.` |
| No regions | `The profile has no regions.` |
| Empty loop | `A profile loop has no curves.` |
| Circle not alone | `Circle <tag> must be the only curve in its loop.` |
| Zero-length curve | `Curve <tag> of the profile has zero length.` |
| Arc ends off its circle | `Arc <tag> does not have its start and end on its circle.` |
| Closed curve not alone (M2, section 3.4.2 item 7.4) | `Closed curve <tag> must be the only curve in its loop.` |
| Ellipse or hyperbola radius below `LINEAR` (M2) | `Curve <tag> of the profile needs radii greater than 0 mm.` |
| Parabola focus at its vertex (M2) | `Curve <tag> of the profile needs its focus away from its vertex.` |
| Conic arc ends off its curve (M2, section 3.4.2 item 7.6) | `Curve <tag> does not have its start and end on its curve.` |
| Spline degree (M2) | `Spline <tag> needs a degree from 1 to 25.` |
| Spline knots (M2) | `Spline <tag> has knots out of order, too close together or with the wrong multiplicities.` |
| Spline poles (M2) | `Spline <tag> needs <count> poles for its knots and degree.` |
| Spline weights (M2) | `Spline <tag> needs one weight greater than 0 per pole.` |
| Spline range (M2) | `Spline <tag> has a range outside its knots.` |
| Gap | `The profile is not closed: curves <tag a> and <tag b> do not meet (gap <gap> mm).` |
| Invalid region | `The profile region crosses itself or has a hole outside its outer boundary.` |
| Zero extrude direction | `The extrude direction needs to be nonzero.` |
| Direction in plane | `The extrude direction lies in the sketch plane, so the profile cannot be extruded.` |
| End not beyond start | `The extrude end must be beyond its start.` |
| Target surface not planar or cylindrical (M2, section 3.4.3 item 1.3) | `The extrude can end only on a planar or cylindrical face, not on <surface>.` with `<surface>` one of `a cone`, `a sphere`, `a torus`, `a surface of revolution`, `a surface of extrusion`, `a B-spline surface`, `a Bezier surface`, `an offset surface` and `a surface of another kind` for the kinds `Cone`, `Sphere`, `Torus`, `Revolution`, `Extrusion`, `BsplineSurface`, `BezierSurface`, `OffsetSurface` and `OtherSurface` |
| No target face (M2, section 3.4.3 item 1.3) | `An extrude up to a face needs at least one face.` |
| Target faces on several surfaces (M2, section 3.4.3 item 1.3) | `The faces an extrude ends on must lie on one plane or one cylinder.` |
| Target shape without a solid (M2) | `An extrude up to a shape needs a shape with a solid.` |
| Up-to off the normal (M2, section 3.4.3 item 1.5) | `An extrude up to a face or a shape runs along the normal of its sketch plane.` |
| Taper of 90 degrees or more (M2) | `The extrude taper must be less than 90 degrees either way.` |
| Taper off the normal (M2) | `A tapered extrude runs along the normal of its sketch plane.` |
| Taper on another end (M2) | `A tapered extrude ends at an offset or on a planar face.` |
| Up-to or taper on several regions (M2) | `An extrude up to a face or a shape, or with a taper, needs a profile of one region.` |
| Up-to or taper on conics or splines (M2) | `An extrude up to a face or a shape, or with a taper, needs a profile of lines, arcs and circles.` |
| Target never met (M2, kind `TargetNotReached`) | `The extrusion meets no point of the face or shape it ends on.` |
| Target met in part (M2, kind `TargetPartlyReached`) | `Only part of the profile meets the face or shape the extrusion ends on.` |
| Profile inside the target shape (M2, kind `TargetStartsInside`) | `The profile starts inside the shape the extrusion ends on.` |
| Revolve angle | `The revolve angle must be greater than 0 and at most 360 degrees.` |
| Axis not in plane | `The revolve axis must lie in the sketch plane.` |
| Profile crosses axis | `The profile crosses the revolve axis. Keep the profile on one side of the axis.` |
| Wrap surface radius (M2, section 3.4.14 item 1) | `A wrap surface needs a radius greater than 0 mm.` |
| Cone half-angle (M2) | `A cone needs a half-angle other than 0 and less than 90 degrees either way.` |
| No wrap curve (M2) | `A wrap needs at least one curve.` |
| Segment on a cone (M2) | `A segment of the developed plane maps exactly only onto a cylinder.` |
| Segment length (M2) | `A segment needs a length greater than 0 mm.` |
| Helix pitch (M2) | `A helix needs a pitch greater than 0 mm.` |
| Helix turns (M2) | `A helix needs more than 0 turns.` |
| Helix across a cone's apex (M2) | `A helix on a cone must stay on one side of the cone's apex.` |
| Thread depth (M2) | `A thread needs a depth greater than 0 mm.` |
| Thread outer width (M2) | `A thread needs an outer side wider than 0 mm.` |
| Thread widths (M2) | `A thread needs an outer side narrower than its inner side and an inner side narrower than its pitch.` |
| Thread base without a solid (M2) | `A thread needs a base with a solid.` |
| No tools | `A boolean operation needs at least one tool body.` |
| Boolean on non-solid | `Boolean operations work on solid bodies only.` |
| No fillet edges | `Select at least one edge to fillet.` |
| Fillet radius | `The fillet radius must be greater than 0 mm.` |
| No chamfer edges | `Select at least one edge to chamfer.` |
| Chamfer distance | `The chamfer distances must be greater than 0 mm.` |
| Chamfer face not adjacent | `Face <face> is not next to edge <edge>, so it cannot set the chamfer side.` |
| Edge out of range | `Edge <index> does not exist in this body.` |
| Edge repeated | `Edge <index> is selected twice.` |
| Seam or degenerated edge | `Edge <index> is a seam or a degenerated edge and cannot be rounded or beveled.` |
| Tangent edges with different sizes | `Edges <a> and <b> are tangent to each other, so they must use the same size.` |
| Fillet or chamfer on non-solid | `Fillets and chamfers work on solid bodies only.` |
| Shell thickness | `The shell thickness must be greater than 0 mm.` |
| Shell solid count | `Shell works on a body made of exactly one solid.` |
| Face out of range | `Face <index> does not exist in this body.` |
| Face repeated | `Face <index> is selected twice.` |
| All faces removed | `A shell must keep at least one face.` |
| Not a similarity | `The transform must be a combination of moves, rotations, mirrors and uniform scaling.` |
| Split on non-solid | `Split works on solid bodies only.` |
| Center of mass without volume | `This shape has no volume, so it has no center of mass.` |
| Bounding box of empty shape | `This shape is empty, so it has no bounding box.` |
| Element index out of range | `<Kind> <index> does not exist in this body.` (`<Kind>` is `Face`, `Edge` or `Vertex`) |
| Element query on solids | `Only faces, edges and vertices can be queried.` |
| Edge normals of a degenerated edge | `Edge <index> is a degenerated edge, so it has no middle point.` |
| Edge without a tangent at its middle | `Edge <index> has no direction at its middle.` |
| Face normal undefined at an edge's middle | `Face <face> has no defined normal at the middle of edge <index>.` |
| Width across or display of a degenerated edge | `Edge <index> is a degenerated edge, so it has no curve to measure or draw.` |
| Mesh tolerances | `Mesh tolerances must be greater than 0, with an angle of at most 90 degrees.` |
| Nothing to export | `There is nothing to export.` |
| Body without solid | `Body "<name>" has no solid to export.` |
| Color out of range | `Color components must be between 0 and 1.` |

### 4.3 User-facing messages

A `KernelError` gives three texts ([ADR 0026](../adr/0026-kernel-facade-additions-for-errors.md)):

- `hint()`: one sentence that follows a feature's own sentence. Features report a kernel error as `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build {this}. {hint}` (features.md section 4.1, code `kernel`), so their message stays at two sentences. Each hint is true whenever the kernel fails in that operation, and never blames input that was checked before the kernel: features check sizes, ranges and preconditions first (features.md sections 3.4 and 3.10), so a hint never stands in for a range check.
- `user_message()`: the whole message, for hosts that show a kernel error without a sentence of their own (STEP export and import and the meshes of an export, io.md section 4; the result cache). `Display` writes it.
- `detail()`: the technical text for reports, never shown as the message: the operation as `Operation` displays it, a space, the kind in snake_case (`invalid_input`, `failed`, `invalid_result`, `invalid_input_shape`, `empty_result`, `read_failed`, `internal`, since M2 `canceled`, and since wave 2 of M2 `target_not_reached`, `target_partly_reached` and `target_starts_inside`), then `: <exception>` when `exception` is not empty and `: <message>` when `message` is not empty. Examples: `fillet failed: StdFail_NotDone: BRep_API: command not done`, `make_box invalid_input: A box needs a length, width and height greater than 0 mm.` Features put it in their message's detail (features.md section 2.8), which hosts offer behind Copy details.

By kind:

| Kind | `hint()` | `user_message()` |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `InvalidInput` | `It rejected the shapes or values it was given, which is likely a bug; report it with the details.` | `message` unchanged (section 4.2) |
| `Failed`, `InvalidResult`, `EmptyResult` | the operation's hint in the tables below | `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the {noun}. {hint}` for an operation with a noun in the first table; the hint for the others |
| `InvalidInputShape` | `The shape it was given was already not valid, so repair the STEP file that shape came from, or export the file again.` | `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the {noun}. {hint}` for an operation with a noun in the first table; the hint for the others |
| `ReadFailed` | `read_step`: `The file is not a valid STEP file.`; `read_brep`: `The cached geometry could not be read, so it will be rebuilt.`; any other operation as `Failed` | the hint for `read_step` and `read_brep`; any other operation as `Failed` |
| `Internal` | `It hit an unexpected problem; report it with the details.` | `Partsmith's geometry kernel hit an unexpected problem.` |
| `Canceled` (since M2) | `It was stopped before it finished.` | `Partsmith stopped this step before it finished.` |
| `TargetNotReached` (since wave 2 of M2) | `The extrusion never meets the face or body it should end on.` | `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the {noun}. {hint}` |
| `TargetPartlyReached` (since wave 2 of M2) | `Only part of the profile meets the face or body it should end on.` | `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the {noun}. {hint}` |
| `TargetStartsInside` (since wave 2 of M2) | `The profile starts inside the body it should end on.` | `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the {noun}. {hint}` |

The `InvalidInput` hint does not repeat the facade's sentence: features check what they pass before calling the kernel, so a rejection that reaches a feature means Partsmith's own checks disagree, and the sentence (which may name kernel indices and tags) goes to the detail.

The three target kinds name what the newcomer did. Features word them in their own terms (features/extrude.md, the newcomer error NC14) and never show these texts. The hint names no face index and no tag (the element words of features.md section 3.12 name the face for the user). The texts serve a host that shows a kernel error with no sentence of its own. Only these texts and the hints keep face indices out. The `InvalidInput` sentences of `extrude_to`'s face list (`Face <index> does not exist in this body.` and `Face <index> is selected twice.`, section 3.4.3 item 1.3) name indices, as `fillet`'s and `shell`'s do: features check the list first, and such a sentence reaches only the detail, as the `InvalidInput` paragraph above says.

A stop is not a failure. partsmith-features reports a kernel error of kind `Canceled` as `FeatureError::Canceled` and never shows its texts (features.md sections 2.8 and 4.1). engine.md section 3.26 words a stopped export or import itself. The `Canceled` texts serve a host that shows a kernel error with no sentence of its own. They are the same for every operation.

The `InvalidInputShape` hint is the same for every operation, although only a mirror or scale `transform` raises the kind (section 3.5). The shim raises it only when the shape the transform was given was not valid before it ran. In Partsmith's own use such a shape comes from a STEP file. `read_step` keeps a solid that repair could not fix and says so (section 3.8.1 read item 3). Checked operations return only valid results or results that keep such a solid's invalid parts (test 111). The cache gives back what the kernel made. The hint names the file as the way forward and words the failure as the file's, never as a fault of Partsmith (docs/guidelines/errors.md, Tone). Before section 3.5 told it apart, a mirror of such a solid failed with `InvalidResult` and the `transform` hint `Report it with the details.` The mirror feature words this failure itself, with the body and the import that put the solid in it (features.md section 3.5).

Operations that build geometry for features, with the noun of their user message:

| Operation | Hint | Noun |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `make_box`, `make_cylinder`, `make_sphere`, `make_cone`, `make_torus` | `Its sizes passed every check, so this is likely a bug; report it with the details.` | `box`, `cylinder`, `sphere`, `cone`, `torus` |
| `make_profile` | `The closed shape could not be turned into a face; report it with the details if the sketch shows no problem.` | `profile` |
| `extrude`, `extrude_to` (M2), `revolve` | `Report it with the details if the profile and the sizes look right.` | `extrusion`, `extrusion`, `revolution` |
| `fuse`, `cut`, `common` | `Bodies that touch only along an edge or at a point, or faces that almost coincide, often cause this; move one of them slightly.` | `join`, `cut`, `intersection` |
| `fillet`, `chamfer`, `shell` | `A smaller radius may work.`, `A smaller distance may work.` and `A thinner wall may work.`, for callers that do not search; built-in features run the "too large" search instead (features.md section 4.3) | `fillet`, `chamfer`, `shell` |
| `transform` (move body, mirror, patterns) | `Report it with the details.` | `moved or copied body` |
| `split` | `Try moving the split plane slightly.` | `split` |
| `wrap_curves` (M2) | `Report it with the details if the surface and the curve sizes look right.` | `curve` |
| `modeled_thread` (M2, [ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md) decision 4) | `A cosmetic thread keeps the face as it is and builds instead.` | `thread` |

The `modeled_thread` hint offers the cosmetic thread, as ADR 0074 decision 4 asks. It is true whenever the modeled thread fails, the check of section 3.4.14 item 8 included: a cosmetic thread changes no geometry (features/thread.md) and builds wherever its face exists.

The other operations say what could not be done in the user's terms, as hint and as user message. The texts the message inventory found at fault are rewritten (`compound`, `measure` and `query`, which gave no way forward, and `query` named a selection where there may be none); the others are unchanged:

| Operation | Hint and user message |
| --- | --- |
| `section` | `The cross-section could not be computed.` |
| `compound` | `The solids could not be grouped into one body; report it with the details.` |
| `measure` | `The body could not be measured; report it with the details.` |
| `query` | `The faces and edges of the body could not be read; report it with the details.` |
| `tessellate` | `The body could not be meshed for display or export.` |
| `write_step` | `The STEP file could not be written.` |
| `read_step` | `The STEP file could not be read.`; for `EmptyResult`, `The STEP file contains no solid bodies.` |
| `write_brep` | `The geometry could not be saved to the cache.` |
| `read_brep` | `The cached geometry could not be read, so it will be rebuilt.` |
| `wait_for_interrupt` (since M2) | `The test wait could not run; report it with the details.` |

These replace the operation sentences of the first M1 facade, which blamed input that features check first (`The extrusion failed. Check that the profile is a closed region that does not cross itself.` for a distance of 1e200 mm, which the modeling limit now rejects with its field) or named something nobody did (`Moving the body failed.` under Mirror and patterns). Every hint is one sentence. Every hint and every user message, for `InvalidInput` those of the checks hosts show (section 4.2), passes the message lint of docs/guidelines/errors.md and contains no exception name and no text from the kernel (test 130).

Features write `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build {this}.` followed by `hint()` and keep `detail()` for the report; fillet, chamfer and shell run the "too large" search instead (features.md section 4.3) and keep the first kernel error as its cause. Hosts that call the kernel without a feature show `user_message()` (io.md section 4).

## 5. Performance budgets

Release builds on the reference laptop (technical design section 14), single thread, measured by benchmarks in `crates/partsmith-occt-sys/benches/` (not by acceptance tests). Web budgets are three times these. "Part" is the 80 x 50 x 30 extrude of naming spec test 81 with a cut hole, a slot, 4 fillets and a shell (about 40 faces) unless stated. `PLATE` is a 200 x 200 x 10 box (origin (0,0,-5) on `Plane::XY`) with 194 through holes of radius 4 cut in one `cut` call (tool cylinders of height 20 on `Plane::XY` at origins (10 + 13.5 c, 10 + 13.5 r, -10) for c = i mod 14, r = i div 14, i = 0 to 193): 200 faces, 594 edges, 396 vertices, the body of the `BodyState::elements` budget in features.md section 5. `L` is the L-shaped body of test 102. `PLATE1000` is `PLATE` grown to 1,000 faces: a 440 x 440 x 10 box (origin (0,0,-5) on `Plane::XY`) with 994 through holes of radius 4 cut in one `cut` call (tool cylinders of height 20 at (10 + 13.5 c, 10 + 13.5 r, -10) for c = i mod 32, r = i div 32, i = 0 to 993): 1,000 faces, 2,994 edges, 1,996 vertices. `STAIR` is the stepped block of test 119.

| Operation | Budget |
| --- | --- |
| Any primitive | 1 ms |
| `make_profile` of 100 curves | 5 ms |
| `extrude` of a 100-edge profile | 20 ms |
| `fuse` or `cut` of two primitives | 10 ms |
| `cut` of 20 cylinders from a box (one call) | 60 ms |
| `fillet` of the 12 edges of a box | 40 ms |
| `shell` of a box | 20 ms |
| `shell` of `L` with its step face removed (first attempt and fallback, section 3.4.8) | 40 ms |
| `shell` of `RC` with `RT` removed and a 3 mm wall, thicker than its rounds (test 131, section 3.4.8 item 6) | 40 ms |
| `shell` of `RC` with `RT` removed and a 2 mm wall, as thick as its rounds (test 131, section 3.4.8 item 6.2) | 200 ms |
| `shell` of the result of test 45 with its top removed and a 0.5 mm wall, an opening tangent to its rounds (test 133, section 3.4.8 item 7) | 60 ms |
| The `shell` calls of the "too large" search of features.md section 4.3 on `RC` with `RT` removed at 12 mm: the failing call and the 10 attempts (test 131, section 3.4.8 item 6.1) | 600 ms |
| `transform` (rigid) | 0.1 ms |
| `elements(Face)` plus `topology()` for a 200-face shape | 10 ms |
| `elements` of faces, edges and vertices plus `topology()` for `PLATE` | 8 ms |
| `width_across` of any face across any of its edges, on `STAIR` and on the result of test 44 (one call) | 5 ms |
| `element_display` of one hole wall of `PLATE1000` at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees | 5 ms |
| `volume` of the part | 2 ms |
| `tessellate` the part at 0.05 mm and 0.1 rad | 30 ms |
| `tessellate` a sphere of radius 250 at 0.0125 mm and 5 degrees (about 200 000 triangles) | 1.5 s |
| `tessellate` `PLATE` at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees | 1.5 s |
| `to_brep` plus `from_brep` of the part | 5 ms |
| `write_step` of the part | 100 ms |
| `read_step` of the part | 150 ms |
| `Interrupt::is_requested` of `new` or `word` (M2) | 50 ns |
| `Interrupt::is_requested` of `page`, in the WebAssembly build under Node, which calls the import (M2; its own budget, not three times a native one) | 1 µs |
| The polls' cost on the web: `cut` of `PLATE`'s box and its 194 cylinders in one call, and `tessellate` of the sphere of radius 250 at 0.0125 mm and 5 degrees, each in the WebAssembly build under Node with `Interrupt::page(1)`, which the word of section 3.12 item 9 never requests, against the same call with `Interrupt::new()` (M2; its own budget, not three times a native one) | at most 10 percent longer |
| Cancel latency of `shell` of `RC` with `RT` removed and a 2 mm wall (test 131, M2) | 50 ms |
| Cancel latency of `cut` of `PLATE`'s box and its 194 cylinders in one call, of `split` of `PLATE` by the plane z = 0, of `write_step` of `PLATE`, of `read_step` of the bytes it writes, of `fillet` of `PLATE`'s 4 outer vertical edges at radius 5, of `tessellate` of `PLATE` at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees, and of `tessellate` of the sphere of radius 250 at 0.0125 mm and 5 degrees (M2) | none: measured and recorded in open issue 17 |
| `make_profile` on `Plane::XY` of one loop of a cubic `Bspline` with 20 poles and a line (M2): `Bspline { degree: 3, poles, weights: None, knots: [0, 1, ..., 17], multiplicities: [4, 1, ..., 1, 4], periodic: false, range: None }` tagged 1, with pole `i` at `(50 - 50 cos(pi i / 19), 50 sin(pi i / 19))` for `i` from 0 to 19 and 16 multiplicities of 1 between the two of 4, and `Line` tagged 2 from (100,0) to (0,0) | set in wave 4 from its measured median plus 20 percent ([ADR 0057](../adr/0057-budgets-set-from-the-measured-median-plus-20-percent.md)) |
| `volume` of the extrusion from 0 to 10 of test 201's face, a piece of a periodic spline across its start (M2, section 3.6 item 1) | set in wave 4 as above; Gauss-Kronrod took 18.2 ms in a probe on 2026-11-23, where `BRepGProp_Vinert` at its default took 0.26 ms |
| `extrude_to` of `RECT` up to the bottom face of `BLOCK` (test 212, M2) | set in wave 4 as above; spike 8.5 measured the split in 4.89 to 7.28 ms against its 30 ms criterion |
| `extrude_to` of the 100-edge profile over 10 mm with a taper of 5 degrees, outward and inward (M2) | set in wave 4 as above; spike 8.5 measured `DraftAngle` in 50.65 to 57.41 ms against its 60 ms criterion, with 4 of 20 single-set medians above it |
| Cancel latency of the tapered `extrude_to` of the 100-edge profile (M2) | none: measured and recorded in open issue 17 |
| `modeled_thread` of M8 x 1.25 x 20 on `ROD8` and in `BLOCK8`, each with its boolean (tests 231 and 232, M2) | set in wave 4 from the median of three sets plus 20 percent ([ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md) decision 3); spike 8.2 measured 416.5 ms and 418.0 ms against its 300 ms criterion |
| `modeled_thread` of the cap: the internal M30 x 2 thread of section 8.2, 4.75 turns in a cylinder of radius 18 mm and height 14 mm with a bore 12 mm deep (M2) | set in wave 4 as above; spike 8.2 measured 160.0 ms |
| Cancel latency of the cap's `modeled_thread` (M2) | set in wave 4 as above; spike 8.2 measured its longest gap between polls at 4.443 ms, and gate 11 cancels the cap's preview |

**Cancel latency** (M2, section 3.13 item 10). The call runs on one thread. Another thread calls `request()` after a delay. The latency is the time from `request()` to the call's return. A row's value is the largest latency over 10 delays, k d / 11 for k = 1 to 10, with d the median duration of the uncanceled call. A call that returns `Ok`, because the request came after its last poll, counts the time from `request()` to its return. The row reads the median of 5 such runs, release. These rows have no web column: the WebAssembly build has one thread. engine.md section 5 sets the web's cancel budget, which ui.md's P-13 measures from the page. Every other row of this section holds with an interrupt that is never requested.

Tessellation with the shim's copy of Delabella (section 3.7 item 2, ADR 0039), medians of 3 runs alternating with OCCT's copy on one 4-core container (release, 2026-10-27): the sphere 0.97 s (OCCT's copy 0.89 s) and `PLATE` 0.85 s (0.79 s) natively, within 1.5 s; under Node 2.44 s (3.52 s) and 1.17 s (2.16 s), within the web's 4.5 s; the part 15 to 16 ms either way, and `element_display` of a hole wall 1.8 to 2.1 ms either way, since cylinders keep Watson.

Interrupts (M2), measured on 2026-11-07 on the same 4-core container (release): `Interrupt::is_requested` of `word` under 1 ns natively, and of `page` 87 to 101 ns under Node, medians of 7 runs. On the web the polls' cost stayed within the noise of the runs: with the two forms alternating, medians of 7 runs each, the `cut` of `PLATE`'s box and its 194 cylinders took 615 ms with `Interrupt::page(1)` and 681 ms with `Interrupt::new()`, and the tessellation of the sphere took 2.35 s and 2.46 s. The cancel latency of the `shell` of `RC` with `RT` removed and a 2 mm wall, measured as defined above (median of 5 runs), was 2.0 ms. Open issue 17 records the other cancel latencies.

**Core size** (M2, [ADR 0076](../adr/0076-occt-built-for-the-web-at-oz.md) decision 6). Every change to this crate's shim, its bridge or its toolkit list records the size in bytes of `ui/public/core/partsmith_core.wasm` built by `scripts/build-wasm.sh` with the change and at its base commit, both against the same OCCT install (delivery.md open issue 23), in its pull request. The stage's delivery.md owner adds the two sizes to delivery.md section 3.5. The next size ADR is due when a build of `main`, or a change's recorded size, is above 24,117,248 bytes (within 2 MiB of Cloudflare Pages' 25 MiB limit). On 2026-11-19 `main`'s core was 15,720,762 bytes. Spikes 8.8 to 8.10 measure their deltas the same way (section 8.1 item 4).

The `PLATE` budget leaves 2 ms of the 10 ms `BodyState::elements` budget for naming. Baseline before it was set (release, x86_64, single thread, median of repeated runs on a 4-core build container): `elements(Face)` 25 ms, `elements(Edge)` 0.6 ms, `elements(Vertex)` under 0.1 ms and `topology()` 1.8 ms, about 27 ms in total. Callgrind puts 98% of the face time in `BRepGProp::SurfaceProperties` (`BRepGProp_Gauss`): 18 x 18 integration points on every boundary edge of every face, half of the instructions in surface derivative evaluation and a third in inertia terms the query discards. The 194 cylindrical hole faces take about 21 ms (three of each wall's four boundary edges contribute nothing: its two circles are iso-parametric in v and one seam occurrence lies at the start of the u range), and the two planar faces with 194 holes each about 4 ms. Closed-form properties of a cylinder face whose parameter domain is a rectangle (`GProp_SelGProps` on the face's `gp_Cylinder` and `BRepTools::UVBounds`) took 0.85 ms for the 194 faces and matched `BRepGProp` within 1e-14 relative, inside the accuracy of section 3.6 item 2.

## 6. Acceptance tests

Tests live in `crates/partsmith-occt-sys/tests/` and use only the public API. Since M2 the calls that poll take a trailing `&Interrupt` (section 3.13). Tests 1 to 145 were written before it. They pass `&Interrupt::new()`, which their text leaves out. "Relative 1e-9" means `abs(actual - expected) <= 1e-9 * abs(expected)`. `BOX` is `make_box(&Plane::XY, 10.0, 20.0, 30.0)`. A face "with role R" is found through `Primitive::faces`. Unless stated, vectors and points compare within 1e-9.

Value types:

1. `Plane::XY.y_axis()` is (0,1,0); `Plane::XZ.y_axis()` is (0,0,1); `Plane::YZ.y_axis()` is (0,0,1); `Plane::XZ.to_world(Point2 { x: 2, y: 3 })` is (2,0,3).
2. `Transform::rotation(&Axis { origin: (0,0,0), direction: (0,0,1) }, pi/2).apply_point((1,0,0))` is (0,1,0) within 1e-12.
3. `Transform::translation((1,2,3)).then(&rotation about Z by pi/2).apply_point((0,0,0))` is (-2,1,3) within 1e-12.
4. `Transform::mirror(&Plane::YZ).apply_point((3,4,5))` is (-3,4,5); `Transform::scale((1,1,1), 2).apply_point((2,2,2))` is (3,3,3); `apply_vector` ignores translation.

Primitives:

5. `BOX` has kind `Solid`, counts solids 1, faces 6, edges 12, vertices 8; volume 6000 relative 1e-9; area 2200 relative 1e-9; center of mass (5,10,15); bounding box (0,0,0) to (10,20,30) within 1e-6; `is_valid()` is true.
6. `BOX` face roles: `Top` has centroid (5,10,30) and direction (0,0,1); `Bottom` (5,10,0), (0,0,-1); `Front` (5,0,15), (0,-1,0); `Back` (5,20,15), (0,1,0); `Left` (0,10,15), (-1,0,0); `Right` (10,10,15), (1,0,0). Each role appears exactly once.
7. `make_box(&Plane { origin: (1,2,3), normal: (1,0,0), x_axis: (0,1,0) }, 10, 20, 30)` has bounding box (1,2,3) to (31,12,23) within 1e-6 and its `Top` face direction is (1,0,0).
8. `make_box` with a length of 0, of -1 and of NaN fail with `InvalidInput`, empty `exception`, and messages `A box needs a length, width and height greater than 0 mm.`, the same, and `A value is not a finite number.`
9. `make_box` with `Plane { normal: (0,0,1), x_axis: (0,0,1), .. }` fails with `InvalidInput` and the invalid plane message; a plane with `x_axis` (1,0,0.5) and normal (0,0,1) fails the same way.
10. `make_cylinder(&Plane::XY, 5, 10)`: volume 785.3981633974482 relative 1e-9; counts faces 3, edges 3, vertices 2; roles `Side`, `Top`, `Bottom`; the side face geometry has kind `Cylinder`, radius 5, direction parallel to Z, location (0,0,5), measure 314.1592653589793 relative 1e-9.
11. `make_sphere(&Plane::XY, 10)`: volume 4188.790204786391 relative 1e-9; area 1256.6370614359173 relative 1e-9; faces 1 (`Surface`), edges 3 of which exactly 2 are degenerate and 1 is a seam, vertices 2 at (0,0,10) and (0,0,-10).
12. `make_cone(&Plane::XY, 5, 2, 10)`: volume 408.4070449666731 relative 1e-9; 3 faces with roles `Side`, `Bottom`, `Top`. `make_cone(&Plane::XY, 5, 0, 10)`: volume 261.79938779914943 relative 1e-9; 2 faces (`Side`, `Bottom`); 3 edges with one degenerate.
13. `make_cone` with equal radii (5, 5) and with both radii 0 fail with `InvalidInput`.
14. `make_torus(&Plane::XY, 10, 2)`: volume 789.5683520871487 relative 1e-9; faces 1, edges 2 (both seams), vertices 1. `make_torus(&Plane::XY, 2, 2)` fails with `InvalidInput`.

Profiles:

15. The rectangle profile `RECT`: XY plane, one region with lines tagged 1 (0,0)-(80,0), 2 (80,0)-(80,50), 3 (80,50)-(0,50), 4 (0,50)-(0,0). The shape is a `Face` with area 4000 relative 1e-9; `edge_tags` is a permutation of [1,2,3,4], and the edge tagged 1 has centroid (40,0,0) and length 80.
16. The same rectangle listed clockwise ((0,0)-(0,50), (0,50)-(80,50), ...) and with curves listed in reverse direction (line 1 given as (80,0)-(0,0)) gives a face whose element direction is (0,0,1).
17. A circle tagged 7, center (3,4), radius 5 on XY gives one face with one edge tagged 7 and area 78.53981633974483 relative 1e-9.
18. `RECT` with a hole loop of a circle tagged 5, center (40,25), radius 5 gives area 3921.460183660255 relative 1e-9 and 5 edges.
19. A half disc (arc tagged 1 center (0,0) radius 10 from (10,0) to (-10,0), line tagged 2 from (-10,0) to (10,0)) gives area 157.07963267948966 relative 1e-9.
20. On `Plane::XZ`, `RECT` gives a face with direction (0,-1,0) and centroid (40,0,25).
21. `RECT` with line 3 ending at (0,50.01) instead of (0,50) fails with `InvalidInput` and message `The profile is not closed: curves 3 and 4 do not meet (gap 0.01 mm).`
22. A loop with a circle and a line fails with `Circle <tag> must be the only curve in its loop.`; a zero-length line fails with `Curve <tag> of the profile has zero length.`; an empty region list fails with `The profile has no regions.`
23. A figure-eight loop (bow tie: (0,0)-(10,10), (10,10)-(10,0), (10,0)-(0,10), (0,10)-(0,0)) fails with `InvalidInput` and the invalid region message.

Extrude:

24. `extrude(&RECT, (0,0,1), 0, 30)`: volume 120000 relative 1e-9; 6 faces; roles are 4 `Lateral` (tags 1 to 4, each exactly once), one `StartCap` with centroid (40,25,0) and direction (0,0,-1), one `EndCap` with centroid (40,25,30) and direction (0,0,1); the lateral face tagged 1 has direction (0,-1,0).
25. `extrude(&RECT, (0,0,2), -15, 15)` has bounding box z from -15 to 15 and the `StartCap` at z = -15.
26. `extrude` of the circle profile of test 17 from 0 to 10: volume 785.3981633974483 relative 1e-9; 3 faces: one `Lateral { tag: 7 }` of kind `Cylinder`, `StartCap`, `EndCap`.
27. `extrude` of the profile of test 18 from 0 to 30: 7 faces (5 lateral, 2 caps); volume 117643.80550980766 relative 1e-9.
28. `extrude(&RECT, (0,1,1), 0, 10)`: volume 28284.2712474619 relative 1e-9 (oblique extrusion).
29. `extrude(&RECT, (1,0,0), 0, 10)` fails with the direction-in-plane message; `extrude(&RECT, (0,0,1), 5, 5)` fails with `The extrude end must be beyond its start.`; direction (0,0,0) fails with the zero direction message.

Revolve:

30. Profile `RING` on `Plane::XZ`: lines tagged 1 (10,0)-(20,0), 2 (20,0)-(20,10), 3 (20,10)-(10,10), 4 (10,10)-(10,0). `revolve(&RING, &Axis { origin: (0,0,0), direction: (0,0,1) }, 2 pi)`: volume 9424.77796076938 relative 1e-9; 4 faces, all `Lateral` with tags 1 to 4 each once; tags 2 and 4 are `Cylinder` faces with radii 20 and 10; tags 1 and 3 are `Plane` faces.
31. The same with angle pi/2: volume 2356.194490192345 relative 1e-9; 6 faces including one `StartCap` and one `EndCap`. With angle -pi/2 the volume is the same and the end cap lies in the half-plane y < 0.
32. A rectangle with one side on the axis (lines tagged 1 (0,0)-(10,0), 2 (10,0)-(10,10), 3 (10,10)-(0,10), 4 (0,10)-(0,0) on XZ) revolved fully about Z: volume 3141.5926535897934 relative 1e-9; 3 faces with tags 1, 2, 3; no face has tag 4.
33. `RING` shifted to x from -5 to 5 (crossing the Z axis) fails with `The profile crosses the revolve axis. Keep the profile on one side of the axis.`; an axis along X through (0,5,0) (not in the XZ plane) fails with `The revolve axis must lie in the sketch plane.`; angles 0 and 7 fail with the revolve angle message.

Booleans (`A` is a 10 x 10 x 10 box at the origin, `B` the same box placed at (5,5,5), `C` the same box placed at (10,0,0)):

34. `fuse(&A, &[B])`: volume 1875 relative 1e-9; 12 faces; valid; `history.inputs.len() == 2`.
35. `cut(&A, &[B])`: volume 875 relative 1e-9; 9 faces. `common(&A, &[B])`: volume 125 relative 1e-9; 6 faces.
36. `fuse(&A, &[C])` (touching on the face x = 10): a single box, volume 2000, 6 faces, 12 edges, 8 vertices (coplanar faces unified). A's `Top` face and C's `Top` face both list the same single output face in `modified`; A's `Right` face and C's `Left` face are deleted.
37. `cut(&BOX, &[cylinder radius 2 placed with origin (5,10,-1) on XY, height 32])`: volume 5623.008881569225 relative 1e-7; 7 faces, 15 edges, 10 vertices; BOX's `Top` lists one output face in `modified`; the cylinder's `Side` lists one output face in `modified`; the cylinder's `Top` and `Bottom` are deleted.
38. In test 37, BOX's `Front` face is unchanged: its `modified` has one index whose element geometry equals the input face's (centroid (5,0,15), measure 300), and `history.modified_from(Face, that index)` is `[Origin { input: 0, element: Front face }]`.
39. `cut(&BOX, &[cylinder r 1 at (3,10,-1), cylinder r 1 at (7,10,-1)])` (both height 32): volume 5811.504440784613 relative 1e-7; 8 faces; `history.inputs.len() == 3`.
40. `common(&A, &[B, D])` with `D` a 10 x 10 x 10 box at (7,7,7): volume 27 relative 1e-9 (intersection with every tool, the cube from 7 to 10).
41. `cut(&A, &[box 20 x 20 x 20 at (-5,-5,-5)])` is `Ok` with kind `Compound`, 0 solids, volume 0, and every face of A deleted.
42. `fuse(&A, &[])` fails with `A boolean operation needs at least one tool body.`; `fuse(&A, &[Shape::empty()])` fails with `Boolean operations work on solid bodies only.`
43. After tests 34 to 41, `to_brep` of every input shape is byte-identical to its value before the operation.

Fillet and chamfer (`TF` is BOX's edge between `Top` and `Front`, found through `topology()` and the face roles):

44. `fillet(&BOX, &[EdgeRadius { edge: TF, radius: 2 }])`: volume 5991.415926535898 relative 1e-7; 7 faces; valid; the input edge TF is deleted and generates exactly one face, of kind `Cylinder` with radius 2; BOX's `Top` and `Front` faces each list one output face in `modified`.
45. Filleting all 12 BOX edges with radius 1: 26 faces (6 planar, 12 cylindrical, 8 corner); volume 5949.834793498635 relative 1e-5; each of the 8 input vertices lists exactly one face in `generated`.
46. Filleting all 12 BOX edges with radius 6 fails with kind `Failed` (or `InvalidResult`) and user message `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the fillet. A smaller radius may work.`
47. `fillet` with radius 0, with an edge index 13, with TF listed twice, and with an empty list fail with `InvalidInput` and the messages of section 4.2; a fillet of the cylinder seam fails with the seam message.
48. `chamfer(&BOX, &[ChamferEdge { edge: TF, size: Equal { distance: 2 } }])`: volume 5980 relative 1e-9; 7 faces; TF generates one `Plane` face.
49. `chamfer` of TF with `TwoDistances { distance: 2, other_distance: 3, face: <Top> }`: volume 5970 relative 1e-9; the chamfer face's edge shared with the output top face lies at y = 2 (2 mm from the front plane, measured on the top face), and its edge shared with the output front face lies at z = 27.
