up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM` with `BLOCK`'s `Top` face, and up to `ROLL`'s `Side` with its `Bottom` face, each with `The faces an extrude ends on must lie on one plane or one cylinder.`; up to `ROLL`'s `Side` and `Bottom` faces and face 99 with `Face 99 does not exist in this body.`, since the index checks come before the surface checks; up to `ExtrudeEnd::Shape(Shape::empty())` with `An extrude up to a shape needs a shape with a solid.`; `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,1,1), 0, &Face { shape: BLOCK, faces: vec![BLOCK_BOTTOM] }, 0)` and `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,1,1), 0, &ExtrudeEnd::Shape(BLOCK), 0)` with `An extrude up to a face or a shape runs along the normal of its sketch plane.`, and so does the first with `T5`, since item 1.5 comes before the taper's checks; a direction against the normal is parallel to it, and `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,0,-1), 0, &Face { shape: <the box of test 216 behind the profile>, faces: vec![<its Top face>] }, 0)` builds a valid solid of volume 120000 whose `EndCap` lies at z = -30; `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,0,1), 0, &ExtrudeEnd::Offset(10), pi / 2)` and `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,0,1), 0, &ExtrudeEnd::Offset(10), -pi / 2)` with `The extrude taper must be less than 90 degrees either way.`; `extrude_to(&RECT, (0,1,1), 0, &ExtrudeEnd::Offset(10), T5)` with `A tapered extrude runs along the normal of its sketch plane.`; `T5` up to `ROLL`'s side and `T5` up to `ExtrudeEnd::Shape(BLOCK)` with `A tapered extrude ends at an offset or on a planar face.`; up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM` from a profile of two regions (`RECT` and the rectangle from (100,0) to (120,20)) with `An extrude up to a face or a shape, or with a taper, needs a profile of one region.`; and up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM`, and with `T5` over an offset, from the ellipse profile of test 191 with `An extrude up to a face or a shape, or with a taper, needs a profile of lines, arcs and circles.` The ellipse profile with `Offset(10)` and no taper builds, as in test 208. With their `regions` emptied (`Vec::new()`), the ellipse profile up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM` still fails with the conics message and the two-region profile with the one-region message: item 1.7 reads the face count and the edge kinds from `shape`.
222. Interrupts. `extrude_to(&RECT, Z, 0, &Face { shape: BLOCK, faces: vec![BLOCK_BOTTOM] }, 0, &interrupt)` and `extrude_to(&RECT, Z, 0, &Offset(10), T5, &interrupt)` with `interrupt` requested before the call fail with operation `ExtrudeTo`, kind `Canceled` and exception `Partsmith_Canceled`. The same calls with `Interrupt::word(word, 1)` and `word` at 0 give `to_brep` bytes equal to those with `Interrupt::new()` (section 3.13 item 9). Argument checks come first: the tapered call with `taper` `pi / 2` and a requested interrupt fails with `InvalidInput` (section 3.13 item 2).
223. Native only. Threads. 8 threads each run, 20 times in turn, the calls of tests 212, 214, 215 and 218 (outward) on their own copies of the inputs made by `from_brep(to_brep(..))` of shared values. Every result's `to_brep` bytes and roles equal those of the same call run alone before the threads start (section 3.10 item 9).
224. Revolve between two angles. `revolve(&RING, &ZAXIS, -pi / 4, pi / 2)`: volume `9424.77796076938 * 3 / 8` = 3534.2917352885174, 6 faces, the `StartCap`'s centroid at (15 cos(-pi / 4), 15 sin(-pi / 4), 5) = (10.606601717798213, -10.606601717798213, 5) and the `EndCap`'s at (0, 15, 5).
225. The other way and a rotated start. `revolve(&RING, &ZAXIS, pi / 2, 0)` has volume 2356.194490192345 with its `StartCap` centroid at (0,15,5) and its `EndCap` centroid at (15,0,5). `revolve(&RING, &ZAXIS, pi / 6, pi / 6 + pi / 2)` has the volume and face count of `revolve(&RING, &ZAXIS, 0, pi / 2)`, and its bounding box equals that of the latter's shape rotated by `pi / 6` about the Z axis (`transform`) within 1e-6, the bound of section 3.6 item 2.
226. Revolve angle checks. `revolve` of `RING` from 1 to 1 and from 0 to 7 fails with `The revolve angle must be greater than 0 and at most 360 degrees.`; from `pi / 3` to `pi / 3 + 2 pi` it is a full revolution: 4 `Lateral` faces, no cap, volume 9424.77796076938. Its seam lies in the half-plane of the start (section 3.4.4): the seam edge of its `Lateral { tag: 2 }` cylinder (the edge `topology()` flags `seam` on that face) has its middle (`edge_normals`) at (20 cos(pi / 3), 20 sin(pi / 3), 5) = (10, 17.320508075688775, 5) within 1e-9.
227. Texts of the new kinds, in `values.rs` beside test 167. `Operation::ExtrudeTo.to_string()` is `extrude_to`. For `KernelError { operation: ExtrudeTo, kind: TargetNotReached, exception: "Partsmith_TargetNotReached", message: .. }` the hint is `The extrusion never meets the face or body it should end on.`, the user message `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the extrusion. The extrusion never meets the face or body it should end on.` and the detail starts with `extrude_to target_not_reached: Partsmith_TargetNotReached: `. For `TargetPartlyReached` the hint is `Only part of the profile meets the face or body it should end on.` and the detail's kind is `target_partly_reached`. For `TargetStartsInside` the hint is `The profile starts inside the body it should end on.` and the detail's kind is `target_starts_inside`. Every new hint and user message passes the message lint of docs/guidelines/errors.md (test 130).

Wrap curves and modeled threads (M2, items 19 and 20, sections 2.15 and 3.4.14), in `threads.rs`, a module of `tests/integration.rs`, natively and under WebAssembly unless a test says otherwise. `CYL10` is `WrapSurface::Cylinder { frame: Plane::XY, radius: 10 }` and `CONE5` is `WrapSurface::Cone { frame: Plane::XY, radius: 10, half_angle: T5 }`, with `T5` of the paragraph before test 211. `H(phase, height, pitch, turns, hand)` is that `Helix`. An edge's points are its `element_display` polyline at 0.001 mm and 1 degree. A point lies on a helix within `t` when its distance from the surface's axis differs by at most `t` from the surface's radius at the point's height, and its height differs by at most `t` from the nearest height at which the helix passes the point's angle about the axis. `D8` is the depth of ISO 68-1's basic profile for a pitch of 1.25 mm, `5 sqrt(3) / 16 * 1.25` = 0.6765823467065926. `M8E` is section 8.2's external M8 x 1.25 x 20 tooth, `ThreadTooth { surface: Cylinder { frame: Plane::XY, radius: RE }, depth: D8, inner_width: 0.9375, outer_width: 0.15625, helix: H(3 pi / 4, 0.3125, 1.25, 14.75, Right) }` with `RE = 4 - D8 - 0.2` = 3.1234176532934073: the basic profile with 0.2 mm of radial clearance, from P/4 above the base's bottom to P/4 below its top, ending at the angle `pi / 4`. `ROD8` is `make_cylinder(&Plane::XY, RE, 20)`. `M8I` is `M8E` with radius `RI = 4 - D8 + 0.2` = 3.5234176532934076, and `BLOCK8` is the extrusion from 0 to 20 along `Z` of the square from (-9,-9) to (9,9) with a circle of radius `RI` at (0,0) as its hole: section 8.2's internal M8 x 1.25 x 20 input. `FAR` is `make_box(&Plane { origin: (100,100,0), normal: (0,0,1), x_axis: (1,0,0) }, 10, 10, 10)`.

228. Wrap checks, each `wrap_curves` call failing with `InvalidInput` and its message of section 4.2: `CYL10` with radius 0 and a helix (`A wrap surface needs a radius greater than 0 mm.`); `CONE5` with half-angle 0, `pi / 2` and `-pi / 2` (`A cone needs a half-angle other than 0 and less than 90 degrees either way.`); `CYL10` with the frame `Plane { origin: (0,0,0), normal: (0,0,0), x_axis: (1,0,0) }` (the invalid plane message); `CYL10` with no curves, `wrap_curves(&CYL10, &[])` (`A wrap needs at least one curve.`); `Segment { start: (0,0), end: (5,0) }` on `CONE5` (`A segment of the developed plane maps exactly only onto a cylinder.`); `Segment { start: (1,1), end: (1,1) }` on `CYL10` (`A segment needs a length greater than 0 mm.`); `H(0, 0, 0, 3, Right)` (`A helix needs a pitch greater than 0 mm.`); `H(0, 0, 4, 0, Right)` and `H(0, 0, 4, -1, Right)` (`A helix needs more than 0 turns.`); `H(0, 0, 20, 6, Right)` on `CONE5` with half-angle `-T5`, whose radius at its end height 120 is `10 - 120 tan(5 degrees)` = -0.4986 mm (`A helix on a cone must stay on one side of the cone's apex.`); and `H(NaN, 0, 4, 1, Right)` (`A value is not a finite number.`). The checks run in section 3.4.14 item 1's order: `CYL10` with radius 0 and `H(0, 0, 4, 0, Right)` fails with the radius message, and `[H(0, 0, 4, 1, Right), Segment { start: (1,1), end: (1,1) }]` on `CYL10` with the segment message.
229. A helix on a cylinder, of either hand. `wrap_curves(&CYL10, &[Helix(H(0, 0, 4, 2.25, Right))])` gives a compound of one wire with 3 edges and 4 vertices. `edges` is `[{0, 0}, {0, 1}, {0, 2}]` (as `WrapPiece { curve, piece }`), and the vertex whose generator is `{0, k}` lies at (10,0,0), (10,0,4), (10,0,8) and (0,10,9) for `k` 0 to 3, within 1e-9. Each edge has kind `BsplineCurve`. The first two have length `hypot(20 pi, 4)` = 62.959048280892425 and the last `0.25 * hypot(20 pi, 4)` = 15.739762070223106, within relative 1e-7. Every point of every edge lies on the helix within 1e-6 mm. With `Left` the vertices lie at (10,0,0), (10,0,4), (10,0,8) and (0,-10,9), the lengths are the same, and every point lies on the left helix within 1e-6 mm. The two hands meet at every half turn. The middles of the two whole turns (`edge_normals`) lie at (-10,0,2) and (-10,0,6) within 1e-6 for both hands, and their tangents tell the hands apart: each is within 1e-6 of `(0, -20 pi, 4) / hypot(20 pi, 4)` for `Right` and of `(0, 20 pi, 4) / hypot(20 pi, 4)` for `Left`. `H(pi / 2, 5, 4, 1, Right)` gives one edge from (0,10,5) to (0,10,9), the start of a helix turned by its phase. `H(0, 0, 4, 2 + 2e-9, Right)` and `H(0, 0, 4, 2 - 2e-9, Right)` each give 2 edges whose last vertex lies at (10,0,8) within 1e-9: within `e` = 1.588e-8 turns of 2, `turns` is taken as 2 (section 3.4.14 item 3).
230. A helix on a cone and a segment on a cylinder. `wrap_curves(&CONE5, &[Helix(H(0, 0, 2, 5, Right))])`, section 8.2's coil helix, gives one wire of 5 edges and 6 vertices. The vertex whose generator is `{0, k}` lies at (10 + 2 k tan(5 degrees), 0, 2 k) within 1e-9: (10,0,0), (10.174977327051849,0,2) and so on to (10.87488663525924,0,10). Every point of every edge lies on the helix within 1e-6 mm, at the distance `10 + z tan(5 degrees)` from the axis. `wrap_curves(&CYL10, &[Segment { start: (0,0), end: (10 pi, 10) }, Helix(H(0, 20, 4, 1, Left))])` gives two wires. `edges` is `[{0, 0}, {1, 0}]`, and the vertices `{0, 0}`, `{0, 1}`, `{1, 0}` and `{1, 1}` lie at (10,0,0), (-10,0,10), (10,0,20) and (10,0,24). The segment's edge has the segment's length, `hypot(10 pi, 10)` = 32.96908309475615, within relative 1e-7, and every point of it lies on the helix `H(0, 0, 20, 0.5, Right)` within 1e-6 mm: on a cylinder a segment of the developed plane is a piece of a helix.
231. An external thread. `modeled_thread(&ROD8, &M8E, External)`: `result.shape` is one solid, valid, with 64 faces (section 8.2's count). `tooth` has 62 faces, 124 edges and 64 vertices (15 pieces, section 3.4.14 item 6), and its volume is within relative 1e-6 of the exact tooth's, the trapezoid's area times the length of the circle its centroid runs along times the turns (Pappus' theorem for a screw motion), 115.9435967291399. The result's volume is within relative 1e-6 of `pi RE^2 20 + 115.9435967291399` = 728.9146831045078. `faces` holds `Side { Crest, t }`, `Side { Root, t }`, `Flank { Lower, t }` and `Flank { Upper, t }` for each `t` from 0 to 14 once, and `Start` and `End` once. `edges` holds `Helix { side, flank, t }` for both sides, both flanks and each `t` from 0 to 14, and `Line { flank, p }` and `Across { side, p }` for each `p` from 0 to 15, each once: 124 distinct generators. `vertices` holds `ToothVertex { side, flank, p }` for each `p` from 0 to 15 once: 64. The `Side { Crest, .. }` faces have kind `Cylinder` and radius 3.8, and the `Side { Root, .. }` faces radius `RE`. `{Crest, Lower, 0}` lies at (-2.68700576850888, 2.6870057685088806, 0.703125) and `{Root, Lower, 15}` at (2.208589803121541, 2.2085898031215407, 18.75), within 1e-9. Every point of every `Helix` edge lies on its helix within 1e-6 mm: the crest's at radius 3.8 from heights 0.703125 (lower) and 0.859375 (upper), the root's at radius `RE` from 0.3125 and 1.25, each from the angle `3 pi / 4` with pitch 1.25. By `topology()`, `Line { Upper, 0 }` joins `{Root, Upper, 0}` and `{Crest, Upper, 0}`, and `Line { Upper, 15 }` joins `{Root, Upper, 15}` and `{Crest, Upper, 15}`: the start's and the end's upper lines (section 8.2's history named them wrong, open issue 21 (a)). Each `Across { Crest, p }` for `p` from 1 to 14 joins `{Crest, Lower, p}` and `{Crest, Upper, p}`. In `result.history.inputs[1]`, every `Helix { Crest, .. }` edge lists exactly one image, every `Helix { Root, .. }` edge at least one, and every `Side { Root, .. }` face none. Parity: the counts and volumes of the tooth and the result are constants of the test, recorded from the native run, and both targets match them: counts equal, values within relative 1e-9.
232. An internal thread and a left-hand thread. `modeled_thread(&BLOCK8, &M8I, Internal)`: one solid, valid, with 68 faces, and a volume within relative 1e-6 of `18^2 * 20 - pi RI^2 20 - 129.66001159905076` = 5570.315710275226, the tooth's exact volume being 129.66001159905076. Its `faces`, `edges` and `vertices` hold the generators of test 231. The `Side { Crest, .. }` faces have radius `RI` and the `Side { Root, .. }` faces radius 4.2: an internal thread's crest is its inner side. In `result.history.inputs[1]`, every `Side { Crest, .. }` face lists no image (it lies on the bore) and every `Side { Root, .. }` face one. `modeled_thread(&ROD8, .., External)` of `M8E` with its helix's `hand` `Left`: one solid, valid, with 64 faces, the tooth's volume and the result's within relative 1e-6 of test 231's, `{Root, Lower, 15}` at (-2.2085898031215416, -2.2085898031215407, 18.75) within 1e-9, and every point of every `Helix` edge on its left-hand helix within 1e-6 mm.
233. Thread checks. Each `modeled_thread` call fails with `InvalidInput` and its message of section 4.2: `M8E` with depth 0 (`A thread needs a depth greater than 0 mm.`); with outer width 0 (`A thread needs an outer side wider than 0 mm.`); with outer width 1 and with inner width 1.25 (`A thread needs an outer side narrower than its inner side and an inner side narrower than its pitch.`); on `Shape::empty()` (`A thread needs a base with a solid.`); and on `WrapSurface::Cone { frame: Plane::XY, radius: 1, half_angle: -T5 }`, whose apex at height 11.430052302761343 lies below the tooth's top at 19.6875 (`A helix on a cone must stay on one side of the cone's apex.`). Both arguments kept (section 3.4.14 item 8): `modeled_thread(&FAR, &M8I, Internal)` fails with kind `Failed`, exception `StdFail_NotDone` and message `the thread's cut dropped its tooth`, and `modeled_thread(&FAR, &M8E, External)` with `the thread's fuse left its tooth apart from its base`.
234. Interrupts, repeats and threads. `modeled_thread(&ROD8, &M8E, External, &interrupt)` with `interrupt` requested before the call fails with operation `ModeledThread`, kind `Canceled` and exception `Partsmith_Canceled`. With `Interrupt::word(word, 1)` and `word` at 0 it gives `to_brep` bytes of the result and of the tooth, and `faces`, `edges` and `vertices`, equal to those with `Interrupt::new()` (section 3.13 item 9). The argument checks come first: `M8E` with depth 0 and a requested interrupt fails with `InvalidInput` (section 3.13 item 2). The calls of tests 231 and 232 (the internal one), run three times in one process with other allocations between them, give equal bytes and lists each time (section 3.11). Native only: 8 threads each run the same two calls 5 times in turn, on their own copies of the inputs made by `from_brep(to_brep(..))` of shared values, and every result's bytes and lists equal those of the same call run alone before the threads start (section 3.10 item 10).
235. Texts of the new operations, in `values.rs` beside test 167. `Operation::WrapCurves.to_string()` is `wrap_curves` and `Operation::ModeledThread.to_string()` is `modeled_thread`. For `KernelError { operation: ModeledThread, kind: Failed, exception: "StdFail_NotDone", message: "the thread's cut dropped its tooth" }` the hint is `A cosmetic thread keeps the face as it is and builds instead.`, the user message `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the thread. A cosmetic thread keeps the face as it is and builds instead.` and the detail `modeled_thread failed: StdFail_NotDone: the thread's cut dropped its tooth`; with kind `InvalidResult` the hint is the same. For `WrapCurves` with kind `Failed` the hint is `Report it with the details if the surface and the curve sizes look right.` and the user message `Partsmith's geometry kernel could not build the curve. Report it with the details if the surface and the curve sizes look right.` Every new hint and user message passes the message lint of docs/guidelines/errors.md (test 130).

## 7. Open issues

1. **History for constructive operations.** The architecture says operations return `OpResult { shape, history }` with history per input index. For primitives and sweeps the inputs are parameters or a profile, so this spec returns explicit face roles instead (`Primitive::faces`, `SweepResult::faces`), which is what naming needs; `OpResult` is used for every operation on existing shapes.
2. **Unification after booleans.** Booleans always unify coplanar faces and collinear edges (`SimplifyResult`). This matches Fusion's behavior and the naming spec's merge aliases, but changes topology compared with plain OCCT; it is not optional in M1.
3. **Shell rim history (section 3.4.9).** The contract that rim faces appear in the removed face's `modified` list and inner faces in the kept face's `generated` list follows `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid` and `BRepOffset_MakeOffset` (7.9.3 sources: closing faces' images via `Modified`, offset faces via `Generated`) but must be verified on the built library; the shim augments the history if it differs.
4. **Fillet and chamfer corner history.** Resolved by tests 45 and 142: on OCCT 7.9.3, `ChFi3d_Builder::Generated` lists the corner face where three fillets or three chamfers meet in its vertex's `generated` list, so the shim adds nothing.
5. **Tolerance constant.** This crate cannot depend on `partsmith-model` (architecture graph: `occt-sys -> mesh` only), so it defines its own public `LINEAR_TOLERANCE` (1e-6); a test in `partsmith-features` checks that the two are equal.
6. **Exceptions from OCCT classified as `Failed`.** Types such as `Standard_NullObject` or `Standard_OutOfRange` can signal OCCT bugs as well as bad geometry; this spec maps every `Standard_Failure` to `Failed` (an actionable message) and only non-OCCT exceptions to `Internal`.
7. **Openings tangent to kept faces along some edges only.** Section 3.4.8 item 7 opens a planar removed face that meets the kept faces only tangentially, convex rounds and concave ones alike (test 133). A removed face that meets kept faces tangentially along some edges and at an angle along others still fails: the body of test 102 with the concave edge filleted at radius 2 (fillet.md test 4) and the step face removed, and a box with only some of its top edges rounded and its top removed, make the first attempt return the solid unchanged; the fallback of item 3 does not run, since the edge between the step face and the fillet is tangent rather than concave, and running it anyway fails the distance check. Item 7's prisms would open these too, but along an edge where the removed face meets a kept face at an angle the prism's side lies in the plane of that face's inner face and splits it in two, one part named as the inner face and one as the wall end; supporting them needs those parts merged (`ShapeUpgrade_UnifySameDomain` limited to those edges, with its history), or the kept faces' inner offsets extended to the removed face as the thick solid does. A curved removed face that meets kept faces only tangentially (a round of test 45's result) fails as well: item 7 sweeps planar faces only, and OCCT's thick solid returns the input unchanged there.
8. **Mesher change and expected results (signed off by the owner on 2026-11-05).** Section 3.7 item 2 replaces OCCT's default mesher (Watson) with Delabella, which brings the tessellation of large spheres and of faces with many holes from 11 to 12 s down to about 1 s at the engine's tolerances. Meshes change wherever nodes are cocircular, and with them the print checks computed from meshes that `examples/*/*.expected.json` record (delivery.md section 2.8): those files must be regenerated with `PARTSMITH_BLESS=1 cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`, and delivery.md asks for the sign-off of technical design section 16 when a kernel change alters them. They were regenerated on October 13 2026 so that `cargo test --workspace` passes again. The owner signed them off as built on 2026-11-05. Reverting the mesher means blessing them again. Only print checks changed beyond the comparison tolerance, in 12 examples (the regenerated files also take the last digits of volumes and areas that the exact volume of section 3.6 item 1 and the stored curves of section 3.5 item 5 moved within 1e-15 relative): `bench/fifty-features`, the four cone examples, `fillet/every-edge`, `hole/blind-tip`, `revolve/sphere` and the four sphere examples. Most changes move finding boxes and values in the last digits; the pointed and apex-below-tolerance cones report 4 warnings instead of 11, because the thin walls the mesh shows at the apex group into fewer findings. A further speedup for whole spheres and tori would have to avoid OCCT classifying every surface node against the face's boundary, which only trimmed faces need. Since ADR 0039 the triangulation runs in the shim's copy with double-double arithmetic; the corpus's expected results stayed within the tolerance of delivery.md 2.8 on Linux (`cargo test -p partsmith-cli --test integration examples::`, 2026-10-27), and no file was regenerated for it.
9. **Validity check limited to the change (signed off by the owner on 2026-11-05).** Section 3.5 once checked every result in full with `BRepCheck_Analyzer`; it now checks what the operation produced or changed, which halves the time of most operations on large bodies (the measurements in section 3.5). The guarantee changes in one case: a shape that is invalid when it reaches an operation (a solid `read_step` kept although repair could not make it valid, or invalid bytes read with `from_brep`) passes its invalid unchanged parts through, and the operation returns `Ok` with an invalid result (test 111), where the full check repaired it or failed with `InvalidResult`. The guarantee for valid inputs is unchanged, since no operation writes to its inputs (section 3.1 item 3) and faces changed in place count as changed (section 3.5 item 1). A validity flag on each shape (the full check whenever an input is not known to be valid) was considered and not adopted: a flag that `read_step` clears would not cover bytes read with `from_brep` (the input of test 111), and treating every `from_brep` shape as unchecked would run the full check on every body that the disk cache recompute.md plans would read back; `Shape::is_valid` already lets a caller check an imported body once. The owner signed this decision off as built on 2026-11-05.
10. **Budgets after the October 13 changes (decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: revised to what is measured; resolved).** Release builds on the 4-core reference container at load 1, medians of nine alternating runs of the section 5 benchmark before and after: `extrude` of a 100-edge profile went from 17.0 to 15.4 ms (15 ms) once the check trusted the sweep's caps (section 3.5 item 4); it stays over budget on this container, mostly the check of the 100 lateral faces and of the caps' edges in their context, so it needs a measurement on the reference laptop or a budget decision. `volume` of the part went from 1.52 to 0.053 ms (section 3.6 item 1). The copies booleans now run on (section 3.1 item 3) cost a few percent: `fuse` of two primitives 5.9 to 6.2 ms, `cut` of two primitives 5.3 to 5.5 ms, `cut` of 20 cylinders from a box 39.4 to 40.8 ms, all within budget. A lighter copy (new elements sharing every representation instead of `BRepBuilderAPI_Copy`, which rebuilds each curve on a face) would save most of that, but `BRep_TVertex::EmptyCopy` drops a vertex's parameters on its edges, so it needs its own tests before it replaces the tested copy. Decided by the owner on 2026-11-05: the extrude row of a 100-edge profile is revised to what is measured, about 20 ms. On 2026-11-05 three runs of `cargo bench -p partsmith-occt-sys --bench budgets -- 100-edge` read 14.62, 14.78 and 14.84 ms. Section 5's row is 20 ms, the owner's number. It is more than that median plus the 20 percent of [ADR 0057](../adr/0057-budgets-set-from-the-measured-median-plus-20-percent.md), 17.7 ms. A faster check of the lateral faces, or the lighter copy above, is later work.
11. **Facade additions for helpful errors (ADR 0026, signed off by the owner on 2026-11-04).** Section 3.6 items 6 and 7, section 3.7 item 9 and section 4.3 add to the facade what the reference stability and errors study (sections 7.5 and 9, decision D7) needs: shell counts, so features can warn about closed cavities and floating joins; `width_across`, so a "too large" message names a face only when it was measured narrower than the requested size; `element_display`, so the engine can draw a lost element's ghost; and hints that are true for every failure of their operation, with the kernel's own text moved to `detail`. Points for review: `SubShapeKind` gains `Shell`, a variant code outside this crate may match on, and the shim keeps a fifth index map; `width_across` samples the face (its vertices, its edge middles and an 8 x 8 parameter grid), so on a curved face it can report less than the true width, never more; `element_display` meshes the element alone, so a face's ghost can differ in its triangles from the same face in `tessellate`; and every text of section 4.3 changes, which the features' `kernel` message (features.md section 4.1) and the export and import messages of io.md section 4 read. The `InvalidInput` sentences of section 4.2 keep naming indices and tags for callers and tests, and reach users only in the detail. None of them needs a new OCCT toolkit or dependency: `BRepExtrema`, `BRepClass` and `GCPnts` are in `TKTopAlgo` and `TKGeomBase`, already linked (section 3.12 item 3).
12. **Walls just under a round's radius.** Resolved by section 3.4.8 item 6, which counts a round as vanishing once its offset would be thinner than 0.01 mm, the margin item 6.2 already used above the radius, instead of `LINEAR`. Below that, OCCT's join offset of items 1 and 2 had to build inner rounds thinner than a micrometer: on `RC` of test 131, closed hollows failed from walls of about 1.9993 mm (0.0007 mm under the radius, the same for radii from 0.5 mm to 10 mm), so a 1.9995 mm wall reported `shell_too_thick` with a limit of about 1.9 mm while walls from 2 mm to 9 mm built, and open shells built with the vertices of their inner rounds merged. Test 132 and features/shell.md test 22 cover both.
13. **Blends built one at a time (ADR 0003 amendment of 2026-10-17, signed off by the owner on 2026-11-04).** Section 3.10 item 5 serializes `fillet` and `chamfer` because OCCT's blend builder keeps state in statics of `TopOpeBRepBuild` (test 134). Nothing is slower today: the budgets are single-threaded and recompute runs features in order. Once recompute builds independent branches in parallel (recompute.md section 1, "Later"), blends on different bodies will wait for each other. The alternatives are a modified OCCT whose `TopOpeBRepBuild_Builder` keeps the solid index as a member (a source change built by `scripts/build-occt.sh` from a patched tree, delivery.md section 2.13, with its source published under ADR 0034, and reported upstream), or running blends in worker processes. Neither is needed for M1.
14. **Mesh nodes across platforms.** ADR 0039 makes the triangulation's arithmetic the same on every platform (section 3.7 item 2), but not the nodes it triangulates. They come from OCCT's curve and surface evaluation, which Apple Clang compiles on arm64 with fused multiply-adds (OCCT is built with the compiler's defaults, and Clang contracts by default) and which calls the platform's libm, and Watson and node insertion compute in OCCT's own code with the same fused operations; where nodes are cocircular, a node moved in the last bits can flip a diagonal (section 3.11). The first platforms run (delivery.md open issue 1 (d)) showed it: `chamfer/pipe-tee` and `fillet/pipe-tee` swapped their mirror overhangs on macOS, which no variant of Delabella reproduced on Linux, and which print.md 3.7 item 7 now orders by triangles; the cone examples' results were not reproduced by the Delabella change alone either. Decision rule: test 137 passing on a platform proves that its triangulation gives the same triangles as everywhere else, so a mesh-dependent result that still leaves the tolerance of delivery.md 2.8 there comes from its nodes, with no further experiment needed. The second run (delivery.md open issue 1 (d)) met the rule on both runners: test 137 passed on `windows-2025` and `macos-15`, and only the thin walls of the four cone examples still differed (one value of `cone/inverted` on Windows; counts, bounds and values of all four on macOS). The nodes that decide them are the 144 rim points of the cones' flat ends, all on one circle, so their last bits pick the diagonals there. On Windows the source is the C runtime's `cos`: MSVC compiles OCCT with `/fp:precise`, which fuses nothing on x64, and on Linux raising glibc's cosine of `cone/inverted`'s 147.5° rim node (argument `0x1.4984a650d3328p+1`) by one ulp gives the Windows line bit for bit; OCCT built with `-ffp-contract=off` would not have helped and is not taken. On macOS the run does not tell Apple's libm from fused operations in OCCT's own arithmetic (a rim point is `R cos u` and `R sin u` in an axis-aligned frame, exact even where operations are fused, but its parameter `u` comes from OCCT's discretization), and the answer taken does not depend on which: thin walls measure only faces within `WALL_FACING_ANGLE` of opposite (print.md 3.9.6, ADR 0041), so no diagonal at a cone's rim decides a finding. What remains is any other mesh-dependent result on cocircular nodes: a tapering wall within that angle (print.md open issue 10), or a check that sums or compares triangles next to such a rim. On Linux on 2026-10-27, a wrapper preloaded around the examples runner moved results of glibc's `sin`, `cos`, `sincos`, `acos` and `pow` by one ulp in ten modes (the Windows cosine alone; one result in 4 of `sin`, `cos` and `sincos`, up or down, with four seeds, and one in 32 with two; every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up; every `acos` up; every `pow` up): with that rule, the four cone examples and `shell/thin-wall` matched in every mode, and three other results moved, each of which matched on both runners in the second run: `fillet/every-edge`'s first overhang (every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` up, 4 lines, and every `acos` up, 5 lines: its area 96 or 95.4 mm² instead of 96.6, its value 1.50095 instead of 1.50078 and its bounds, as the fillet's triangles next to the 45° limit join or leave it), `bench/fifty-features`' overhangs of 18.8 mm² (every `pow` up, 64 lines: findings 5 to 20 in another order, with their messages unchanged) and `hole/blind-tip`'s degenerate triangle (one result in 4 with seed 3, 3 lines: its one zero-area triangle elsewhere at the drill point's tip). The same check on the nine examples of gap list items EX-2 and EX-4 moved none of their results in any mode, `shell/concave-edge-join` (features/shell.md test 24) among them, but those modes do not reach what decides that example's first overhang (313.1 mm², bounds up to x = 1.414 and z = 8.586). The overhang takes in the 36 triangles of the arc join's facets below 45°: OCCT divides each quarter-turn edge of the join into `Ceiling(aDiff / Du)` steps (`GCPnts_TangentialDeflection::PerformCircular`), `aDiff` the edge's parameter range and `Du` half the 5° angle (`BRepMesh_CurveTessellator`), and π/2 over that step is exactly 36.0 in double arithmetic. A range one ulp wider gives 36.00000000000001, so 37 steps, whose middle facet's normal lies exactly on the 45° limit: the message then reads 312.5 or 313.8 mm² and the bounds end near x = 1.384 or 1.444. The range comes from the edge's parameters, which OCCT computes in its own arithmetic and through `atan2`: the two `atan2` modes below move all 36 `atan2` results of that example one ulp up, then down, and leave its overhang as it is, and no wrapper moves OCCT's own arithmetic, so only a run on Windows or macOS shows whether a platform gets another range; no real platform is known to. Every quarter-turn circular edge that the angle rather than the chord deviation divides (radius under about 100 mm at `generic-fdm`'s tolerance) has the same 36.0 boundary. The same check on the 29 examples of gap list item EX-5 (2026-10-27, with a wrapper rebuilt for it: its deterministic modes moved `fillet/every-edge` and `bench/fifty-features` by the lines above, its own hash chose the sampled results, and the sampled modes also ran with twelve more seeds) moved none of their results in any mode, among them the degenerate triangles at their cone apexes and sphere poles, the overhangs of their curved faces and the `small_hole` at the tip of `cone/cut`'s cone. The same check on the 23 examples of gap list item EX-3 (2026-10-27, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, whose deterministic modes moved `fillet/every-edge` and `bench/fifty-features` by the lines above and whose own hash moved `hole/blind-tip`'s 3 lines with seed 3) moved none of their results in any mode, and the sketch and extrude tests that load them (features/sketch.md tests 34 to 50, features/extrude.md tests 62 and 63) passed in every mode; none of the 23 has a print finding. Two more modes, every `atan2` result one ulp up and every one down, moved no result of the corpus, `shell/concave-edge-join` among them. The same check on the 11 examples of gap list items EX-7 and EX-8 (2026-10-28, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, whose deterministic modes moved `fillet/every-edge` and `bench/fifty-features` by the lines above, whose own hash chose the sampled results, and whose sampled modes also ran with twelve more seeds, plus the two `atan2` modes) moved one result: `shell/moved-body`'s first overhang (features/shell.md test 14), which is `fillet/every-edge`'s, since the shell keeps the fillet's outer triangles next to the 45° limit. It moved in every mode and seed that moved `fillet/every-edge`, by the same lines with the same values: 4 lines with every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up (96 mm² instead of 96.6, value 1.50095 instead of 1.50078, bounds from y = -9.4039 and z = 0.0018), 5 with every `acos` up (95.4 mm², and the bounds' upper y 9.4039 too), and 4 in three of the 18 sampled runs. Its overhang on the inner rounds under the opening (52.1 mm²) and its 16 degenerate triangles stayed in every mode, as did the touch line and thin wall of `extrude/union-touching-itself` (features/extrude.md test 43), the overhangs and thin walls of `shell/step-face-removed-thin` (features/shell.md test 26) and every result of the other eight examples, `chamfer/every-edge` (features/chamfer.md test 19) among them; one sampled seed moved `hole/blind-tip`'s 3 lines above. Their tests fix the documents, so `shell/moved-body` is blessed as it builds on Linux, like `fillet/every-edge`. The same check on the seven examples of gap list item EX-9 (2026-10-30, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, whose deterministic modes moved `fillet/every-edge` by the lines above, in the ten modes, the two `atan2` modes and twelve more sampled seeds) moved none of their results in any mode, the rod overhangs of `linear_pattern/second-face-direction` and the resin examples' findings of `mesh_validity`, `first_layer_contact`, `islands` and `small_features` among them, and the tests that load them (features/sketch.md test 51, features/linear_pattern.md tests 22 to 24 and print.md tests 125 to 127) passed in every mode. The same check on the 31 examples of gap list item EX-11 (2026-10-30, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, whose deterministic modes moved `fillet/every-edge` and `shell/moved-body` by the lines above, in the ten modes, the two `atan2` modes and twelve more sampled seeds, its own hash choosing the sampled results) moved the results of one: the first-layer contact of `circular_pattern/leaning-edge` (features/circular_pattern.md test 14), by 3 to 9 lines in 15 of the 24 modes (every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up, every `pow` up and 13 of the 18 sampled runs; not the Windows cosine, every `acos` up or the two `atan2` modes). The pattern turns its copy of the peg about the leaning edge, so the copy's bottom disc tilts and meets the bed only beside one point of its rim. Its contact triangles (print.md section 3.9.3: all three vertices within `BED_CONTACT_TOLERANCE` of the bed) are those Delabella draws between that disc's 21 rim nodes within 0.01 mm of the bed, which are cocircular, so their last bits pick the diagonals: with every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up the 576 nodes and the 21 near the bed stay where they were, and 16 contact triangles of 0.132 mm² replace 15 of 0.050 mm². So `low_bed_contact`'s value (0.000336, from 0.0000954 to 0.000884 under the modes), its bounds and in 4 modes its message (`0%` becomes `0.1%`), `tall_and_thin`'s value (23.3, from 14.4 to 43.8) and in 8 modes its message (`0.1 mm²` becomes `0 mm²`), and the lower z bound of the copy's overhang (-0.89109, the bottom disc less its contact triangles) move, as does that overhang's message with every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up (12.5 becomes 12.4 mm²); the island 0.893 mm above the bed and the peg's overhang stay. It is the check that sums triangles next to a cocircular rim named above, and in the second platforms run the cones' rims got other diagonals on Windows and macOS, so of the 31 this example is the most likely to differ in the next run of `rust-platforms`. Every result of the other 30 examples stayed in every mode, `hole/blind-tip` with them, and the 513 tests of the 13 converted `feature_<type>.rs` files, the 71 converted test functions among them, passed in every mode. Its test fixes the document, so `circular_pattern/leaning-edge` is blessed as it builds on Linux, like `fillet/every-edge`. The same check on the trust region of ADR 0058 (2026-11-07, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, in the ten modes, the two `atan2` modes and twelve more sampled seeds, its own hash choosing the sampled results) had no example on its list: no result of the new solver left the tolerance. Run on the rebuild of every example, it moved only the lines above: `circular_pattern/leaning-edge` in 16 of the 24 modes (4 to 9 lines), `fillet/every-edge` and `shell/moved-body` in 5 (4 or 5 lines each) and `bench/fifty-features` with every `pow` result up (64 lines). No other example moved in any mode. If a triangulation-dependent result shows on a platform, ADR 0041's option (b) is the next answer: the shim rounds the unit-square coordinates Delabella sees to multiples of 2^-24, so nodes that differ only in their last bits give the same triangles except within that difference of a rounding boundary; it amends ADR 0039 decision 4, changes test 137's triangle list and the cone results on Linux, and reaches only the faces Delabella triangulates, not OCCT's node insertion and Watson. From wave F's windows-crt track to the owner's decision of 2026-11-05, the Windows examples runner and the test executables of this crate and the engine linked the Universal C Runtime of the runner's Windows SDK statically, while the app called the `ucrtbase.dll` of the user's Windows. The owner then decided that every Windows program takes Windows' own UCRT while the Visual C++ runtime stays static (delivery.md section 8, option (c), and 3.6 item 2; ADR 0038's amendment of 2026-11-05). The `cos` that decided the Windows line above is now the `ucrtbase.dll` of `windows-2025` in `rust-platforms` and the user's in the app, as before wave F. The two are the same code only when the user's Windows has the runner's UCRT. On another Windows a result can still differ in its last bits. The decision rule above applies to it. The same check ran before the bless of the model corpus of [corpus.md](corpus.md) (2026-11-08, wave 1's corpus track, with a wrapper rebuilt for it, in the ten modes, the two `atan2` modes and twelve more sampled seeds, with its own hash for the sampled results). It ran corpus checks 4 and 8 against the files that a run without the wrapper had just written. No result moved in any mode: the expected results of the 46 corpus models, the stored outcomes of the 143 edits files and the metric line stayed as they were. The same wrapper moved the lines above for `circular_pattern/leaning-edge`, `fillet/every-edge` and `shell/moved-body` with every `sin`, `cos` and `sincos` result up. A second preloaded wrapper reported each check of the shim's Delabella copy that fails. It found no face of a corpus model or an example that takes ADR 0072's Watson fallback, in `partsmith rebuild --checks` or in an STL export.
15. **STEP import counts (ADR 0045, accepted by the owner with option (b) on 2026-11-04).** Section 2.12 adds `StepImport::skipped_shapes` and `invalid_solids` and keeps `warnings` as technical text (section 3.8.1 read item 3, tests 135 and 139 to 141), a public API change that the owner accepted on 2026-11-04. A solid without volume (section 3.8.1 read item 3) is kept and counted like any other invalid solid; leaving such solids out of the import and counting them, ENG-7's other option, would change these counts again. The review of 2026-11-04 kept the counts as built (engine.md open issue 22).
16. **Invalid inputs told apart (ADR 0053, accepted by the owner with option (a) on 2026-11-04).** Section 3.5 checks the shape a mirror or scale `transform` was given when its copy stays invalid after repair. It raises `InvalidInputShape` when that shape was already invalid. The `transform` hint `Report it with the details.` no longer words a STEP file that repair could not fix as a fault of Partsmith (engine.md open issue 22). `KernelErrorKind` gains the variant, a public API change that code outside this crate may match on, as `SubShapeKind::Shell` was (open issue 11). The check runs only after repair has failed. A mirror or scale of a valid shape does not reach it. On bench50's 426-face body `BRepCheck_Analyzer` took 91 ms in a release build here on 2026-11-03. The other operations keep `InvalidResult` and their hints, none of which words the failure as a fault of Partsmith. On `VOID_TWICE` and `SELF_VOID` a fillet and a shell fail that way (test 144). The booleans and the split gave no kernel error on them (features.md open issue 23). The owner can still pick ADR 0053's other options: the same check for every operation, whose failing calls then pay it, at each attempt of the "too large" search too, or no new kind, with the mirror feature asking `Shape::is_valid` of its source after `InvalidResult`.
17. **Steps that OCCT 7.9.3 does not interrupt inside** (M2, section 3.13). A stop waits for the longest such step of the call. `fillet` and `chamfer` never poll inside the blend builder: `ChFi3d_Builder` takes no range, and the fillet of `BOX`'s 12 edges polled 0 times in its 11.8 ms. `extrude` and `revolve` never poll inside the prism or revolution (0 polls for `RECT`). The validity check of section 3.5, the unification of booleans, the STEP parse and the STEP writer's `WriteStream` take no range, and neither do the copies of `split` (section 3.4.11). The check of the cut that builds `PLATE` covers its two new planar faces with 194 holes each. These faces take most of the 128 ms of section 3.5's whole check of `PLATE`. A request during that check waits for it. `tessellate` does not poll in every phase of a face: with OCCT's default mesher, the one face of a sphere of radius 250 at 0.0125 mm polled 100,473 times and still ran 583 ms without a poll. With the shim's Delabella, most of that face's time is OCCT's classification of its nodes (section 3.7 item 2). Measured on 2026-11-07 in a release build on the 4-core development container, with the shim's own mesher context: the tessellation of that sphere polled 9 times in 0.81 s (the shim's own two polls among them), and its longest gap was 350 to 415 ms over 7 runs. `PLATE` at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees polled 1,198 times in 0.78 s, and its longest gap was 157 to 262 ms. After the last poll, each call spends 20 to 30 ms turning the triangulation into a mesh. The cancel latencies of section 5's last row, measured as that section says on the same day: the `cut` of `PLATE`'s box and its 194 cylinders 74 ms, the `split` of `PLATE` 130 ms, the `write_step` of `PLATE` 46 ms, the `read_step` of the bytes it writes 341 ms (most of it the parse, which takes no range), the `fillet` of `PLATE`'s 4 outer vertical edges 91 ms, the `tessellate` of `PLATE` 120 ms and the `tessellate` of the sphere 316 ms. Remedies, each later work with its own spec: polls in the shim's face algorithms between their stages (`cpp/mesh_algorithm.cpp`) and a STEP parse in chunks. Neither changes OCCT. A blend can stop only between its calls, such as the attempts of the "too large" search of features.md section 4.3. The kernel spikes of section 8 measured M2's constructions the same way (release, natively, 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16). `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections::Build`, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell::Build`, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeFilling::Build` and `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle::Build` take a range and never poll. `BRepOffset_MakeSimpleOffset::Perform`, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset::Perform`, `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle`, `BRepFeat_MakePrism`, `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism`, `BRepFeat_MakeLinearForm`, `GeomProjLib::ProjectOnPlane`, `BRepProj_Projection`, `HLRBRep_Algo` and `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` take none. Each is one step between the shim's polls. In constructions that no decision takes, the longest such steps were these. The pipe shell's rail modes `contact` and `contact curvilinear` ran 1,139 and 1,543 ms before they failed, counted under gdb as upper bounds. `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism` ran 110.3 ms on the 100-edge profile's outward taper, and 171.9 ms in one run. A loft with a `MakeFilling` face waited up to 56.61 ms between polls, and 80.97 ms in one run. A phase of a tessellation ran long too: the shim's tessellation of the torus before `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` went 89.2 to 89.5 ms without a poll, and 151.3 ms in one run. The constructions the spikes' decisions take stayed under 150 ms between polls in every run measured. The cap's thread with its boolean has a longest gap of 4.443 ms, and `lofted-divided`'s longest on any input is 47.97 ms (68.27 ms in one run). The three-section loft's builder takes 3.15 to 5.30 ms. The 20-character label's longest gap is its construction's whole check, 31.4 to 40.2 ms, and 121.30 ms in one run. `DraftAngle` on the 100-edge profile takes 34.7 to 36.8 ms, and 54.4 ms in one run. The split prism up to a face takes 1.32 to 1.81 ms, and the bracket's rib by (b) 0.90 to 1.01 ms. The booleans, `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter`, `BRepAlgoAPI_Section`, `BRepBuilderAPI_Sewing` and `ShapeFix_Shape` poll inside. Since wave 2, `extrude_to` with a taper runs `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle::Build` as one unpolled step (section 3.13's table), and section 5's row measures its cancel latency. `measure_splines` and `spline_extremes` take no range either: they evaluate a few curves and run inside the checks of `make_profile` and `revolve`, where neither polls. Since wave 2, `modeled_thread` runs `ThruSections::Build`, `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle`, the edges' `BRepLib::BuildCurve3d` and the volumes of its check (section 3.4.14 item 8) as unpolled steps, and section 5's row measures its cancel latency on the cap. Spikes 8.8 to 8.10 measure the pipe shell, the groove cut and the split revolution the same way.
18. **OCCT's leaks in `read_step`** (M2, delivery.md section 3.8 and open issue 22 (b), [ADR 0067](../adr/0067-fuzzing-with-cargo-fuzz-in-a-crate-outside-the-workspace.md) choice 6). OCCT 7.9.3 leaks in `read_step`. On 2026-11-07 the fuzz target `step_read` with LeakSanitizer found two leaks. The first is 164 bytes in two `TCollection_AsciiString` objects from `StepFile_ReadData::AddError`, on a file that does not parse. The second is 12 bytes of an `OSD_Path` that `STEPCAFControl_Reader::Transfer` makes, on the import example's valid STEP file. A long session that imports many files grows by these bytes on each read. The fuzz job runs `step_read` without leak detection. Neither leak is reported to OCCT yet. The next step is a report upstream with each stack. A fix in a later OCCT release moves under ADR 0003's pin.
19. **What the check of STEP bytes refuses** (M2, section 3.8.1 read items 6 to 8, [ADR 0073](../adr/0073-a-check-of-step-bytes-and-a-patch-to-occt-7-9-3.md), delivery.md section 3.8 findings 2 to 7). `read_step` refuses a file whose instance references form a cycle or a chain of more than 500 references, a file that defines a number more than once or holds a scope, a file with a parameter list that breaks STEP's syntax or an instance number above 2147483647, a file with an edge curve whose ends are not vertex points, and a file whose error recovery builds a cycle of oriented edges (an oriented edge whose edge element is an oriented edge, ISO 10303-42 oriented_edge WR1). OCCT 7.9.3 crashed on, hung in, printed for or misread each of them before. Another program could still write such a file: a plain `VERTEX` is valid in ISO 10303-42's topology schema, a deep chain is valid STEP, and so is a scope, inside which ISO 10303-21 lets a file define a number again. A valid string with a doubled apostrophe right before `,` or `)`, such as `'He said ''hi'', ok'`, is refused too: OCCT's lexer ends the string at that apostrophe, its parser records a syntax fail, and the check sees the same broken list (read item 7). Measured on 2026-11-15: no STEP file Git tracks, none of the 270 STEP files `partsmith export` writes for the examples and neither of OCCT's own `data/step/linkrods.step` and `screw.step` is refused. The deepest chain among them holds 15 references, and none defines a number twice or holds a scope. Files from other programs are barely sampled: OCCT's two files are the only ones. The check follows `src/StepFile/step.lex` of V7_9_3 and is read against it again whenever the OCCT pin moves. The offer of the patch of read item 8 to OCCT upstream goes to the owner's report.
20. **OCCT's semantic checks are lost** (M2, section 3.8.1 read item 4, [ADR 0073](../adr/0073-a-check-of-step-bytes-and-a-patch-to-occt-7-9-3.md) choice 10). `Interface_CheckTool::VerifyCheckList` of OCCT 7.9.3, which `IFSelect_WorkSession::SetModel` runs inside `ReadStream`, adds one `Interface_Check` object to its list for every entity with a message, and clears and fills that same object for the next entity. The model stores the list, and the complete check list of read item 4 reads it back. Every entity's semantic check is then the messages of the last entity checked. Read item 4 therefore refuses the load fails of every entity and the semantic fails of the last one alone. OCCT's `master` of 2026-11-15 has the same code. Checked one entity at a time on 2026-11-15, the STEP files of ADR 0073's note hold two semantic fails besides the fuzz inputs, both `ERROR: non 2-manifold topology`, in `corpus/imported/assets/b6c7d671b8523a0077d44803532d9df1f9833ee845901c587e839cddc901d23c.step`, which Partsmith's export wrote. A mended list would make read item 4 refuse that file. The patch leaves `VerifyCheckList` as it is. If semantic fails are to count, ADR 0067 choice 10 is weighed again with this measurement. The report of the fault to OCCT goes to the owner's report.
21. **What the kernel spikes leave to later specs** (M2, section 8). Each spec settles its part before its code, by an ADR under the owner's rule where a choice is open. (a) features/thread.md ([ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md)): `ThruSections::Generated` of a section vertex is one piece late on a wire walked reversed (open issue 22). The spec either builds its section wires with every edge forward or names the flank lines by their two faces, and tests the start's and the end's lines. Its tests also check that a thread's boolean kept both arguments, which `Shape::is_valid()` does not (open issue 22). Settled for the kernel on 2026-11-23 by section 3.4.14: its section wires have every edge forward (item 5.4). `ThruSections::Generated` then gives every flank line its own section vertex, and `ThreadResult` reports a generator for every edge and vertex of the tooth, the start's and the end's flank lines among them (test 231). The shim checks that the join kept both arguments (item 8). Whether features/thread.md names the flank lines by these generators or by their two faces is track 3A's choice (naming.md section 3.6). (b) features/emboss.md: the orientation rule (a raised face given reversed and offset by minus the depth), with tests of a positive construction volume, of every top face kept and of a closed mesh on a cone. The body side's islands, one per counter, need a role of their own or take order-dependent names. (c) features/loft.md ([ADR 0075](../adr/0075-loft-rails-and-end-conditions-wait-for-a-later-milestone.md)): `ThruSections::Generated` names a lateral face by its position, not its segment, and a ruled loft's middle-section edges and vertices have no name. (d) features/extrude.md: the shim's check that the end lies within the target face and its message, gate 7's newcomer error; the face the prism never reaches; targets other than planes and cylinders, which the spike did not measure; inward tapers through deep bodies. features/revolve.md has no measured construction for up to a face. Settled in part on 2026-11-19 by `extrude_to` (section 3.4.3, [ADR 0083](../adr/0083-extrusions-that-end-on-a-face-or-a-shape-and-revolutions-between-two-angles.md)): the checks of a face reached in part or never are the shim's (item 5 there), with error kinds of their own, and only the measured targets and tapers are taken. The rest is open issue 23. (e) features/rib.md: section 8.6 confirms choice 19's (b) for straight profiles, thickened in a plane the section does not name, which the spec names. A profile of more than one direction, such as the V, leaves choice 19's "unless" clause open: (b) builds a wall across the rib's plane, and (a) with `Modify` true builds the rib. An ADR under the owner's rule settles it before features/rib.md: (a) for those profiles, with (a)'s mutex and its history of faces only, or a refusal with a message of its own. A rib over an opening of its body, which neither construction builds or reports, gets a refusal with a message of its own. The shim must detect it: for (b), the prism's far cap left among the split's kept pieces. Ends that stop short of the body were not an input. A rib profile that ends inside its body, as section 8.6's `inside` does, starts partly inside the shape `extrude_to` runs up to, and section 3.4.3 item 3 refuses it with `TargetStartsInside` (section 2.6). (b) built that rib in the spike because the fuse that follows absorbs what the split keeps inside the body. features/rib.md either trims the profile to the body first or specifies a kernel option that skips the start check, by an ADR of item 28's spec. (f) Item 26's kernel spec and features/sketch.md: projection and silhouette, where no construction passed section 8.7. The measurements point to `GeomProjLib` for lines and for circles not seen edge on, with the arc orientation of open issue 22 handled, and a segment for a circle seen edge on (not run as one construction). The spec says what a link to a line along the direction or to a circle seen edge on makes, and what a silhouette includes: outlines on seams and smooth edges, and sharp edges seen edge on. Its tests see a cylinder from a view that puts its seam on the outline. An ADR settles both before features/sketch.md in wave 3. (g) features/sweep.md (item 18, its rail modes in wave 4): a guide rail through `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell::SetMode` with an auxiliary spine meets the pipe shell's defects of open issue 22. `ContactOnBorder` keeps only the last section added, and `GeomFill_GuideTrihedronPlan` never sets the point on the guide that its law reads. On the loft spike's rail, `contact` and `contact curvilinear` failed after 1.16 and 1.50 s, with no poll after their second (section 8.4). That input was a closed section with the rail through its corners. OCCT documents `ContactOnBorder` as making the rail a boundary of the swept surface, the case of an open profile, which no spike ran. The sweep's spec measures its own rail inputs, with criteria fixed first, before it picks a mode.
22. **Defects the kernel spikes found** (M2, section 8). Five are in the kernel. `Shape::volume` misses section 3.6 item 2's 1e-6 on faces whose boundary curves span many turns in one interval (`lofted-whole`'s tooth, section 8.2). It also misses it on cylinders and cones bounded by B-spline 3D curves with ruled B-spline walls (the emboss constructions, section 8.3). On the emboss shapes, `BRepGProp::VolumePropertiesGK` puts each result at the body's volume plus or minus the construction's within 1.4e-10. On the thread's tooth, a mesh at 0.002 mm and the same faces cut into turns measured close to the exact volume (section 8.2). Remedies: faces that span at most one period, more integration points, or an adaptive volume. Print results and the examples' expected volumes depend on it. Wave 2's spec round found the same on its profile curves (measured on 2026-11-23). Section 3.6 items 1 and 3 integrate their planar faces and their solids precisely, but two measures still miss item 2's 1e-6. `BRepGProp::SurfaceProperties` puts the `Extrusion` face of the extrusion of test 199's region 1.9e-3 below its exact area at its default and 9.0e-3 below with a precision of 1e-12, and that of test 202's closed spline 3.0e-3 and 1.2e-2 below. `BRepGProp::LinearProperties` (it takes no precision) puts the length of test 191's ellipse 1.3e-3 above its exact 48.44224110273838 mm, with its length centroid 0.018 mm off, and the length of test 194's parabola 4.5e-4 below. `Shape::area` of such an extrusion and the fingerprints of its lateral faces and edges carry these errors. Fingerprints stay deterministic and names do not move, but a later fix changes them. Remedy: a Gauss-Kronrod area of a face, as `BRepGProp_VinertGK` integrates its volume, and an adaptive length and centroid (`GCPnts_AbscissaPoint::Length` at 1e-9 measured both lengths exactly). Track 2B (kernel round 2) specifies it before a feature records a fingerprint of these kinds. `Shape::is_valid()` passes a boolean result that dropped an argument (plain `swept`, section 8.2). `modeled_thread` checks its own join for it (section 3.4.14 item 8). The shim's mesher leaves open edges in the side of an embossed frustum at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees, on both targets (sections 3.7 item 2 and 8.3). A boolean of near-coincident faces can exhaust memory, and under Node it then aborts the module instead of failing the call (section 8.2). The coil of section 8.2 fused with its box leaves 4 open mesh edges on both targets. The meshes of gate 3 and the print checks see a gap there (section 8.2's Results). Spike 8.8 checks the coil's fuse for a closed mesh. Seven are in OCCT 7.9.3, none patched or reported upstream yet. `ProjLib_ProjectOnPlane::Load` returns the complement of an arc whose circle's axis points against the projection direction when `KeepParametrization` is off. `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections::Generated` of a vertex resolves an edge the wire walks reversed to its other end (`BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections.cxx` lines 452 to 469 and 1447 to 1494). Section 3.4.14 item 5.4 builds the thread's wires so that no edge is walked reversed. `BRepFill_Filling` passes the `GeomAbs_Shape` value as the plate's order, and `GeomAbs_G2` then throws. `BRepFill_Filling::AddConstraints` dereferences a null curve on a support face that is not a plane. `GeomFill_GuideTrihedronPlan` never sets the point on the guide that `ContactOnBorder`'s law reads. `BRepFill_PipeShell::Add` keeps only the last section in `ContactOnBorder` mode. `BRepFeat_RibSlot::Modified` and `Generated` return one function-static list (`BRepFeat_RibSlot.cxx` lines 277, 307 and 325). Calls on several threads corrupt it. The reports to OCCT go to the owner's report.
23. **What `extrude_to` takes in stage 1 of wave 2** (M2, section 3.4.3 item 1, [ADR 0083](../adr/0083-extrusions-that-end-on-a-face-or-a-shape-and-revolutions-between-two-angles.md)). Stage 1 specifies only the constructions spike 8.5 measured. `extrude_to` refuses, each with its own message: targets other than planar and cylindrical faces (cones included), a taper up to a cylindrical face or up to a shape, and up-to or a taper on a profile of several regions or of ellipses, parabolas, hyperbolas or B-splines. A start face and revolve up to a face have no call yet, and a profile on the boundary of the shape it runs up to has no measured outcome (section 3.4.3 item 3). Up to a shape, a profile that starts partly inside it is refused with `TargetStartsInside`, item 28's rib profiles that end inside their body among them (open issue 21 (e)). An inward taper whose lateral faces meet before the end has no check of its own: it is expected to fail in `DraftAngle` or in the validity check (features/extrude.md test 107 maps either to a message of its own), and no spike has run one (section 3.4.3's closing paragraph). Spike 8.10 (section 8.10) measures each class with criteria fixed first, and track 4A widens section 3.4.3 and features/extrude.md from its Results in stage 4, or an ADR keeps a class refused. m2-plan.md routes these deferrals (a dated note in its section 5, by wave 2's stage 1 integrator).
24. **Splines the sketch accepts and the kernel refuses** (M2, section 3.4.2 item 7.2). Settled on 2026-11-23. OCCT's `Geom2d_BSplineCurve` refuses a knot gap of at most its `Epsilon` of the knot before (`f64::MIN_POSITIVE` after 0) and a weight of at most `gp::Resolution()` (`f64::MIN_POSITIVE`), and the kernel also refuses knots closer than 1e-6 of their range, since OCCT builds them but integrates them wrong. sketch.md section 3.20.5 (track 1A) states the same three rules, and sketch.md test 319 refuses each of them, the knots with `Knots` and the weight with `WeightNotPositive`. The sketch also clamps open splines and gives periodic ones at least `degree + 1` poles, as section 3.4.2 item 7.2 asks. The two rule sets must stay equal: a change to one changes the other. A spline that reaches `make_profile` past them fails with `InvalidInput`, whose hint calls the failure a likely bug (section 4.3). sketch.md section 3.20.5 also states section 2.5 item 5's periodic convention for its own evaluation, and tests 198 and 201 check it against OCCT's.

## 8. Kernel spikes (M2 waves 1 and 2)

The M2 plan's item 10, gate 18 and risks 1, 2, 7, 8 and 12. Each spike answers one question about an OCCT construction before the feature spec that uses it is written in waves 2 to 4 ([ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)). The plan's choices 15, 17, 18 and 19 name the constructions to confirm. No product code merges from a spike.

### 8.1 How a spike runs

1. **Branch.** Each spike is a branch `spike/kernel-<name>` (`helix-thread`, `wrap-emboss`, `loft-rails`, `prism-extents`, `rib` and `projection`) from the head of the wave 1 branch, once section 3.13 is built there. It is never merged into `main`, and it stays in the development container, unpushed (amended on 2026-11-16 by [ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)'s note of that date). Its diff against its base commit is committed as `docs/design/spikes/<name>.patch`. The patch's first lines name the spike, its subsection, the branch, its last commit and the base commit `2c56ff9`, and say how to apply it: `git apply` of the file at the root of a checkout of `2c56ff9`, which skips those lines. On 2026-11-16 each patch passed `git apply --check` there, and applied there each gave its last commit's tree exactly. Every measurement can be reproduced from the patch. Each patch also changes three build files that item 2 does not mention: `crates/partsmith-occt-sys/build.rs` (the new shim sources in `SOURCES`, section 3.9 item 1), `crates/partsmith-wasm/Cargo.toml` (the dependency on partsmith-occt-sys that `main`'s calls need) and `Cargo.lock`. Beside each patch, `docs/design/spikes/<name>.json` holds every run's measurements. `prism-extents-sizes.py`, `rib-sizes.py` and `projection-sizes.py` there rebuild the cores of one construction that sections 8.5 to 8.7 report. `projection-views.rs` there is section 8.7's view check of the cylinder. It is not on the branch. Its header says how to run it at the branch's last commit.
2. **Code.** On the branch, the construction is added to partsmith-occt-sys as shim functions behind facade functions, under the rules of section 3.9. Tests in `tests/` and rows in `benches/budgets.rs` on the branch measure it. The web core's `main` in `crates/partsmith-wasm` calls each spike function once on a small input. The linker then keeps its code for the size measurement.
3. **Inputs.** Each subsection lists its inputs. The branch's tests build exactly those.
4. **Measurements**, for every input and every construction tried:
   - Validity: `Shape::is_valid()` of the construction and of each boolean result its subsection names, recorded as valid as built, valid after `ShapeFix_Shape` repair, or invalid.
   - Closed mesh: tessellated at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees and welded with `WELD_TOLERANCE`, every undirected edge is used by exactly two triangles (test 138's check).
   - The geometry checks its subsection names.
   - Time: release build, the median of 5 runs, natively on the 4-core development container and under Node on `wasm32-unknown-emscripten` on the same container (ADR 0057's method).
   - Parity: the native and WebAssembly results compared as ADR 0042 compares examples. Face, edge and vertex counts are equal. Volume, area and bounding box agree within 1e-9 relative.
   - Size: the size in bytes of `ui/public/core/partsmith_core.wasm` built by `scripts/build-wasm.sh` on the branch, less the size of the same build at the branch's base commit. delivery.md open issue 2 recorded 19,710,890 bytes (18.8 MiB) on 2026-10-23.
   - Interrupts: the number of `UserBreak` calls with an interrupt never requested, and the longest gap between two (section 3.13).
   - History: which OCCT history of the construction names each output face, edge and vertex from its inputs (section 3.3 item 8).
5. **Passing.** A construction passes when every input passes every criterion of its subsection and its size delta is at most 1 MiB (1,048,576 bytes). For the thread on the cap (section 8.2) and the 20-character label (section 8.3), the longest gap between two polls of the construction with its boolean is also at most 150 ms natively. That leaves 50 ms of gate 11's 200 ms on the desktop for the rest of the response. Three times both fit the web's 600 ms. A construction over it passes only with a new ADR that records the gap and its effect on gate 11. When the six spikes' deltas together would leave the core within 2 MiB of Cloudflare Pages' 25 MiB limit, delivery.md section 3.5's plan starts with its ADR before wave 2 (m2-plan.md risk 8).
6. **Records.** Once a spike has run, its subsection's Results get the date, the branch and its commit, the machine, and the measurements of item 4 for each input and construction. A result that confirms the plan's choice adds a dated note to ADR 0071. A result that replaces it needs a new ADR settled under the owner's rule before the feature's spec. The thread spike's result goes into ADR 0071's note or such an ADR either way (m2-plan.md choice 15). delivery.md section 3.5 records the size deltas. Note (2026-11-16): where a spike ran many inputs, its Results give the measurements by construction, and `docs/design/spikes/<name>.json` holds them for each input and construction (item 1). In the Results tables, an empty cell does not apply.
7. **No product code.** Nothing of a spike branch merges. The feature spec of waves 2 to 4 specifies the construction its spike picked. Its acceptance tests and code are then written from that spec.
8. **Wave 2's spikes** (note of 2026-11-19, [ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md) alternative 2). Sections 8.8 to 8.10 are the follow-up spikes `sweep-coil`, `thread-ends` and `extents-follow-up`. Their questions, constructions, inputs, checks, pass criteria and decisions are committed on wave 2's stage branch before any of their spike commits. No criterion then bends to a result. Items 1 to 7 hold for them with these changes. Each branches as `spike/kernel-<name>` from the commit that holds its criteria, not from `2c56ff9`, and its patch's first lines name that commit as its base. Item 4's size is measured against the core at that base, built with OCCT at `-Oz` ([ADR 0076](../adr/0076-occt-built-for-the-web-at-oz.md)) and against the same OCCT install as the spike's build (delivery.md open issue 23), not against item 4's figure of 2026-10-23 from before ADR 0076. Item 5's 2 MiB margin is ADR 0076 decision 6's: a spike whose core is above 24,117,248 bytes calls for the next size ADR. Item 5's 150 ms longest poll gap applies to the constructions each Pass names. The spikes run in wave 2's stage 2, and track 2B records their Results in this section before the stage 3 and 4 specs that use them.

### 8.2 Exact helix and modeled thread on a cap (`helix-thread`)

- **Question.** Does option (a) of the M2 plan's choice 15 give valid, exact and comparable threads for the inputs below, and does it do better than option (b)?
- **Constructions.** (a) Each helix is a `Geom2d_Line` on the parameter plane of a `Geom_CylindricalSurface` or a `Geom_ConicalSurface`. `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeEdge` makes it an edge on the surface, and `BRepLib::BuildCurves3d` gives it its 3D curve. The thread's flanks, crest and root are faces of `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections` between helical wires, the construction of OCCT's bottle tutorial. (b) `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` sweeps the thread profile along the helix approximated by a `Geom_BSplineCurve` (`GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline` of points on it, with a 3D tolerance of 1e-4 mm), in binormal mode along the axis. Both are fused to their base for an external thread, or cut from it for an internal one, with `BRepAlgoAPI_Fuse` or `BRepAlgoAPI_Cut` and the settings of section 3.4.5.
- **Inputs.** External threads on rods 20 mm long and internal threads in blocks, for ISO 261 M3 x 0.5, M5 x 0.8, M8 x 1.25, M12 x 1.75, M20 x 2.5 and M30 x 2, and for ASME B1.1 1/4-20 UNC. The profile is ISO 68-1's basic profile (flats of P/8 at the major diameter and P/4 at the minor diameter, P the pitch) with 0.2 mm of radial clearance. features/thread.md sets the print truncation of technical design section 11 in wave 2. The cap: an internal M30 x 2 thread 12 mm long in a cap of outer diameter 36 mm, and its neck with the external thread. For (a) also a helix of 5 turns on a cone of half-angle 5 degrees, the coil of item 19.
- **Checks.** For (a), 20 points sampled along each crest and root edge lie on their exact helix within 1e-6 mm (the exactness check). Later booleans stay valid: a cut by a 2 mm slot across the threaded part, then a fuse with a box touching the thread's end face. The volumes of (a) and (b) agree within 1e-3 relative. The face count is recorded, with whether the crest and root faces split per turn: their number for 20 mm and for 30 mm of thread (naming's order-dependent names, m2-plan.md risk 3).
- **Pass.** (a) passes when every input is valid as built, both later booleans are valid, the exactness check and parity hold, and the M8 x 1.25 x 20 thread with its boolean builds within 300 ms natively. (b) passes when every input is valid as built, both later booleans are valid, parity holds, the 20 sampled points of each crest and root edge lie within 1e-4 mm of the exact helix, and the M8 x 1.25 x 20 thread with its boolean builds within 300 ms natively. Gate 10's row for it is set in wave 4 from its measured median.
- **Decision.** (a) is confirmed when it passes. (b) replaces it by a new ADR when (b) passes and (a) does not. When neither passes every input, an ADR lists the failing sizes, and features/thread.md offers a cosmetic thread in their failure message (m2-plan.md risk 1).
- **Feeds.** features/helix.md and features/thread.md in wave 2 (items 19 and 20), and the roles `helix`, `crest`, `root`, `start` and `end` of naming.md section 3.12.1. The results name the history that maps each helix edge and each thread face to its section edge.

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-15 and 2026-11-16 (UTC). Machine: the 4-core development container (Intel Xeon at 2.10 GHz, 15 GB RAM), with Rust 1.97.0, Emscripten 4.0.15, Node 22.23.4 and OCCT 7.9.3. Branch `spike/kernel-helix-thread`, last commit `fbd7d46`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/helix-thread.patch`](../design/spikes/helix-thread.patch). Times are release builds by ADR 0057's method: the median of three sets, each set the row's median of 5 runs. Neither construction passes every input. [ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md) settles choice 15 under the Decision's last case.

**Inputs as built.** The profile is ISO 68-1's basic profile with 0.2 mm of radial clearance. An external tooth sits on a rod of the tooth's inner radius. An internal groove is cut from a block of side D + 10 mm around a bore. The tooth runs from P/4 above the base's bottom to P/4 below its top: 14.75 turns for M8 x 1.25 x 20 and 38.75 for M3 x 0.5 x 20. The cap is a cylinder of radius 18 mm and height 14 mm with a bore 12 mm deep. The neck is a tube of the tooth's inner radius (13.717 mm) around a bore of 11 mm. The coil is a tooth of 2 mm pitch over 5 turns on a cone of half-angle 5 degrees. M8 x 1.25 also ran at 30 mm (22.75 turns) for the face counts. The slot is 2 mm wide, through the part up to half the threaded length. The box is 0.5 mm wide and touches the thread's end face.

**Constructions tried**, on 19 inputs for (a) and 18 for (b), the coil for (a) alone:

- `lofted-whole`: (a) as written, one edge per helix.
- `lofted-edge-per-turn`: (a) with one edge per turn, an input option of `ThruSections`.
- `lofted-divided`: `lofted-edge-per-turn`, then `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle(2 pi)` on the tooth with its frame turned to the thread's start. Each face then spans one period of its surface. This adds an OCCT algorithm section 8.2 does not name.
- `swept`: (b) as written, the helix approximated by `GeomAPI_PointsToBSpline` of 32 points per turn.
- `swept-overlap`: (b) with the profile's flanks run 0.05 mm into the base.

| Construction | Inputs that pass every per-input criterion (valid as built, both later booleans, exactness, parity) | What fails | M8 x 1.25 x 20 with its boolean natively: rod, block | Cap natively: time, longest poll gap |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `lofted-divided` | 19 of 19 | the 300 ms only: every set of both M8 rows is over it (350.8 to 434.2 ms) | 416.5 ms, 418.0 ms | 160.0 ms, 4.443 ms |
| `lofted-whole` | 2 of 19 (cap, neck) | 16 slot cuts return a valid empty shape, and the box fuse refuses it; on the M3 rod both later booleans are invalid, and parity differs (6.4e-7) | 1,259 ms, 1,334 ms | 210.8 ms, 8.949 ms |
| `lofted-edge-per-turn` | 3 of 19 (M3 rod, cap, neck) | 14 empty slot cuts and refused box fuses; the M12 rod's fuse returns the rod alone, with no crest or root edge; the M12 block's cut is invalid | 338.3 ms, 413.1 ms | 142.2 ms, 4.716 ms |
| `swept` | 0 of 18 | invalid results (the M3, M5 and M8 30 mm rods, the M8 block, the cap); `Standard_OutOfMemory` at 6 GB on 5 blocks; the M8 30 mm block unfinished after 300 s; parity only on the M30 x 2 rod, whose result lost the base | 6,741 ms, 15,852 ms | 3,368 ms, 2,461 ms |
| `swept-overlap` | 0 of 18 | parity on all 18 (1.8e-9 to 6.2e-4 relative); exactness on the M3, M5 and 1/4-20 rods (up to 6.4e-4 mm) | 974.2 ms, 998.2 ms | 368.3 ms, 76.30 ms |

**Validity, checks and volumes.** Every construction of the lofted variants and of `swept-overlap` is valid as built on every input. `lofted-divided`'s thread with its boolean, its slot cut and its box fuse are valid as built on all 19. Its crest and root samples lie within 7.5e-8 mm of the exact helix (`lofted-whole` within 4.5e-8 mm, the bare cone helix within 4.07e-8 mm). The swept crest and root edges lie 5.8e-5 to 1.008e-4 mm off. Every `lofted-divided` mesh is closed except the coil's box fuse, which has 4 open edges on both targets. Its tooth matches the exact tooth volume within 6e-8 relative, and its result the base with the tooth within 1e-8. `lofted-divided` and `swept-overlap` agree within 1.1e-4 relative on every input, within the Checks' 1e-3.

**Why the literal (a) fails.** `ThruSections`' caps lie on the periodic cylinder or cone over many periods (u over 92.7 rad for M8). The fuse with the base works. A later boolean classifies points against such a face in its first period and picks the wrong turn. Faces divided at every turn keep every later boolean valid. `Shape::volume` also misreads faces that span many turns: `lofted-whole`'s M8 tooth measures 164.006 mm³ against the exact 115.944 mm³ (open issue 22). A mesh of that tooth at 0.002 mm holds 115.706 mm³, and the same faces cut into turns measure 115.94359 mm³. Plain (b)'s inner side lies within 1e-4 mm of the base's cylinder but not on it. Its boolean of near-coincident faces returns valid shapes that lost the base (the M8 rod keeps 0.05531 of 728.91 mm³), invalid shapes, or runs out of memory. Under Node that aborts the module at 4 GB. `Shape::is_valid()` accepts the shapes that lost the base (open issue 22).

**Faces.** M8 x 1.25 at 20 and 30 mm: `lofted-divided`'s rod has 64 and 96 faces, with 15 and 23 crest faces and 15 and 23 root faces. Its block has 68 and 100. The other constructions keep the tooth's outer face whole. The faces on the base's cylinder split at its seam once per turn in every construction. Crest and root face names are order dependent in `lofted-divided` (m2-plan.md risk 3).

**Time.** `lofted-divided` takes 128.0 ms (coil) to 1,046 ms (M3 x 0.5 block) natively, growing with the turns. Under Node it takes 220.8 to 1,693 ms, 1.39 to 1.91 times its native medians: M8 x 1.25 x 20 663.6 ms on the rod and 646.1 ms in the block, the cap 268.2 ms. The set medians of one row spread by up to 33 percent natively and 31 percent under Node. One process's median of 5 runs of a row of 130 to 270 ms can lie up to 39 percent above the median of three sets. The rows run before a row in the same process move it too.
