**Parity.** `lofted-divided` matches on all 19 inputs, the largest gap 1.1e-14 relative. `lofted-whole` and `lofted-edge-per-turn` match on every input except `lofted-whole`'s M3 rod. `swept-overlap`'s construction matches, and its thread with its boolean differs on all 18 inputs. Of the 9 inputs plain `swept` ran on both targets, parity holds only on the M30 x 2 rod, whose result lost the base. No input of (b) passes with parity.

**Size.** The core is 20,704,118 bytes, a delta of 707,153 bytes. One build holds both constructions and every variant. No construction was built alone.

**Interrupts.** The cap's `lofted-divided` thread with its boolean polls 2,931 times. Its longest gap is 4.443 ms (sets 4.443, 4.016 and 4.729 ms, most 5.260 ms), within section 8.1 item 5's 150 ms. The cap's longest gap is 4.716 ms with `lofted-edge-per-turn`, 8.949 ms with `lofted-whole` and 76.30 ms with `swept-overlap` (most 103.8 ms). Plain `swept` fails the 150 ms at 2,461 ms (most 2,685 ms). `lofted-divided`'s longest gap on any input is 47.97 ms (M3 rod; most of any run 68.27 ms, M3 block), inside the boolean or one unpolled step. The first poll comes within 0.1 ms of the shim's entry. The facade spends at most 0.150 ms outside the shim in any run. Plain `swept` reaches 22,321 ms (M8 30 mm rod), and 24,260 ms in one run (M5 block). `ThruSections::Build` and `MakePipeShell::Build` ignore their range, and `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle` takes none (open issue 17).

**History.** Every crest and root helix edge is a section edge of `ThruSections`. The outer ones stay whole. The boolean splits each inner one in two where the base's seam crosses it. The flanks are `ThruSections::GeneratedFace` of the inner section edges, the start and end faces `GeneratedFace` of the end segments, and the outer face `LastShape` divided through `ShapeBuild_ReShape`'s history. The corner vertices are the section vertices. `ThruSections::Generated` of a vertex is one piece late along the upper helix, which the wire walks reversed (open issue 22). The start's upper flank line then has no generator, and the end's has two. The 14 edges the division adds across the outer face have none. In (b) the inner crest or root edges are the boolean's intersection curves, with no history.

**The Decision applied.** Neither construction passes every input. (b) passes no input and cannot replace (a). The Decision's last case applies. [ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md) lists the failing sizes, takes `lofted-divided` as choice 15's (a) and accepts its measured M8 time. That acceptance is for the owner's review at the M2 gate, since the 300 ms was fixed before the run. Gate 10's row is set in wave 4. ADR 0071 gets no confirming note.

### 8.3 Wrap emboss on a cylinder and a cone (`wrap-emboss`)

- **Question.** Does a planar profile mapped onto the parameter plane of a cylindrical or conical face and thickened along the face normals give valid raised and recessed solids, exact on the surface and fast enough for a 20-character label (the M2 plan's choice 17, option (b))?
- **Constructions.** The profile lies in the plane tangent to the face. On a cylinder of radius r, the point (x, y) maps to u = x / r and v = y. The map is affine: lines map to `Geom2d_Line`, circles and arcs to arcs of `Geom2d_Ellipse`, and B-splines to `Geom2d_BSplineCurve` with mapped poles, all exact. On a cone, (x, y) maps through the polar coordinates of the developed sector. Its curves are approximated by `Geom2dConvert_ApproxCurve` or `Geom2dAPI_Interpolate` within 1e-6 mm. The face is `BRepBuilderAPI_MakeFace` of the surface and the mapped wire, with `BRepLib::BuildCurves3d`. Its solid is tried with `BRepOffset_MakeSimpleOffset` and `SetBuildSolidFlag(true)`, and with `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid::MakeThickSolidBySimple`. An emboss fuses it to the body, and a deboss cuts it.
- **Inputs.** A 20-character label on a cylinder of radius 15 mm (the cap's side), raised and recessed by 0.6 mm. Its characters are `PARTSMITH LABEL 2026`, 5 mm tall, drawn from lines and arcs, with one closed B-spline outline (the font reader comes with item 27). The same label across the cylinder's seam. A label longer than half the circumference. A 5-character label on a frustum of radii 20 and 15 mm, 30 mm tall.
- **Checks.** Every vertex and 20 sampled points of each raised top face lie at distance r + 0.6 mm from the axis within 1e-6 mm on the cylinder, and on the cone offset by 0.6 mm along its normals within 1e-6 mm. On the cylinder, each wrapped outline's length equals its planar length within 1e-6 relative.
- **Pass.** Every input is valid as built, the checks and parity hold, and the 20-character label with its boolean builds within 1 s natively. Gate 10's row is set in wave 4.
- **Decision.** A pass confirms choice 17's exact wrap. features/emboss.md takes the thickening that passes, the first listed where both do. A failure on the cone keeps cones on projection with a warning, by a new ADR.
- **Feeds.** features/emboss.md in wave 4 (item 31). The thread's parameter-plane map of section 8.2 and this one are one code path.

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), on section 8.2's machine and tools. Branch `spike/kernel-wrap-emboss`, last commit `7ddebc5`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/wrap-emboss.patch`](../design/spikes/wrap-emboss.patch). It brings section 8.2's parameter-plane map from `fbd7d46`. The times come from `c471b27`, whose bench and test binaries `7ddebc5` builds byte for byte. Both thickenings pass every input and every criterion. The pass confirms choice 17 ([ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)'s note of 2026-11-16).

**Inputs as built.** The 18 characters of `PARTSMITH LABEL 2026`, 14 of them distinct, are closed outlines 5 mm tall with strokes 0.8 mm wide. The S is one periodic cubic B-spline, and every other curve is a segment or an arc. The label has 18 regions, 26 loops and 162 curves (140 segments, 21 arcs, 1 B-spline). It is 68.1 mm long, 72.3 percent of the circumference of the cylinder of radius 15 mm and height 14 mm. Its baseline is at 4.5 mm. `label` centers it opposite the seam, and `seam` runs the seam through its 13th character, B. The label is already longer than half the circumference. `long` therefore adds an underline of 69.1 mm by 0.8 mm, one outline over 4.607 rad (73.3 percent). `cone` puts `PARTS` (43 curves) on the frustum of radii 20 and 15 mm and height 30 mm. Each input is raised by 0.6 mm and fused, and recessed by 0.6 mm and cut.

**Constructions.** `simple-offset` (`BRepOffset_MakeSimpleOffset` with `SetBuildSolidFlag(true)`) and `thick-solid` (`BRepOffsetAPI_MakeThickSolid::MakeThickSolidBySimple`), one face and one thickening per region, the solids in one compound. In OCCT 7.9.3 the second runs the first. Their shapes and history are identical, and only their times differ. Each face is given as its solid's boundary: reversed when raised, as built when recessed, offset by -0.6 mm. Given as built and offset outward (the first run, `37f5706`), every raised solid came out inside out: a negative volume that `BRepCheck_Analyzer` accepts, and an empty fuse.

| Input | Result: faces, edges, vertices | Top faces kept | Top faces off the offset surface | Wrapped length, relative | Natively: `simple-offset`, `thick-solid` | Node: `simple-offset`, `thick-solid` | Longest poll gap natively |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `label` raised | 191, 489, 326 | 18 of 18 | 9.2e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 382.1 ms, 347.5 ms | 612.3 ms, 540.9 ms | 40.19 ms, 36.21 ms |
| `label` recessed | 191, 489, 326 | 18 of 18 | 8.5e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 402.6 ms, 409.8 ms | 577.6 ms, 593.2 ms | 39.99 ms, 39.44 ms |
| `seam` raised | 191, 492, 328 | 18 of 18 | 9.2e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 414.7 ms, 417.9 ms | 535.0 ms, 541.8 ms | 39.29 ms, 38.38 ms |
| `seam` recessed | 191, 492, 328 | 18 of 18 | 7.4e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 344.2 ms, 323.9 ms | 603.8 ms, 600.7 ms | 35.86 ms, 31.39 ms |
| `long` raised | 196, 501, 334 | 19 of 19 | 9.2e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 410.6 ms, 395.3 ms | 561.8 ms, 563.3 ms | 44.21 ms, 42.24 ms |
| `long` recessed | 196, 501, 334 | 19 of 19 | 8.5e-15 mm | 7.020e-9 | 341.7 ms, 362.3 ms | 585.9 ms, 588.5 ms | 33.25 ms, 41.51 ms |
| `cone` raised | 54, 132, 88 | 5 of 5 | 7.8e-15 mm | 2.081e-9 (measured) | 185.2 ms, 185.9 ms | 266.0 ms, 253.1 ms | 20.87 ms, 20.83 ms |
| `cone` recessed | 54, 132, 88 | 5 of 5 | 4.3e-15 mm (Node 6.2e-15 mm) | 2.081e-9 (measured) | 198.1 ms, 197.8 ms | 276.9 ms, 273.3 ms | 21.08 ms, 21.25 ms |

**Validity and meshes.** Every construction and every emboss with its boolean is valid as built, on every input with both thickenings, natively and under Node. No call needed a repair. Every construction's mesh is closed, and so is every result's on the cylinder. The cone's results have 8 open mesh edges on both targets: two gaps in the triangulation of the frustum's side next to the label (open issue 22). Section 8.3 does not count closed meshes.

**Checks.** The vertices and 20 inner points of every top face and floor lie on the offset surface within 9.2e-15 mm on the cylinder and 7.8e-15 mm on the cone. Their edges' 3D curves lie within 3.6e-8 to 8.4e-8 mm (measured). Every wrapped outline on the cylinder keeps its planar length within 7.02e-9 relative. The map's images lie within 1.5e-14 mm of the exact images on the cylinder. On the cone `Geom2dConvert_ApproxCurve` approximates them within 3.52e-8 mm.

**Volumes.** `BRepGProp::VolumePropertiesGK` puts every result at the body's volume plus or minus the construction's, within 2.6e-11 to 1.4e-10 relative. `Shape::volume` misses section 3.6 item 2's 1e-6: the label's construction measures 87.080325 mm³ against 87.496070 mm³ (4.75e-3 relative), and the cone's 23.476585 mm³ against 23.891793 mm³ (1.74e-2, open issue 22).

**Time.** The 20-character label with its boolean (`label` and `seam`, raised and recessed, both thickenings) takes 323.9 to 417.9 ms natively against 1 s. Its largest set is 429.7 ms. Under Node it takes 535.0 to 612.3 ms. A single set on this container can be off by about a quarter. The native medians of three sets moved by -25 to +11 percent between two commits whose code differs only in names. Gate 10's wave 4 row needs the median of several sets.

**Parity.** 32 lines, the construction and the result of 16 runs: counts equal, values within 2.76e-12 relative. Three measurements differ by rounding only: the seam's map deviation (9.058e-15 mm natively, 8.882e-15 mm under Node), the cone recessed floors' distance (4.330e-15 mm against 6.217e-15 mm) and the adaptive volumes' last digits.

**Size.** The core is 20,144,453 bytes, a delta of 147,488 bytes, with both thickenings and the map with its cone approximation.

**Interrupts.** The label polls 12,654 (`label` raised) to 17,361 times (`seam` recessed). Its longest gap is 31.4 to 40.2 ms, always after poll 37: the construction's whole `BRepCheck_Analyzer` over 18 solids. The most of any run is 121.30 ms, in one run of `seam` raised `thick-solid`. It passes section 8.1 item 5's 150 ms, also with the last poll to the end (32.1 to 40.5 ms) and the facade's time outside the shim (at most 1.27 ms) added. `BRepOffset_MakeSimpleOffset::Perform` takes no range, and the shim polls between regions. Under Node the label's longest gap is 52.5 to 61.4 ms (most 90.36 ms).

**History.** The top faces, edges and vertices are the thickening's `Generated` of the wrapped face's elements. The base edges and vertices are the wrapped face's own, kept by the boolean. Across the seam, B's two base edges split in two. The walls and the edges from base to top vertices have no OCCT history: `BuildMissingWalls` makes them. The shim finds them by adjacency, each from one outline curve. The body's side becomes the side with holes and one island per counter, 8 for the label, which the face rule names in order.

**The Decision applied.** Both thickenings pass. The pass confirms choice 17's exact wrap on cylinders and on cones, and cones need no projection ADR. features/emboss.md takes the first listed, `BRepOffset_MakeSimpleOffset` with `SetBuildSolidFlag(true)`. It must state the orientation rule and test a positive construction volume and every top face kept, since validity alone does not catch an inside-out solid.

### 8.4 Loft with rails (`loft-rails`)

- **Question.** Do `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections` between profiles and `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` with several sections along a spine give valid lofts with rails and a centerline, and can either meet a tangent or curvature end condition (the M2 plan's choice 18)?
- **Constructions.** `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections`, solid, ruled and smooth, with `AddWire`, `AddVertex` for a point end and `CheckCompatibility`. `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` with each section added (`Add`), a centerline as its spine, and a guide rail through `SetMode` with an auxiliary spine. For end conditions: an extra section offset along the end's tangent, and the tangency constraints of `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeFilling` on the end faces.
- **Inputs.** Three sections: a 40 mm square at z = 0, a circle of radius 15 mm at z = 30 and a 20 mm square turned 45 degrees at z = 60. A loft from a 40 mm square to a point at z = 40. Two sections with one guide rail, a planar B-spline from a corner of one to a corner of the other. Two sections along an arc centerline. Sections of 4 and of 3 edges.
- **Checks.** Each section lies on the loft within 1e-6 mm. The rail lies on its lateral face within 1e-6 mm. For an end condition, the angle between the loft's face and the neighbor face along the end edge, from `Shape::edge_normals`, is within 1e-6 rad of the one asked for.
- **Pass.** Every input is valid as built, the checks and parity hold, and the three-section loft builds within 100 ms natively. End conditions pass when one construction meets them on every input that has one.
- **Decision.** A pass confirms choice 18. End conditions that no construction meets are listed in features/loft.md as later, by a new ADR.
- **Feeds.** features/loft.md in wave 3 (item 30, first in the cut order of m2-plan.md risk 11).

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), on section 8.2's machine and tools. Branch `spike/kernel-loft-rails`, last commit `34ef52f`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/loft-rails.patch`](../design/spikes/loft-rails.patch). `34ef52f` changes only a doc comment of the web core's `main`. Its bench binaries are the ones timed at `ceffb12`, byte for byte, and its core is byte-identical to `6a73b75`'s. No construction follows the guide rail. Choice 18 is not confirmed, and [ADR 0075](../adr/0075-loft-rails-and-end-conditions-wait-for-a-later-milestone.md) settles rails and end conditions.

**Inputs as built.** `three-sections`: the three sections above, with the line from z = 0 to z = 60 as the pipe shell's centerline. `point-end`: the 40 mm square at z = 0 to the point (0, 0, 40). `guide-rail`: the 40 mm square at z = 0 and a 20 mm square at z = 40, with a cubic Bezier rail in the plane x = y from the corner (20, 20, 0) to the corner (10, 10, 40). The rail bulges up to 7.14 mm from the straight line between the corners. `arc-centerline`: a 20 mm square at z = 0 and a circle of radius 10 mm, along a quarter circle of radius 60 mm. `four-to-three`: the 40 mm square and an equilateral triangle at z = 40 with its corners 20 mm from the axis. End conditions ran on `three-sections`, `point-end` and `four-to-three`, tangent and curvature, at every end that is a wire. The neighbor at each end is a prism of the loft's end face, 10 mm long. The angle asked is 0 rad. 79 runs in all.

**Constructions.** `ruled` and `smooth`: `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections`, solid, `CheckCompatibility(true)`, `pres3d` 1e-6, `AddVertex` for the point. `pipe-shell`: `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` along the centerline with each section added and `SetTolerance(1e-6, 1e-6, 1e-6)`. The rail ran through `SetMode` in all six modes the method documents. End conditions ran with one extra section (tangent) or two (curvature) offset 1, 0.1 and 0.01 mm along the end's tangent, on all three constructions. They also ran with `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeFilling` constraints on the neighbor's side faces, on the smooth loft only. `ThruSections` takes no spine and no rail, and did not run on `guide-rail` or `arc-centerline`.

| Construction | Inputs | Valid as built | Sections on the loft, worst | Rail on its lateral faces | Parity | Three-section loft natively (sets); Node |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ruled` | `three-sections`, `point-end`, `four-to-three` | 3 of 3 | 1.4e-14 mm | | 3 of 3 | 7.94 ms (7.94, 11.13, 7.83 ms); 19.07 ms |
| `smooth` | the same | 3 of 3 | 4.9e-9 mm | | 3 of 3 | 6.70 ms (6.68, 9.45, 6.70 ms); 15.77 ms |
| `pipe-shell` | the same and `arc-centerline` | 4 of 4 | 8.9e-9 mm | | 4 of 4 | 8.19 ms (8.19, 11.22, 7.96 ms); 14.68 ms |
| `pipe-shell` with the rail | `guide-rail`, six modes | `free` and `free curvilinear` only; `contact`, `contact curvilinear` and `border curvilinear` not done (status 1) after 1.16, 1.50 and 0.43 s; `border` raises `Standard_ConstructionError: gp_Trsf::SetScaleFactor` | | fails: 7.133 mm (`free`), 6.824 mm (`free curvilinear`) | the same failures on both targets | |

| End method | Tangent: `three-sections`, `point-end`, `four-to-three` | Curvature: the same three |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `ruled` with extra sections, 1, 0.1 and 0.01 mm | meets all three at every offset (at most 5.5e-17 rad), with a crease of 0.13 to 0.48 rad at the extra section | meets all three the same way |
| `smooth` with extra sections | none (the largest angle of a run 8.3e-5 to 1.8e-2 rad) | `three-sections` at 0.01 mm (1.9e-7 to 3.8e-7 rad), which fails parity; `four-to-three` at 0.01 mm (2.7e-7 rad); not `point-end` (at best 3.4e-6 rad) |
| `pipe-shell` with extra sections | none (1.3e-4 to 2.0e-2 rad) | `point-end` (6.3e-8 rad) and `four-to-three` (3.1e-7 rad) at 0.01 mm; `three-sections` not done at 0.01 mm, 1.7e-5 rad at 0.1 mm |
| `smooth` with `MakeFilling` (G1, G2) | none (0.245 to 0.464 rad) | raises on all three |
| `smooth` with `MakeFilling` at `GeomAbs_C1` | not run | none (0.030 to 1.39 rad); two of three fail parity |

**Validity and meshes.** Every built loft, 71 of the 79 runs, is one solid. Each is valid as built natively and under Node, except `four-to-three` curvature with the `GeomAbs_C1` filling, which is valid after repair. Every built loft's mesh is closed. Eight runs raise the same exception on both targets: the four failing rail modes, the three G2 fillings and `three-sections` curvature on the pipe shell with extra sections 0.01 mm apart.

**End conditions.** The plain lofts meet their neighbors at 0.20 to 0.25 rad at the start of `three-sections`, 0.464 rad on `point-end` and 0.245 rad on `four-to-three`. The ruled loft with extra sections meets 0 rad because its first faces are the planar walls of a prism of the end section. The plain loft's crease moves to the extra section. An extra section bends the smooth loft and the pipe shell toward the tangent in proportion to its offset, about 0.002 to 0.020 rad per mm. `GeomAbs_G2` throws `Standard_Failure: BRepFill : The continuity is not G0 G1 or G2`: OCCT 7.9.3 passes the `GeomAbs_Shape` value as the plate's order (open issue 22). `GeomAbs_C1` reaches order 2 and meets nothing. A filled face drops the middle section it spans: the circle of `three-sections` lies 1.110 mm off with G1 and 2.741 mm off at order 2. Every end-condition run was a straight loft between profiles. A filled face cannot be tangent at its corners, where the loft's lateral edges are kept. `MakeFilling` ran on the smooth loft only. On the ruled loft and the pipe shell it is expected to fail at the corners the same way, an inference that was not run.

**Time.** The three-section loft takes 6.70 to 8.19 ms natively against 100 ms, the largest set 11.22 ms. Three later sets gave 8.18 ms ruled, 7.03 ms smooth and 8.15 ms with the pipe shell. Under Node it takes 14.68 to 19.07 ms. The fillings take 40 to 88 ms (G1) and 98 to 388 ms (order 2). The failing rail modes run 6 ms (`border`) to 1,497 ms (`contact curvilinear`) before they raise. A print-only row's median of three sets can move by up to about 57 percent between groups of quiet sets. Those rows give an order of magnitude only.

**Parity.** 68 of 71 lofts agree, the largest difference 3.1e-10 relative. Three ill-conditioned lofts fail: `three-sections` curvature on the smooth loft with extra sections 0.01 mm apart (5.0e-6 relative in volume), and the `GeomAbs_C1` fillings of `three-sections` (3.6e-5) and `four-to-three` (4.5e-3). Every loft without an end method agrees, and so does every ruled loft with extra sections.

**Size.** The core is 20,775,709 bytes, a delta of 778,744 bytes, with `ThruSections`, the pipe shell, `MakeFilling`, the sewing and `GeomProjLib`.

**Interrupts.** `ThruSections::Build`, `MakePipeShell::Build` and `MakeFilling::Build` take a range and ignore it (tag `V7_9_3`: `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections.cxx` line 329, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell.cxx` line 208, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeFilling.cxx` line 141). The three-section loft polls 7 times, 8 with the pipe shell. Its longest gap is the builder: 5.30 ms ruled (most 9.91 ms), 4.28 ms smooth (most 6.14 ms) and 3.15 ms with the pipe shell (most 4.66 ms). Any built run's longest gap is at most 21.76 ms without a filling (most 31.50 ms) and 56.61 ms with one (most 80.97 ms). Counted under gdb on the timed binary, the rail modes poll twice and then run unpolled until they fail: 1,139 ms `contact`, 1,543 ms `contact curvilinear` and 490 ms `border curvilinear`. Those gaps are upper bounds.

**History.** `ThruSections::Generated` of a section edge is by position in the compatible sections, not by segment. A ruled loft's faces at one position in different segments carry the same names. A ruled loft's middle-section edges and vertices, the edges and vertices of extra sections and the pipe shell's point vertex have no name. Split end edges are named only as `FirstShape` and `LastShape`. Every face has a name. The rail names nothing.

**The Decision applied.** No construction passes `guide-rail`, and the spike does not pass. ADR 0071 gets no confirming note. [ADR 0075](../adr/0075-loft-rails-and-end-conditions-wait-for-a-later-milestone.md) moves rails to M3, and features/loft.md lists them as later. Under the Checks as written, the ruled loft with extra sections met both end conditions on all six inputs. The Decision's second sentence therefore did not apply as written. That loft meets the angle at the end edge with a prism wall, and keeps the plain loft's crease of 0.13 to 0.48 rad at the extra section, 0.01 to 1 mm inside the loft. ADR 0075 sets that check's reading aside after the run, for the owner's review at the M2 gate. Under its reading, an end condition holds when the loft's lateral surface is tangent, or curvature continuous, with the neighbor across the end edge and has no crease before the next profile. Under the check as written, a loft asked for a tangent end would keep its crease. No construction tried meets the new reading on every input, and ADR 0075 moves end conditions to M3 too. Lofts between profiles (ruled or smooth `ThruSections`, point ends, 4 to 3 edges) and along a straight or arc centerline (the pipe shell) passed their criteria. features/loft.md takes them.

### 8.5 Up-to-face and tapered prisms (`prism-extents`)

- **Question.** Which construction gives an extrude up to a face or a body, and a tapered extrude, valid and with each lateral face generated by exactly one profile edge in OCCT's history (item 17, and the up-to-body extent that choice 19 reuses)?
- **Constructions.** Up to a face: (i) `BRepFeat_MakePrism` with `Perform(Until)`; (ii) a prism through the body, `BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism` to beyond its bounding box, split at the target face's surface by `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter` and kept on the profile's side. Taper: (i) `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism`; (ii) `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle` on the lateral faces of a prism; (iii) `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections` between the profile and its offset at the far end (`BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset` of the profile's wires), exact for lines and arcs.
- **Inputs.** `RECT` up to a plane parallel to it and up to one tilted 20 degrees. Up to a cylinder face. Up to a face that the profile reaches only in part, gate 7's newcomer mistake. Up to the body, the first face the prism meets. A taper of 5 degrees outward and inward on `RECT`, on the profile of test 18 and on the 100-edge profile of section 5. A taper up to a tilted face.
- **Checks.** Each end face lies on its target surface within 1e-6 mm. Each lateral face of a taper meets the profile's plane at the taper angle within 1e-9 rad, measured with `Shape::edge_normals` on its edge in the start cap. Every lateral face is in the `Generated` list of exactly one profile edge.
- **Pass.** Every input is valid as built, the checks and parity hold, `RECT` up to a face builds within 30 ms natively and the tapered 100-edge profile within 60 ms. The face that the profile reaches only in part fails with a status the shim can turn into a message of its own.
- **Decision.** features/extrude.md and features/revolve.md (wave 2) take the construction that passes, the first listed where several do.
- **Feeds.** Items 17 and 28.

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), on section 8.2's machine and tools. Branch `spike/kernel-prism-extents`, last commit `003f9ac`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/prism-extents.patch`](../design/spikes/prism-extents.patch). `003f9ac` renames two locals. Its benches and every core are byte-identical to `40c7833`'s, where the sets were timed. Up to a face and up to the body take (ii), the split prism. A taper takes (ii), `BRepOffsetAPI_DraftAngle`. No plan choice is confirmed or replaced.

**Inputs as built.** `RECT` runs along +Z up to the bottom face (z = 30) of a block (`rect-parallel`), up to the bottom face of a block tilted 20 degrees about X (`rect-tilted`), up to the side of a cylinder of radius 60 mm lying along X (`rect-cylinder`), up to a block that covers the profile only for x of 40 and more (`rect-in-part`) and up to the body of `rect-parallel`'s block (`rect-body`). Tapers of 5 degrees outward and inward run 10 mm on `RECT`, on test 18's profile and on the 100-edge profile. `RECT` also tapers 5 degrees outward up to `rect-tilted`'s face. (i) ran with the body as basis (mode 2) and with an empty compound (mode 1). 37 runs in all.

| Criterion | Up to: (i) `BRepFeat_MakePrism`, body basis | (i) no basis | (ii) split | Taper: (i) `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism` | (ii) `DraftAngle` | (iii) `ThruSections` |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Valid as built | yes | yes | yes | no: a null shape on test 18's profile | yes | yes |
| End on its target within 1e-6 mm | no end face named | `rect-body` 10.0 mm off | yes, at most 1.6e-14 mm | where built, but no end face named up to the tilted face with the body basis | yes, at most 7.4e-15 mm | yes, at most 7.9e-15 mm |
| Taper within 1e-9 rad | | | | no: outward on `RECT` and the 100-edge profile, and up to the tilted face | yes, at most 1.5e-16 rad | yes, at most 1.0e-15 rad |
| Each lateral face in one profile edge's `Generated` | no: 0 of 5 | yes | yes | no: outward, and up to the tilted face | yes | yes |
| Parity | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |
| `RECT` up to a face within 30 ms (largest set median) | 8.9 ms | 12.4 ms | 8.0 ms | | | |
| Tapered 100-edge profile within 60 ms: outward, inward (sets 1 to 3 / 4 to 6) | | | | 175.1 / 163.3 ms, 70.8 / 70.0 ms | 51.3 / 57.1 ms, 57.4 / 50.7 ms | 75.7 / 69.1 ms, 58.3 / 62.7 ms |
| The face reached in part: a status of its own | yes, the shim's check | no: `BRepFeat_InvShape` | yes, the shim's check | | | |
| Size delta alone | 938,787 bytes (both `BRepFeat` builders) | the same | 37,635 bytes | 938,787 bytes | 157,541 bytes | 683,777 bytes |
| Passes | no | no | yes | no | yes | no |

**Validity and meshes.** Every result that builds is valid as built, and every mesh is closed. `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism` reports done with a null shape on test 18's profile (`Standard_NullObject`). Outward it adds faces that no profile edge generates: 10 instead of 6 on `RECT`, 202 instead of 102 on the 100-edge profile. With no basis, `BRepFeat_MakePrism` builds valid, closed solids that do not start at the profile: on `rect-cylinder` the part of the prism inside the cylinder (465,724.89 mm³, z from 40 to 160), on `rect-body` the part inside the block (40,000 mm³). Only the spike's own start check catches the first.

**The face reached only in part.** OCCT gives no status of its own with any construction. The splitter cuts at the face's extended surface and reports no error. `BRepFeat_MakePrism` with the body as basis reports done with a wrong solid. With no basis it gives `BRepFeat_InvShape`, which it sets for any invalid cut. The status is the shim's own check: every sample of every end face is classified against the target face, and a point outside it raises `Partsmith_InvalidInput` with a message that names the face and says the extrude cannot end on it. It holds for (ii) and for (i) with the body basis.

**Time.** The split takes `RECT` up to a face in 5.74, 4.89 and 7.28 ms natively (parallel, tilted, cylinder; sets 1 to 3) and in 6.80, 5.34 and 6.90 ms (sets 4 to 6), against 30 ms. `DraftAngle` takes the tapered 100-edge profile in 51.28 and 57.08 ms outward and 57.41 and 50.65 ms inward, against 60 ms. Its margin is thin: 4 of its 20 single-set medians are over 60 ms. Set 7 at `003f9ac` gives the same verdicts (`DraftAngle` 48.49 ms outward, 52.01 ms inward). A row's six set medians span up to a factor of 2.2 on this container, with a median spread of 41 percent. Under Node, which has no limit here, `DraftAngle` takes the 100-edge outward taper in 60.72 ms.

**Parity.** 37 lines, 30 identical. The worst relative gap is 5.9e-16 and the worst absolute gap 8.7e-11. Every measurement line and every history line is byte-identical on both targets.

**Size.** The whole branch is 21,430,927 bytes, a delta of 1,433,962 bytes, over 1 MiB with all five constructions. Section 8.1 item 5 holds each construction to 1 MiB. Each figure in the table comes from a build whose `main` keeps only the calls of the named facade functions in `keep_prism_extents_spike`. The split and `DraftAngle` together, the pair the Decision takes, add 161,175 bytes.

**Interrupts.** The split polls 400 to 663 times, nearly all inside `BRepAlgoAPI_Splitter`. Its longest gap is 1.32 to 1.81 ms up to a face (at most 2.23 ms), the shim's reach check, and 0.58 ms up to the body. `DraftAngle` polls 8 times: its `Build` takes a range and never polls. Its longest gap on the 100-edge profile is 34.7 ms inward and 36.8 ms outward (at most 54.4 ms). `ThruSections` polls 7 times. Its longest gap on the 100-edge profile is 33.2 ms inward and 44.4 ms outward (at most 51.7 ms). `BRepFeat` polls 4 to 6 times, all the shim's, around its whole builder: 110.3 ms on the 100-edge outward taper, 137.4 ms in its slowest timed run and 171.9 ms in one other run.

**History.** The split names every face, edge and vertex through `BRepPrimAPI_MakePrism`'s `Generated`, `FirstShape` and `LastShape` and the splitter's history. Up to a face its end face is the image of the shim's cutting face. Up to the body it is also `Modified` of the body's face. `DraftAngle`'s `Modified` and `Generated` name faces only, and its `ModifiedShape` names the edges and vertices. `ThruSections` leaves the end vertices unnamed (4 of 8 on `RECT`). `BRepFeat` names faces only. With the body as basis, 5 of the result's 6 faces have no name.

**The Decision applied.** features/extrude.md takes the split prism up to a face and up to the body, with the shim's check that the end lies within the face. It takes `DraftAngle` for a taper, split the same way for a taper up to a face. The rib spike reuses `up_to_body` with `split_at`, `make_prism` and `through_length`. (i) fails with the body basis on the `Generated` check (0 of 5 on every up-to input) and the end check (no end face named), and with no basis on the end check on `rect-body`. `BRepFeat_MakeDPrism` fails validity, `Generated`, the taper and the 60 ms. (iii) fails only the 60 ms. features/revolve.md gets no measured construction: no revolve was built. The spike measured plane and cylinder targets only. It ran no inward taper through a deep body, and no face the prism never reaches, one of gate 7's newcomer mistakes. features/extrude.md defines those cases (open issue 21).

### 8.6 The rib construction (`rib`)

- **Question.** Does option (b) of the M2 plan's choice 19 build every rib that option (a) builds?
- **Constructions.** (a) `BRepFeat_MakeLinearForm` with the open wire, its plane and the two thickness directions. (b) The open profile thickened into a planar face (`BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset` of the open wire with `IsOpenResult` true, by half the thickness to each side, join `GeomAbs_Intersection`), extruded up to the body with the construction section 8.5 picks, then fused with the settings of section 3.4.5.
- **Inputs.** The ribbed L-bracket of gate 1: two plates of 60 x 40 x 5 mm at a right angle with a diagonal rib 4 mm thick in their corner. A rib whose ends meet a cylinder. A rib of two segments in a V. A rib whose profile ends inside the body. A rib over a hole in its plate.
- **Checks.** The rib's side faces lie at half the thickness from the profile's plane within 1e-6 mm. Every rib face is generated by a profile edge or is one of its two sides.
- **Pass.** (b) is valid wherever (a) is, parity holds, and the bracket's rib builds within 50 ms natively.
- **Decision.** (b) is confirmed when it passes. A new ADR adopts (a) for the inputs that (b) cannot build.
- **Feeds.** features/rib.md in wave 3 (item 28), and thin-wall extrude (item 29), which thickens open profiles the same way.

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), on section 8.2's machine and tools. Branch `spike/kernel-rib`, last commit `0cf0d87`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/rib.patch`](../design/spikes/rib.patch). It brings section 8.5's picked construction from `003f9ac`. Since `0cf0d87` it builds (a) under a mutex. (b) passes, and choice 19's (b) is confirmed for straight profiles ([ADR 0071](../adr/0071-kernel-spikes-and-facade-calls-that-poll-an-interrupt.md)'s note of 2026-11-16). Choice 19's "unless" clause stays open for the V.

**Inputs as built.** Every rib is 4 mm thick. `bracket`: two plates of 60 x 40 x 5 mm at a right angle (23,000 mm³), with the profile from (45, 5) to (5, 45) on the middle plane y = 20. The rib it describes is the triangle (5, 5), (45, 5), (5, 45) of 800 mm², 26,200 mm³ with the bracket. `cylinder`: a tube of radii 20 and 15 mm, 30 mm long, with the chord of the bore at y = 5 on the plane z = 15 (17,318.38219447849 mm³ described). `v`: the bracket with the profile (45, 5), (20, 20), (5, 45) (25,400 mm³). `inside`: the bracket's diagonal extended 3 mm into each plate (26,200 mm³). `hole`: the bracket with a hole of radius 4 mm through the horizontal plate under the rib (25,948.672587712816 mm³). (b) thickens a straight profile in the plane through it that holds the rib plane's normal, a plane section 8.6 does not name. It thickens the V in the V's own plane, the only plane that holds it.

**Constructions.** (a) `linear-form`, `BRepFeat_MakeLinearForm` with `Modify` true; its option `linear-form-fixed`, `Modify` false; and (b) `thickened`. 15 runs.

| Input | (a) `linear-form` | (a) `linear-form-fixed` | (b) `thickened` | Natively: (a), (a) fixed, (b) | Node: (b) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `bracket` | the rib described; sides at 0.0 mm | the same solid; no side or rib face named | the rib described; sides at 0.0 mm | 6.78, 13.61, 11.03 ms | 18.71 ms |
| `cylinder` | the rib described | the same solid, unnamed | the rib described | 5.95, 12.28, 9.77 ms | 19.13 ms |
| `v` | the rib described | the same solid, unnamed | a wall across the rib's plane, its sides 19.00000015 mm off; 27,764.66629332243 mm³ (+9.3 percent) | 7.53, 13.23, 15.98 ms | 28.33 ms |
| `inside` | the rib described | the same solid, unnamed | the rib described | 6.97, 13.84, 15.99 ms | 23.39 ms |
| `hole` | a solid far out of the body, x from -120 to 160 mm (189,101.73146811128 mm³); no side named | the same solid | a tongue through the hole (29,528.00464209099 mm³, +13.8 percent) | 26.82, 21.90, 24.24 ms | 42.97 ms |

**Validity and checks.** Every construction and every result is valid as built on all 5 inputs, and every mesh is closed. Section 8.6's Pass names validity, parity and the bracket's time, and section 8.1 item 5 adds the size. Its Checks are measured: the sides at half the thickness within 1e-6 mm, and every rib face generated by a profile edge or a side. (b) meets both on `bracket`, `cylinder` and `inside`. (a) with `Modify` true meets both on every input except `hole`. With `Modify` false its history names no side and no rib face. Neither construction reports a status on `hole`.

**Threads.** `BRepFeat_RibSlot::Modified` and `Generated` clear, fill and return one function-static list (`BRepFeat_RibSlot.cxx` lines 277, 307 and 325). Ribs built by (a) on several threads at once corrupted the heap. 16 of 40 runs of a test that builds every rib on 4 threads aborted, and 2 of 40 runs of the other rib tests with the harness's default threads. `0cf0d87` builds (a) under one mutex, as section 3.10 item 5 builds blends. Since then each of those tests passed 40 of 40 runs, and the crate passed 10 of 10. (b) needs no mutex.

**Time.** (b) builds the bracket's rib in 11.03 ms natively (sets 10.21, 11.03 and 11.04 ms; most 12.99 ms) against 50 ms, and in 18.71 ms under Node. Before the mutex it took 10.65, 14.12 and 11.07 ms. With the mutex every row moved within -9.0 to +8.8 percent. A single set can sit 30 to 40 percent off a row's median of three sets.

**Parity.** 1,024 of 1,025 lines are identical. The other, the area of `hole`'s (b) construction, differs by 1.9e-16 relative.

**Size.** (a) alone adds 341,965 bytes (341,349 before the mutex), (b) alone 386,794 and both 719,024 (a core of 20,715,989 bytes). Each build's `main` keeps only the named construction's call in `keep_rib_spike`.

**Interrupts.** (a) polls 7 times, all the shim's: neither `Init` nor `Perform` takes a range. Its longest gap is the whole `Init` with sliding (3.3 to 4.2 ms) or the whole `Perform` without sliding or on the hole (6.4 to 13.0 ms). The most is 17.054 ms natively and 34.21 ms under Node, both on `hole`. A call that waits for the mutex waits in the gap that ends at poll 3. (b) polls 1,119 to 4,361 times, nearly all inside the splitter and the fuse. Its longest gap is 0.56 to 2.15 ms (at most 3.075 ms), mostly the result's `BRepCheck_Analyzer`. The bracket's rib by (b) polls 1,992 times, with a longest gap of 0.90 to 1.01 ms. The first and last gaps run from the shim's entry and to its exit.

**History.** (b) names every face, edge and vertex of the ribs it builds. The top face and the two sides come from the profile edge, through the thickening, `BRepOffsetAPI_MakeOffset::Generated` and the prism's `FirstShape` and `Generated`. The top face's end lines come from the profile's end vertices. The edges where the rib meets the body come from the profile edge and the body's face. (a) names faces only: the rib's faces by `Generated` of the profile edges, the sides by `FirstShape` and `LastShape`. On the bracket its 10 rib edges and 6 rib vertices have no name.

**The Decision applied.** (b) passes: it is valid wherever (a) is, parity holds, and the bracket's rib builds within 50 ms. Choice 19's (b) is confirmed for what the spike built: straight profiles, thickened in the plane through the profile that holds the rib plane's normal, a plane section 8.6 does not name. The Checks show two inputs where (b) does not build the rib described. On `v`, (b) builds a wall across the rib's plane, and (a) with `Modify` true builds the rib exactly. Whether `v` is a case that (a) builds and (b) cannot, under choice 19's "unless" clause and the Decision's second sentence, stays open. An ADR under the owner's rule settles profiles of more than one direction before features/rib.md (open issue 21 (e)). On `hole`, neither construction builds the rib, and neither says so. An ADR that adopts (a) must carry its mutex.

### 8.7 Edge projection, section by the sketch plane and silhouette (`projection`)

- **Question.** Which OCCT algorithms project body edges onto a sketch plane, cut a body by it and find a body's silhouette with exact curve kinds, and how much do they grow the web core (item 26, m2-plan.md risk 8)?
- **Constructions.** Projection: `GeomProjLib::ProjectOnPlane` of each edge's curve along the plane's normal, and `BRepProj_Projection` of the edges onto the plane. Section: `BRepAlgoAPI_Section` with approximation on and off (the facade's `section` has it on, section 3.4.12). Silhouette: `HLRBRep_Algo` with `HLRBRep_HLRToShape::OutLineVCompound`, which is exact, and `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`, which works from a mesh. `TKHLR` is already in the link list (section 3.12 item 3), but no shim source calls it at a3f4573.
- **Inputs.** `BOX`'s 12 edges onto `Plane::XY` and onto a plane with normal (0,1,1). A cylinder's circle onto a plane at 30 degrees to it. The 50 edges of test 45's result onto `Plane::XY`. The section of the part of section 5 by its sketch plane. Silhouettes of a cylinder, a sphere, a torus and a cone along a direction oblique to their axes, and of test 45's result along Z.
- **Checks.** The geometry kind of each projected curve: lines project to lines, and circles to circles or ellipses. 20 sampled points of each source edge project within 1e-6 mm of its projected curve. A cylinder's silhouette along a direction perpendicular to its axis is two lines. The tolerance each silhouette method reports is recorded.
- **Pass.** The kinds, the point checks and parity hold, the 50 edges project within 20 ms natively, and the silhouette method picked adds at most 1 MiB to the core.
- **Decision.** sketch.md section 3.20.7 and features/sketch.md (wave 3) take the algorithms that pass. A silhouette method over its size budget gives way to the other, or to a new ADR that weighs the size.
- **Feeds.** Item 26's facade calls, which its kernel spec names in wave 3.

#### Results

Run on 2026-11-16 (UTC), on section 8.2's machine and tools. Branch `spike/kernel-projection`, last commit `1fe1c9a`, base `2c56ff9`, kept as [`docs/design/spikes/projection.patch`](../design/spikes/projection.patch). Times here are the median of 5 sets, each set the row's median of 5 runs. The section passes with `BRepAlgoAPI_Section`. No projection and no silhouette construction passes. No plan choice is confirmed or replaced, and open issue 21 gives projection and silhouette to item 26's kernel spec.

**Inputs as built.** `box-xy` and `box-011`: `BOX`'s 12 edges onto `Plane::XY` and onto the plane of normal (0, 1, 1). `circle-30`: the top circle of test 10's cylinder onto a plane at 30 degrees to it. `rounded-xy`: test 45's result onto `Plane::XY`. It has 56 edges, not 50: 24 lines, 24 arcs and 8 degenerated edges. `part-section`: section 5's part cut by its sketch plane z = 0, which holds its bottom face. The section is that face's boundary, and the input exercises no intersection curve. Silhouettes along (0, 1, 1): `cylinder-oblique` (test 10's cylinder), `sphere-oblique` (test 11's sphere), `torus-oblique` (radii 10 and 3 mm) and `cone-oblique` (radii 10 and 5 mm, 20 mm tall). `cylinder-perpendicular`: test 10's cylinder along (0, 1, 0), for the two-line check. `rounded-z`: test 45's result along Z. The shim turns a degenerated edge or a line along the plane's normal into a point before any algorithm runs. Without that step `GeomProjLib` raised `Geom_TrimmedCurve::U1 == U2` on `BOX`'s vertical edges.

**Constructions.** Projection: `GeomProjLib::ProjectOnPlane` with `KeepParametrization` off (as named) and on, and `BRepProj_Projection`. Section: `BRepAlgoAPI_Section` with approximation on and off. Silhouette: `HLRBRep_Algo` and `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` (on the shim's tessellation at 0.05 mm and 5 degrees) with `OutLineVCompound` (as named), and each with its edges on the outline (`OutLineVCompound3d` projected by `GeomProjLib`, and `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`'s own segments on the outline).

| Construction | Kinds | Points within 1e-6 mm | Cylinder across its axis: two lines | Parity | The 56 edges natively | Size delta alone | Passes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `GeomProjLib::ProjectOnPlane` (as named) | fails on `rounded-xy`: 16 arcs seen edge on as B-splines | fails on `rounded-xy`: 4 arcs 0.7270 mm off | | yes | 0.212 ms | 24,419 bytes | no |
| the same with `KeepParametrization` | fails: 8 lines of `box-011` and 16 arcs of `rounded-xy` as B-splines | yes, 8.2e-12 mm | | yes | 0.198 ms | 24,405 bytes | no |
| `BRepProj_Projection` | fails on `circle-30`: a B-spline of tolerance 5.000001e-6 for the ellipse | yes, 4.24e-7 mm | | yes | 28.49 ms | 27,301 bytes | no |
| `BRepAlgoAPI_Section`, approximation on | yes | yes, 7.1e-15 mm | | yes | | 34,476 bytes | yes |
| `BRepAlgoAPI_Section`, approximation off | yes | yes, 7.1e-15 mm | | yes | | 34,476 bytes | yes |
| `HLRBRep_Algo` with `OutLineVCompound` (as named) | lines and circles on quadrics, B-splines on the torus | | fails: 1 line | yes | | 336,705 bytes | no |
| `HLRBRep_Algo` with its edges on the outline | yes | fails on `rounded-z`'s corner arcs 7 and 8: 0.7270 mm | yes | yes | | 322,907 bytes | no |
| `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` with `OutLineVCompound` (as named) | lines | | fails: 1 line | yes | | 86,244 bytes | no |
| `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` with its edges on the outline | lines | | fails: 146 lines | yes | | 82,064 bytes | no |

**Validity and meshes.** Every result is valid as built. Every result is a compound of edges without a face, and the closed-mesh check holds with 0 triangles.

**Kinds and points.** Read as written, a line along the direction cannot project to a line, nor a circle seen edge on to a circle or an ellipse. No verdict depends on that reading. `GeomProjLib` without `KeepParametrization` returns the 270-degree complement of an arc whose circle's axis points against the plane's normal: `rounded-xy`'s edges 14, 40, 41 and 52 (`ProjLib_ProjectOnPlane::Load`, open issue 22). With `KeepParametrization` those arcs are right, and every line across the plane becomes a degree-1 B-spline. Both approximate a circle seen edge on by a B-spline. `BRepProj_Projection` gives a line there, and approximates `circle-30`'s ellipse: its own section runs with approximation on.

**Section.** 13 edges (6 lines, 7 circles), 337.96459430051425 mm long, on the plane and within 7.1e-15 mm of the body. Both options give the same numbers, bit for bit. Each edge is `Modified` of an edge of the part. A section through curved faces was not measured.

**Silhouettes.** `OutLineVCompound` leaves out outlines that lie on an existing edge, with both methods. Test 10's cylinder has its seam at +X, on the outline for the views (0, 1, 0) and (0, 1, 1). Both named methods give 1 line there. Test 45's result along Z has its whole outline on existing edges, and both give no edge. A scratch check through the branch's facade, not on the branch and kept as [`docs/design/spikes/projection-views.rs`](../design/spikes/projection-views.rs), saw the cylinder from 25 views across its axis. `HLRBRep_Algo` as named gives two lines at every view except exactly +Y and -Y. `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` as named gives two lines at views 5 degrees or more from those, and 3 or 4 collinear pieces within 3 degrees. With its edges on the outline, `HLRBRep_Algo` gives two lines at every view, but its projection step returns two corner arcs of `rounded-z` as their complement (the `GeomProjLib` defect). `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`'s edges on the outline include the end circles' segments. Every silhouette edge reports a tolerance of 1e-7. `HLRBRep_Algo` is exact on quadrics (within 5.3e-15 mm) and 5.4e-4 mm off on the torus. `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` deviates by up to 0.0155 mm (torus).

**Time.** The 56 edges take 0.212 ms with `GeomProjLib` (set medians 0.165 to 0.291 ms), 0.198 ms with `KeepParametrization` and 28.49 ms with `BRepProj_Projection` (27.23 to 31.01 ms), against 20 ms. All 25 runs of `BRepProj_Projection` took 23.53 ms or more. Under Node they take 0.587, 0.518 and 50.30 ms. The section takes 2.489 ms with approximation and 2.433 ms without. `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` takes 204.4 ms on the torus natively and 578.1 ms under Node. A row's set medians span up to a factor of 2.3 on rows under 2 ms.

**Parity.** 686 measurement lines, 641 identical, the rest within 2.8e-13 absolute. Of the 38 summaries, 31 are identical and the rest at most 4.6e-13 relative apart.

**Size.** The whole branch is 20,426,637 bytes, a delta of 429,672 bytes. Each figure in the table comes from a build whose `main` keeps only that construction's call in `keep_projection_spike`. Each is within 1 MiB.

**Interrupts.** Only `BRepAlgoAPI_Section` (524 polls inside its build) and the shim's tessellation poll inside OCCT. `GeomProjLib`, `BRepProj_Projection` and both HLR algorithms take no range. The longest gap is in the shim's tessellation of the torus before `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`: 89.2 ms (most 137.4 ms), 89.5 ms with its edges on the outline (most 151.3 ms). `BRepProj_Projection` of `circle-30` runs 15.6 ms after its last poll (most 22.5 ms). `HLRBRep_Algo::Update` takes at most 4.11 ms (most 6.64 ms). Since `1fe1c9a` the prism projection polls before its covering face, as `split` does.

**History.** No OCCT history names a projected edge. The shim projects one edge per call and names each result by its source edge. The section's 13 edges and 13 vertices are `Modified` of the part's. `HLRBRep_Algo`'s `OutLineVCompound(face)` per face names each outline by its face, through `HLRTopoBRep_Data::NewSOldS`. `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo` names each segment by its face. With the edges on the outline, `HLRBRep_Algo`'s edges have no name, and `HLRBRep_PolyAlgo`'s are named by face or edge.

**The Decision applied.** `BRepAlgoAPI_Section` passes with approximation on and off. sketch.md section 3.20.7 and features/sketch.md take it. It confirms the coplanar case only. No projection construction passes, and the Decision names no case for that. No silhouette method passes, and the size clause does not decide. The silhouette verdict rests on the view of `cylinder-perpendicular`, a front sketch plane on a cylinder built on XY. Open issue 21 gives projection and silhouette to item 26's kernel spec, settled by an ADR under the owner's rule before features/sketch.md in wave 3.

### 8.8 Sweep paths and coil sections (`sweep-coil`)

- **Question.** Does `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` with one section per profile wire give valid sweeps along paths of lines and arcs with tangent and sharp junctions, along closed paths and with profiles with holes, with each lateral face named by one profile edge and one path segment, and does it do better than `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipe` of the face (the M2 plan's item 18)? Which construction builds coils with circle, square and triangle sections along choice 15's exact helix, on a cylinder and on a cone, of either hand (items 18 and 19)?
- **Constructions.** Sweep: (a) `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipeShell` on the path's wire, one per profile wire (the outer wire and each hole), with the profile's wire added by `Add` at the path's start, `SetMode(Standard_False)` (the corrected Frenet frame, the section kept perpendicular to the path), `SetTransitionMode(BRepBuilderAPI_RightCorner)` at sharp junctions (mitered corners) and `MakeSolid`, the holes' solids cut from the outer's with the settings of section 3.4.5; also `BRepBuilderAPI_RoundCorner` at the sharp junctions. (b) `BRepOffsetAPI_MakePipe` of the profile's face along the path's wire with `GeomFill_IsCorrectedFrenet`. Coil: (i) (a) along the exact helix of section 8.2 (a `Geom2d_Line` on the parameter plane of the cylinder or cone, one edge per turn, as `wrap_curves` makes it, section 3.4.14 items 2 and 3), in binormal mode along the axis (`SetMode(gp_Dir)` with the axis direction), with the 3D and boundary tolerances at 1e-7 mm (`SetTolerance(1e-7, 1e-7)`, as section 3.4.14 item 3 builds helix edges; at the default 1e-4 mm section 8.2's swept crest and root edges lay up to 1.008e-4 mm off), then `ShapeUpgrade_ShapeDivideAngle(2 pi)` with its frame turned to the coil's start; (ii) section 8.2's `lofted-divided` with the section's corners on helices, for the square and the triangle only; (iii) `BRepOffsetAPI_ThruSections` through the section rotated and raised every quarter turn, smooth; (iv) (b) along the exact helix in Frenet mode (`GeomFill_IsFrenet`).
- **Inputs.** Profiles: a circle of radius 3 mm, and a 6 x 4 mm rectangle with a hole of radius 1 mm at its center. Paths, each in the plane z = 0 unless stated, the profile in the plane normal to the path at its start: `ten`, 10 segments alternating 5 lines of 20 mm and 5 arcs of radius 15 mm and 90 degrees, starting with a line, the arcs turning left and right in turn (starting left), every junction tangent; `ten-sharp`, the lines L1 to L7 of 20 mm and the arcs A1 to A3 of radius 15 mm and 45 degrees turning left, right and left, in the order L1, A1, L2, L3, A2, L4, L5, A3, L6, L7, each arc tangent to the lines it joins, with sharp junctions where the path turns right by 90 degrees from L2 to L3, left by 60 degrees from L4 to L5 and right by 30 degrees from L6 to L7; `closed`, a 60 x 40 mm rectangle with corners rounded at radius 10 mm, tangent and closed; `edges`, the three edges of `BOX` from (0,0,0) along x, then y, then z (model edges, two sharp junctions out of one plane). Coils: 5 turns of pitch 4 mm, the section in the plane through the axis, its center 10 mm from the axis at the start: a circle of diameter 2 mm, a square of side 2 mm and an equilateral triangle of side 2 mm with one side parallel to the axis, each on a cylinder and on a cone of half-angle 5 degrees widening along the axis (kernel.md section 8.2's coil and m2-plan.md item 19's taper), each right-handed and left-handed (12 coils).
- **Checks.** Section 8.1 item 4's measurements, and: the volume of each sweep along `ten` and `closed` within 1e-6 relative of its exact value (the profile's area times the length of the path through the profile's centroid, Pappus' theorem, where every arc's radius exceeds the profile's reach); on `ten-sharp` and `edges` with mitered corners, the same value within 1e-6 relative (a miter in the plane that bisects a junction keeps the volume of the profile's area times the path's length, since both profiles have their centroid on the path). Each lateral face of a sweep lies in the history of exactly one profile edge and one path segment (`MakePipeShell::Generated` or `BRepFill_PipeShell`'s history of the section edge and the spine edge, never positions), and the start and end caps are `FirstShape` and `LastShape`. Each coil on the cylinder has the volume of the section's area times `2 pi r` times the turns, with `r` the distance of the section's centroid from the axis (Pappus' theorem for a screw motion), within 1e-6 relative; each cone coil agrees with (iii) within 1e-3 relative. On the cylinder, 20 points of each edge that a section vertex sweeps (the square's and the triangle's corners, the circle's seam vertex) lie within 1e-6 mm (for (iii) within 1e-4 mm) of that vertex's exact helix: the helix about the coil's axis with the coil's pitch and hand through the vertex's position at the start. On the cone the volume check against (iii) stands for it: a frame with a constant binormal turns the section out of the plane through the axis by `atan(pitch tan(half_angle) / (2 pi r))`, 0.0056 rad at the start here, and no vertex then follows a helix of its own exactly. Later booleans stay valid: a fuse with a box touching the coil's end face and a cut by a 2 mm slot across it, as section 8.2's. Threads: 8 threads each build the sweep of the holed profile along `ten` 20 times, and every result's `to_brep` bytes equal those of a single run.
- **Pass.** A sweep construction passes when every sweep input is valid as built, meshes are closed, the volume and history checks hold, the threads give equal results, parity holds, the holed profile along `ten` builds within 150 ms natively and its longest gap between polls is at most 150 ms natively (section 8.1 item 5's rule, for gate 11's cancel). The corner mode passes on `ten-sharp` and `edges` by the same terms. A coil construction passes when every coil input is valid as built with both later booleans valid as built, the mesh of the coil and of its fuse is closed (section 8.2's coil fuse left 4 open edges, open issue 22), the volume and helix checks hold, parity holds, and the circle coil on the cylinder builds within 300 ms natively with its longest poll gap at most 150 ms.
- **Decision.** features/sweep.md takes (a) when it passes, with mitered corners when `RightCorner` passes, round corners when only `RoundCorner` passes, and sharp junctions refused with a message that names the junction when neither does. (b) replaces (a) by a new ADR when (b) passes and (a) does not. features/coil.md takes (i) when it passes for all three sections, and (ii) for the polygon sections when (i) fails on them, by an ADR. A section that no construction builds is refused with its own message by an ADR, and an ADR that cuts a coil option adds it to gate 3's exceptions (m2-plan.md risk 11).
- **Feeds.** features/sweep.md and features/coil.md (item 18 and item 19's coil, stage 4), the sweep's and the coil's kernel calls (section 2, stage 2), naming's `lateral/<entity>#n` in path order (naming.md section 3.12.1), and open issues 17 and 22.

### 8.9 Thread ends (`thread-ends`)

- **Question.** Does the groove cut, ADR 0074's construction turned into the groove that a thread leaves in its base, cut from a user's cylinder or from a tap-drill bore in one boolean and run out through the end faces, give valid, exact threads that open at an internal thread's mouth, under a counterbore and with one tool translated to several points, keeping both boolean arguments (items 20 and 21)?
- **Constructions.** (a) The groove cut: the groove's solid is ADR 0074's `lofted-divided` loft of the groove's profile (the space between two teeth of the thread's profile, from the cylinder's surface to the root), started and ended one pitch beyond the threaded length so that it runs out through each end face it reaches, and cut from the base with `BRepAlgoAPI_Cut` and the settings of section 3.4.5. An internal thread's groove is cut from the bore's wall outward. (b) The annulus cut and tooth fuse: the annulus between the major and the minor diameter over the threaded length is cut from the base, and section 8.2's tooth is fused to it, ending P/4 inside each end as section 8.2 built it. Both use section 8.2's thread profile: ISO 68-1's basic profile with flats of P/8 at the major diameter and P/4 at the minor diameter and 0.2 mm of radial clearance. The profile's root lies at the major radius less `5 sqrt(3) / 16 P` and the clearance for an external thread (3.1234176532934073 mm for M8 x 1.25, section 6's `RE`), and at the major radius plus the clearance for an internal one (4.2 mm for M8 x 1.25). (a)'s groove runs from the base's surface to that root, its flanks extended to the surface. Its crest is the base's surface. features/thread.md sets the product's profile in stage 2, and track 3A checks these results again against it.
- **Inputs.** External threads on a user's cylinder at the major diameter (radius 4 mm for M8 x 1.25, 1.5 mm for M3 x 0.5 and 3.175 mm for 1/4-20 UNC), 20 mm long, threaded over its whole length and run out through both end faces: M8 x 1.25, M3 x 0.5 and 1/4-20 UNC, and M8 x 1.25 left-handed (section 3.4.14 item 10: section 8.2 measured right-hand threads only). Internal threads in a 20 mm block: M8 x 1.25 through a tap-drill bore of 6.8 mm (ISO 2306's diameter for M8 x 1.25, radius 3.4 mm, inside the profile's internal crest radius of 3.5234176532934076 mm), and blind, the bore 15 mm deep and the thread 12 mm from the mouth; M3 x 0.5 through a 2.5 mm bore. The cap of section 8.2 (M30 x 2, 12 mm), its thread open at the mouth. An M8 x 1.25 thread under a counterbore 14 mm wide and 8 mm deep, its thread starting at the counterbore's floor. A 40 x 40 x 6 mm plate with 4 blind M3 x 0.5 holes 5 mm deep at (10,10), (30,10), (10,30) and (30,30), one tool built once and translated to each point, cut in one call.
- **Checks.** Section 8.1 item 4's measurements, and: 20 points of each crest and root edge lie on their exact helix within 1e-6 mm, the crest's at the base's radius for (a) and at section 8.2's tooth's crest for (b) (3.8 mm external and 3.5234176532934076 mm internal for M8 x 1.25), and the root's at the profile's root radius. Both arguments kept: the result's volume equals the base's volume minus the volume of `common` of the base and the tool within 1e-6 relative, at least one face of the base and one of the tool are in the result (the history's `modified` lists), and `Shape::is_valid()` is not the only judge (section 8.2's plain `swept` lost its base and stayed valid, open issue 22). The opening: on every internal thread that reaches its mouth and on the cap, the face at the mouth has an inner wire with at least one edge that is not a circle (the groove cuts the mouth's edge). On the external threads, each end face's outer wire has such an edge. The translated tool gives the same face, edge and vertex counts at each of the 4 holes. Later booleans stay valid: section 8.2's slot cut and box fuse.
- **Pass.** (a) passes when every input is valid as built, both arguments are kept, the exactness, opening and later boolean checks hold, meshes are closed, parity holds, the external M8 x 1.25 x 20 thread with its cut builds within 500 ms natively, and the cap's thread with its cut has a longest poll gap of at most 150 ms (section 8.1 item 5). (b) passes on the same terms, the opening check excepted where its tooth ends inside.
- **Decision.** features/thread.md and features/hole.md take (a) when it passes. (b) replaces it by a new ADR when (b) passes and (a) does not, and the threads of (b) then do not open at the mouth. When neither passes an input, an ADR lists the failing inputs and features/thread.md offers the cosmetic thread in their failure message ([ADR 0074](../adr/0074-modeled-threads-lofted-between-helical-wires-and-divided-per-turn.md) decision 4).
- **Feeds.** features/thread.md and features/hole.md (items 20 and 21, stage 3), `modeled_thread`'s base and run-out (section 3.4.14 item 10, stage 2), and open issue 21 (a).

### 8.10 Extents follow-up (`extents-follow-up`)

- **Question.** Do section 8.5's split prism and `DraftAngle` also build what stage 1 of wave 2 refuses (section 3.4.3 item 1, open issue 23): cone targets, start faces, steep inward tapers, profiles of several regions, up-to and tapers on profiles of ellipses and B-splines, and tapers up to a cylinder or a shape; does a cutting cylinder turned away from the profile keep the end cap one face where the target's seam crosses it; and does a split revolution give revolve up to a face (item 17)?
- **Constructions.** Up to a face or a shape and tapers: section 3.4.3's, with the cutting face of a cone made like the cylinder's (the full turn over the corners' `v` range, widened by 1 mm), a profile of several regions swept as one compound of faces, and a start face made by splitting the through prism at both the start face's and the end face's cutting faces and keeping the piece between them. Revolve up to a face: (a) the split revolution, the profile revolved by a full turn from `start` and split at the target's cutting face, keeping the piece that holds the profile and runs from it in the revolution's sense; (b) `BRepFeat_MakeRevol` with `Perform(Until)` on the body.
- **Inputs.** `RECT` up to the side of a cone of half-angle 30 degrees whose axis runs along x above it, reached in full; up to a cone face it reaches only in part, and up to one it never reaches. `RECT` from the `Top` face of a box under it tilted 10 degrees about x up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM`. `RECT` tapered inward by 60 degrees over 30 mm, whose lateral faces meet at 14.43 mm, `RECT` tapered inward by 30 degrees over 50 mm, whose lateral faces meet at 43.30 mm, and over 40 mm, where they do not (features/extrude.md test 107's two builds, the second of volume 68355.588453), and test 18's profile tapered inward 5 degrees up to the far face of a body 200 mm deep. Two disjoint rectangles of 20 x 20 mm up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM`, and tapered 5 degrees over 10 mm. The ellipse of test 191 and the B-spline region of test 196 up to `BLOCK_BOTTOM`, up to `ROLL` and tapered 5 degrees outward and inward over 10 mm. `RECT` tapered 5 degrees up to `ROLL` and up to `BLOCK` as a shape. `RECT` on the top face of a box placed at (-10,-10,-20), 100 x 70 x 20, up to that box as a shape, along `Z` and along -`Z` (a profile on the target's boundary, section 3.4.3 item 3). Seams across the end: `RECT` up to the side of `ROLL` made with x axis (0,0,-1), whose seam then runs along (x, 25, 40) under `RECT`, and the square from (30,15) to (50,35) on `Plane::XY` up to the bore of the `cut` of `make_cylinder(&Plane { origin: (-10,25,0), normal: (1,0,0), x_axis: (0,0,1) }, 80, 100)` by the same cylinder with radius 60, whose seam runs along (x, 25, 60) over the square. Each runs with the cutting cylinder on the target's frame and on that frame turned about its axis so that its seam lies opposite the point where the line through the moved profile's centroid along the direction first meets the cylinder. Revolve: `RING` about Z up to a plane through the axis at 60 degrees, up to a plane at 1 mm from the axis, up to the side of a cylinder coaxial with Z at a distance that cuts it, and up to a plane it never reaches.
- **Checks.** Section 8.5's checks (the end on its target within 1e-6 mm, each lateral face of a taper at the taper angle within 1e-9 rad, every lateral face in one profile edge's `Generated`), section 3.4.3 item 5's outcome for every input that reaches its target only in part or never (`TargetPartlyReached` or `TargetNotReached`, never a solid), for `RECT` on the box's top face `TargetNotReached` along `Z` and no solid along -`Z`, into the box (one of the target kinds, recorded, to tell whether section 3.4.3 item 3 must refuse it as `TargetStartsInside`), the start face's piece bounded by the start face's surface within 1e-6 mm, and a detection of the inward taper whose lateral faces meet: a status the shim can turn into a message of its own, and then no taper whose lateral faces meet before the end returns a valid solid (each fails with `Failed` or `InvalidResult` as built, or the detection refuses it; section 3.4.3's closing paragraph), while `RECT` tapered inward by 30 degrees over 40 mm builds with volume 68355.588453 within 1e-6 relative. The volume of every ellipse and B-spline extrusion up to a plane parallel to its profile within 1e-6 relative of its area times its height, and of every tapered one within 1e-6 relative of the volume of its mesh at 0.002 mm and 1 degree (open issue 22). For the seams across the end: the `EndCap` faces of each frame, counted and recorded, and the two frames' volumes equal within 1e-9 relative. For the revolutions: each end cap on its target within 1e-6 mm, and the angle at which a plane through the axis cuts it within 1e-9 rad.
- **Pass.** An input class (cones, start faces, the steep taper, several regions, ellipses and B-splines, tapers up to a cylinder or a shape) passes when each of its inputs passes every check, is valid as built, parity holds, its meshes are closed, and its longest poll gap is at most 150 ms natively. `RECT` up to the cone builds within 30 ms natively and the tapered B-spline region within 60 ms, section 8.5's numbers. A revolve construction passes when every revolve input passes the checks, parity holds and `RING` up to the plane at 60 degrees builds within 30 ms natively.
- **Decision.** Track 4A widens section 3.4.3 item 1 and features/extrude.md to every class that passes (features/extrude.md tests from 126). A class that fails stays refused, by an ADR that records it. Section 3.4.3 item 5's cutting cylinder takes the turned frame when both seam inputs pass section 8.5's checks with it and each has one `EndCap` face. Revolve up to a face takes (a) when it passes and (b) when only (b) does. When neither passes, an ADR cuts it to M3 and adds it to gate 3's exceptions (it is last in m2-plan.md risk 11's cut order).
- **Feeds.** features/extrude.md and features/revolve.md (item 17's remainder, stage 4), section 3.4.3 and 3.4.4, open issues 21 (d) and 23, and the up-to-body extent of item 28.
